++# Proxima Local Automation Workflow
++
++This document describes the automated code review and repair workflow that runs locally on your machine.
++
++## Overview
++
++Proxima's local automation workflow is designed to be local-first, avoiding external API keys and using your existing browser sessions. It automates the process of reviewing commits, generating fixes (as patches), and validating them on a local bot branch.
++
++### Key Principles
++
++- **Local-Only**: No GitHub Actions or external cloud runners required.
++- **No API Keys**: Uses Proxima's local REST API (which utilizes your logged-in browser sessions).
++- **Safe Execution**: AI-generated fixes are applied only as unified diff patches after a `git apply --check` validation.
++- **Iterative**: The workflow can run multiple iterations (default 3) until tests pass and no critical/high issues remain.
++- **Human-in-the-loop**: Bot branches are pushed to GitHub for final review and merge by a human or Cursor.
++
++## Architecture
++
++The automation consists of three main components:
++
++1.  **Reviewer (`cli/proxima-review.cjs`)**: Performs the initial code audit.
++2.  **Repairer (`scripts/proxima-repair.cjs`)**: Generates a unified diff patch based on the review findings.
++3.  **Orchestrator (`scripts/proxima-loop.cjs`)**: Manages the end-to-end loop (test -> review -> repair -> apply -> test -> commit -> push).
++
++## Setup
++
++1.  **Ensure Proxima is running**: The automation requires the Proxima Agent Hub to be active.
++2.  **Enable REST API**: The repairer communicates with Proxima via `http://localhost:3210`.
++3.  **Install Dependencies**: Ensure you have run `npm install`.
++4.  **Configuration**: Customize the behavior in `proxima-automation.config.json`.
++
++## Usage
++
++Run the automation from the root of your repository:
++
++```bash
++# Run local review and repair loop (no push)
++npm run loop:local
++
++# Run loop and push the bot branch to GitHub
++npm run loop:local:push
++
++# Run loop, push branch, and create a draft Pull Request (requires 'gh' CLI)
++npm run loop:local:pr
++```
++
++## Bot Branches and Commits
++
++- **Branch format**: `proxima/fix-<shortSha>-iter-<n>`
++- **Commit message**: `fix: address Proxima review <shortSha> [proxima-auto-fix]`
++
++**Note**: Automatic reviews are automatically skipped for branches matching `proxima/fix-*` and commits containing `[proxima-auto-fix]`.
++
++## Recovery and Troubleshooting
++
++- **Proxima Unavailable**: If the Agent Hub is not running, the script will exit with an error and update `status.json`.
++- **Patch Failed**: If a generated patch cannot be applied cleanly, the script stops and saves the error to `repair/apply.log`.
++- **Tests Failed**: If tests fail after a patch is applied, the script will record the failure in `repair/test.log` and stop the current iteration.
++- **Max Iterations**: If the issues are not resolved after the configured `maxIterations`, the script exits with `max-iterations-reached` status.
++
++Check the `status.json` file in `/root/review/<shortSha>/` for detailed execution state.

+-# Code Review: 1bff21a2
+-
+-> test: add perplexity-reviews dir for hook test
+-
+-## Summary
+-This commit introduces a new `perplexity-reviews/` directory to the repository by adding an empty `.gitkeep` file. The sole purpose is to test a Git hook (likely a pre-commit, post-commit, or custom hook) that may require this directory to exist at hook execution time. Since Git does not track empty directories natively, `.gitkeep` is the conventional workaround to force directory presence in version history.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## What's Good
+-- **Correct convention used** ╬ô├ç├╢ `.gitkeep` is the widely accepted placeholder for empty directories in Git repositories; it's a zero-byte file that adds no noise.
+-- **Scoped commit** ╬ô├ç├╢ The commit is atomic and single-purpose; it does exactly one thing, which is ideal for clean history.
+-- **Good commit message format** ╬ô├ç├╢ Uses the `test:` conventional commit prefix, correctly categorizing this as test infrastructure rather than a feature or fix.
+-- **Proper author metadata** ╬ô├ç├╢ Commit author and institutional email (`nutech.edu.pk`) are clearly set, which is important for traceability.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Issues & Suggestions
+-
+-- **No README or context file inside the directory** ╬ô├ç├╢ `perplexity-reviews/` gives no indication to other contributors of what belongs here, what format reviews should follow, or which hook populates it. A `README.md` alongside `.gitkeep` would make intent explicit.
+-- **Ambiguous directory name in a production repo** ╬ô├ç├╢ If this directory is purely for local hook testing, it may not belong in the shared repository at all. Consider whether this should be in `.gitignore` instead, keeping the repo clean of test scaffolding.
+-- **Missing hook definition in the same commit (or PR)** ╬ô├ç├╢ The commit message says "for hook test," but the hook itself (presumably in `.git/hooks/` or a `hooks/` folder) is not included. This creates a dangling dependency ╬ô├ç├╢ the directory exists, but reviewers cannot verify its purpose or validate the hook's correctness.
+-- **`.gitkeep` vs `.gitignore` approach** ╬ô├ç├╢ If this directory will eventually hold generated or temporary files, adding it to `.gitignore` (tracking the directory rule, not the files) is often cleaner than `.gitkeep` for output directories.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Security & Performance
+-
+-- **No direct security concerns** ╬ô├ç├╢ An empty directory with a zero-byte placeholder poses no security risk.
+-- **No performance implications** ╬ô├ç├╢ The change is trivially small and has zero runtime impact.
+-- **Minor repo hygiene note** ╬ô├ç├╢ If `perplexity-reviews/` will store output files (e.g., review reports, logs), ensure those outputs are listed in `.gitignore` to prevent accidental commits of generated or sensitive review content in the future.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Overall Score: 7/10
+-
+-The commit is clean, correctly formatted, and uses proper Git conventions. The score is held back because the accompanying hook ╬ô├ç├╢ the actual substance of this "hook test" ╬ô├ç├╢ is absent, leaving the directory's existence unverifiable without additional context. The lack of any inline documentation for the directory's purpose also reduces maintainability.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Recommendations
+-
+-1. **Add or reference the hook** ╬ô├ç├╢ Commit the hook script (or link to it) that depends on `perplexity-reviews/` so reviewers can validate the full picture. If using a hooks manager like `husky` or `lefthook`, include the config change in the same PR.
+-2. **Add a `README.md` to `perplexity-reviews/`** ╬ô├ç├╢ Even two lines explaining the directory's purpose and expected contents will save future contributors (and your future self) significant confusion.
+-3. **Decide on gitignore strategy** ╬ô├ç├╢ If this directory will accumulate generated files, add `perplexity-reviews/*` and `!perplexity-reviews/.gitkeep` to `.gitignore` now, before files accidentally get committed.
+-4. **Consider scoping to test environments** ╬ô├ç├╢ If the hook and this directory are only relevant locally or in CI, document that in the project's `CONTRIBUTING.md` or `README.md` so developers know to set it up.

+-model: claude sonnet 4.6 thinking

+-
+-# Code Review: 3d894744
+-
+-> Enhance pre-push hook background review spawning and unsetting git environment variables
+-
+-## Summary
+-
+-This commit improves the `spawnBackground` function in `proxima-review.cjs` and the generated shell hook in `install-hooks.cjs`. The key changes are: stripping `GIT_*` environment variables from the background child process (preventing git command interference), routing background process stdout/stderr into a persistent log file, adding Windows compatibility via `cmd.exe /c start /b`, introducing a `logToFile` utility so background-mode messages persist, fixing a new-branch `git rev-list` bug with `-n 1`, and hardcoding the Node.js executable path in the generated shell hook instead of relying on ambient `node` in `$PATH`.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## What's Good
+-
+-- **`GIT_*` env stripping is the right fix.** Git sets variables like `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, and `GIT_WORK_TREE` in hook environments. These break child `git` invocations. The allowlist-by-exclusion pattern (`!key.startsWith('GIT_')`) is a well-known, battle-tested mitigation.
+-- **`PROXIMA_BACKGROUND_REVIEW=1` flag is clean.** Using an env flag to toggle dual logging (`console.log` + `logToFile`) rather than threading a boolean argument through the call stack is idiomatic and low-coupling.
+-- **`logToFile` silent catch `{}` is acceptable here.** Logging must never crash the reviewer; swallowing errors on a non-critical diagnostic path is justified. Good judgment call.
+-- **Hardcoding `process.execPath` in the hook** (`NODE_EXE`) avoids PATH resolution failures in environments like VS Code's Git integration or CI runners, where `node` may not be on `$PATH` for shell hooks. This is a meaningful reliability improvement.
+-- **Windows `windowsHide: true`** prevents a terminal window flashing on Windows during background spawn ╬ô├ç├╢ a polished UX detail.
+-- **`-n 1` fix for new branches** (`remoteSha === NULL_SHA`) is a real bug fix. Previously `git rev-list --reverse <sha>` on a new branch would walk the entire commit history, potentially triggering hundreds of reviews. Limiting to 1 is correct for the "only review the tip" intent.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Issues & Suggestions
+-
+-- **`cmd.exe /c start /b` doesn't detach cleanly on all Windows versions.** The `start /b` command runs in the same console window and is not equivalent to Unix `detached: true`. The process may still be tied to the parent's console session. A more robust Windows approach is to use `spawn('node', [...], { detached: true, ... })` directly ╬ô├ç├╢ the `cmd.exe` wrapper adds unnecessary complexity without guaranteed detachment.
+-
+-  ```js
+-  // Simpler and more reliable on Windows too:
+-  const child = spawn(process.execPath, [__filename, sha], {
+-      detached: true,
+-      stdio: ['ignore', out, out],
+-      cwd: findGitRoot(),
+-      env: cleanEnv,
+-      windowsHide: true
+-  });
+-  ```
+-
+-- **`fs.openSync` file descriptor is never closed (`out` leaks).** After `child.unref()`, the `out` fd remains open in the parent process. On high-frequency pushes this accumulates. You should close it after spawn:
+-
+-  ```js
+-  child.unref();
+-  fs.closeSync(out); // ╬ô├Ñ├ë add this
+-  ```
+-
+-- **`logToFile` duplicates directory-creation logic.** `REVIEW_DIR` existence is checked in at least three places now (`runReview`, `spawnBackground`, `logToFile`). Extract a `ensureReviewDir()` helper called once at startup or lazily, not scattered across functions.
+-
+-- **`log` function in `runReview` strips ANSI when writing to file.** `logToFile` writes raw strings including ANSI escape codes (e.g. `cyan(...)`, `red(...)`). Log files will contain garbled color codes like `\u001b[36mΓëí╞Æ├▒├╗\u001b[0m`. Strip colors before writing to file:
+-
+-  ```js
+-  const stripAnsi = (s) => s.replace(/\x1b\[[0-9;]*m/g, '');
+-  fs.appendFileSync(logFile, `[${new Date().toISOString()}] ${stripAnsi(msg)}\n`, 'utf8');
+-  ```
+-
+-- **`-n 1` for new branches may miss multi-commit initial pushes.** If a developer creates a branch and pushes 5 commits at once, only HEAD is reviewed. This may be intentional, but it's worth a comment clarifying the decision:
+-
+-  ```js
+-  // New branch: review only HEAD to avoid reviewing entire repo history
+-  ? '-n 1 ' + localSha
+-  ```
+-
+-- **`install-hooks.cjs` escaping inconsistency.** The fix to `\$REVIEW_SCRIPT` and `\$NODE_EXE` in the generated shell script is correct (they must not be interpolated at hook-generation time), but the surrounding variables like `"$REVIEW_SCRIPT"` (non-escaped) and `"${unixPath}"` (JS template literal) in the same heredoc create a subtle mental model mismatch. Consider adding a comment block at the top of `buildHookScript` clarifying which variables are JS-interpolated vs. shell-runtime.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Security & Performance
+-
+-- **Environment inheritance is conservative but potentially leaky.** The `cleanEnv` strips `GIT_*` but passes everything else ╬ô├ç├╢ including `npm_*`, `NODE_*`, credentials in env vars, CI secrets, etc. ╬ô├ç├╢ to an unmanaged detached background process. For a tool installed on developer machines this is likely acceptable, but worth noting in documentation: background processes inherit the full shell environment minus Git internals.
+-- **Log file has no rotation or size cap.** `background.log` is append-only with no truncation. On active repos over time this file will grow unboundedly. A simple size-check-and-rotate or a max-lines cap would prevent disk issues.
+-- **`fs.openSync` before `spawn` is a TOCTOU-safe pattern**, which is good ╬ô├ç├╢ the fd is opened before the child is created, so the child always inherits a valid write target. This is the correct ordering.
+-- **No sanitization of `sha` passed to `cmd.exe` args on Windows.** While SHAs are hex-only and safe in practice, if `sha` ever originates from user-controlled input (e.g., branch names), the Windows `cmd.exe` invocation path is a command-injection surface. Low risk currently, but worth a `sha.match(/^[0-9a-f]+$/)` guard.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Overall Score: 7.5/10
+-
+-This is a solid, practical commit that fixes a real bug (GIT_* env pollution) and several reliability gaps (PATH, new-branch rev-list, Windows UX). The implementation is readable and intentional. The score is held back by the fd leak, the ANSI codes in log files, and the over-engineered Windows spawn path that doesn't fully deliver on its promise.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Recommendations
+-
+-1. **Fix the `fs.openSync` fd leak** ╬ô├ç├╢ add `fs.closeSync(out)` immediately after `child.unref()`. This is the only functional bug introduced.
+-2. **Strip ANSI escape codes in `logToFile`** ╬ô├ç├╢ log files with raw color codes are unusable in most editors and `grep` workflows.
+-3. **Simplify Windows spawning** ╬ô├ç├╢ drop the `cmd.exe /c start /b` wrapper and use `spawn(process.execPath, ...)` with `detached: true` directly; it works on Windows too.
+-4. **Add log rotation** ╬ô├ç├╢ even a simple "truncate if > 1MB" guard on `background.log` prevents a long-term disk leak.
+-5. **Consolidate `ensureReviewDir()`** ╬ô├ç├╢ deduplicate the three `existsSync/mkdirSync` patterns into one shared call.
+-6. **Document the `-n 1` new-branch behavior** ╬ô├ç├╢ clarify in a comment whether reviewing only HEAD on new branches is by design.

+-
+-# Code Review: 51b6ecc9
+-
+-> Add Deep Research quota-aware routing with fallbacks
+-
+-This commit (`51b6ecc9`) was already reviewed earlier in this conversation. Rather than repeating the same analysis, here is the full review for your records ╬ô├ç├╢ incorporating everything identified the first time, presented as a clean standalone document.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Summary
+-
+-This commit introduces quota-aware routing for Deep Research mode in `perplexity-engine.js`. It adds `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` to pre-flight the `/rest/rate-limit/status` endpoint before deciding whether to route a query as `agentic_research` or fall back to `copilot` mode. It wires in dynamic `supported_block_use_cases`, Deep Research-specific API params (`extended_context`, `always_search_override`, `search_mode`, etc.), and removes the old `deepSearchCount` counter from the MCP tools in `mcp-server-v3.js`. The MCP changes are minor cleanups ╬ô├ç├╢ splitting one-liner `toolResponse(await ...)` calls into two-line `const result = await ...; return toolResponse(result)` patterns and minor description wording tweaks. The intent is sound but the implementation is not production-ready as submitted.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## What's Good
+-
+-- **Graceful fallback design**: `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` returns `{ available: true, remaining: -1 }` on both network failure and unexpected response shapes, ensuring Deep Research requests degrade to Pro Search rather than crashing the call.
+-- **Separation of concerns**: Extracting quota-checking into its own named function rather than inlining it in `send()` is the right architectural choice ╬ô├ç├╢ it is independently testable and readable.
+-- **Dynamic `supported_block_use_cases`**: Conditionally pushing `workflow_steps` and `background_agents` only for `agentic_research` mode is correct and semantically clean.
+-- **Replacing the counter with a live API check**: A live quota check from the API is strictly more correct than a manual local counter, which could desync after app restarts, crashes, or external usage.
+-- **MCP result variable refactor**: Splitting `toolResponse(await p.instance.chat(...))` into `const result = await ...; return toolResponse(result)` is a minor but valid readability improvement that also creates a future insertion point for logging or transformation.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Issues & Suggestions
+-
+-- **`var` throughout an async/modern codebase** (`perplexity-engine.js`, multiple lines): `_checkDeepResearchQuota`, `send()`, and the new param-building block all use `var` (`var res`, `var data`, `var agenticData`, `var quota`, `var params`, `var isDeepSearch`, `var finalMode`, `var supportedBlocks`). The MCP file uses `const`/`let` consistently. In async functions, `var`'s function-scoped hoisting is especially error-prone. **Replace every `var` with `const` or `let`.**
+-
+-- **Indentation regression on `var params`** (`perplexity-engine.js`, line ~307): The `var params = {` block has zero indentation while the surrounding code is indented. The `version: '2.18'` key-value pair inside the object is also flush against column 0 while all other keys are indented. This is a whitespace bug likely caused by a careless manual edit. Fix indentation throughout.
+-
+-- **Comment indentation lost in `mcp-server-v3.js`** (line ~562): The `// If using Perplexity...` comment lost its indentation and sits at column 0. Minor, but reveals the diff was applied sloppily.
+-
+-- **`quota.remaining === 0` edge case is over-blocking**: The condition `quota.available === false || quota.remaining === 0` will block Deep Research when `remaining` is `0`. However, the fallback also sets `remaining: 0` when `remaining_detail` is absent. If the API returns a shape where `agenticData` exists but `remaining_detail` is null, `remaining` becomes `0` and the gate incorrectly blocks an available-but-uncounted quota. Separate the two conditions:
+-  ```js
+-  if (!quota.available) { /* block */ }
+-  else if (quota.remaining !== -1 && quota.remaining === 0) { /* block */ }
+-  ```
+-
+-- **No timeout on the quota `fetch`**: If `https://www.perplexity.ai/rest/rate-limit/status` hangs, the entire `send()` call will hang indefinitely. Add an `AbortController` with a short timeout (e.g., 3 seconds):
+-  ```js
+-  const controller = new AbortController();
+-  const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 3000);
+-  const res = await fetch(url, { signal: controller.signal, ... });
+-  clearTimeout(timer);
+-  ```
+-
+-- **Raw SSE logging left in production path** (`perplexity-engine.js`, line ~149): `console.log('[RAW SSE]', data.substring(0, 500))` fires on **every single SSE message** for every query ╬ô├ç├╢ not just Deep Research queries. This produces massive log output and could expose partial user response content in logs. **Remove or gate behind a debug flag before merging.**
+-
+-- **Block-type logging left in production path** (line ~163): `console.log('[Perplexity block]', Object.keys(block))` has the same problem. Remove or flag.
+-
+-- **`STEP N:` comments throughout production code**: Comments like `// STEP 3:`, `// STEP 4:`, `// STEP 5:`, `// STEP 6:`, `// STEP 7:`, `// STEP 8:` are development-time breadcrumbs that have no place in a committed diff. They signal the code is still in an exploratory state. **Remove all of them.**
+-
+-- **No unit or integration tests added**: A quota-aware routing change of this magnitude warrants at minimum mock tests of `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` covering: success with quota available, `available: false`, `remaining === 0`, missing `agenticData`, network error, and timeout. None were added.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Security & Performance
+-
+-- **`credentials: 'include'` on the rate-limit endpoint**: Presumably intentional (session-based auth), but confirm this endpoint does not return PII or quota details that should not be accessible from the renderer process. If running in the Electron renderer, session cookies are correctly in scope, but ensure the endpoint is only called when the user session is active.
+-
+-- **Duplicated URL in `x-perplexity-request-endpoint` header**: This custom header mirrors the fetch URL. If one is updated but not the other, a mismatched value is silently sent. Extract to a shared constant:
+-  ```js
+-  const RATE_LIMIT_URL = 'https://www.perplexity.ai/rest/rate-limit/status?version=2.18&source=default';
+-  fetch(RATE_LIMIT_URL, { headers: { 'x-perplexity-request-endpoint': RATE_LIMIT_URL, ... } });
+-  ```
+-
+-- **Additional latency on every Deep Research call**: Every `deepSearch: true` request now makes a blocking pre-flight HTTP round-trip before the actual query. Consider **caching the quota response** for a short TTL (e.g., 60 seconds) in memory so repeated requests don't each pay this cost. At typical API latency of 200╬ô├ç├┤500ms, this is directly user-visible.
+-
+-- **`console.log` of `quota.remaining` leaks quota state to renderer logs**: If logs are written to file or sent to a remote service, this may be undesirable. Low-priority but worth noting.
+-
+-- **5-second `setTimeout` in MCP tools** (`await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 5000))`): Pre-existing but worth flagging ╬ô├ç├╢ a hard-coded 5-second delay before every Perplexity call with no back-off, jitter, or queue is fragile. Consider a proper request serialization queue.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Overall Score: 5.5/10
+-
+-The core idea is correct and well-structured, but the commit is not production-ready. It contains debug logging active in every SSE hot path, development-stage `STEP N` comments, inconsistent `var` usage and indentation regressions, a latency concern with no caching mitigation, and an edge case in the quota guard logic. The MCP changes are clean. With the issues above addressed, this would comfortably be a 7.5╬ô├ç├┤8/10.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Recommendations
+-
+-Prioritized from most to least critical:
+-
+-1. **Remove or gate the raw SSE and block-type `console.log` calls immediately** ╬ô├ç├╢ they fire on every query for every user and will flood logs with potentially sensitive response data.
+-2. **Add an `AbortController` timeout** (3s) to `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` to prevent `send()` from hanging on a slow rate-limit endpoint.
+-3. **Cache the quota response with a 60-second in-memory TTL** to eliminate the blocking pre-flight round-trip on every Deep Research call.
+-4. **Fix the `quota.remaining === 0` guard** to avoid blocking requests when `remaining_detail` is absent by distinguishing `0 remaining` from "count unknown."
+-5. **Replace all `var` with `const`/`let`** and fix the indentation regressions on `var params` and `version: '2.18'`.
+-6. **Remove all `STEP N:` comments** ╬ô├ç├╢ they are development scaffolding, not documentation.
+-7. **Extract the rate-limit URL into a named constant** shared between the `fetch` call and the `x-perplexity-request-endpoint` header value.
+-8. **Add tests for `_checkDeepResearchQuota()`** covering the five key scenarios: quota available, `available: false`, `remaining === 0`, missing `agenticData`, and network/timeout failure.

+-## Summary
+-
+-This commit adds exactly one line to `cli/proxima-review.cjs`: a second `console.log` call immediately after the existing "Review queued" confirmation, printing a `Γëí╞Æ├å├¡` tip informing the user that background reviews may take 1╬ô├ç├┤2 minutes. No logic, control flow, or data handling is changed. No research was required ╬ô├ç├╢ this is a pure UX copy addition to a CLI output path.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## What's Good
+-
+-- **The tip is placed at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L372`, immediately after the "Review queued" confirmation at `L371`.** This is the correct position ╬ô├ç├╢ the user has just been told a background job was queued, and the follow-up expectation-setting message arrives in direct visual sequence. No hunt for it.
+-- **Using `cyan('Γëí╞Æ├å├¡')` at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L372` is stylistically consistent** with the preceding line's use of `cyan('Γëí╞Æ├▒├╗')`. Both informational lines share the same color treatment, which signals they are in the same message category (status/info rather than error or warning). The visual pairing is intentional and clean.
+-- **The message content itself is accurate and user-friendly.** "1╬ô├ç├┤2 minutes" is a realistic range for an LLM-backed review pipeline, and framing it as a tip rather than a warning correctly sets the tone ╬ô├ç├╢ this is expected behavior, not a problem.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Issues & Suggestions
+-
+-**Γëí╞Æ╞Æ├│ Minor**
+-
+-- **The time estimate at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L372` is hardcoded as a string literal `'1-2 minutes'`.** If the actual duration changes (e.g., due to a different model, a timeout adjustment, or infrastructure changes), this string will go stale silently. There is no single source of truth for the timeout value in this codebase that this message could reference. This is acceptable for now, but if a `REVIEW_TIMEOUT_MS` or similar constant is ever introduced elsewhere in `proxima-review.cjs`, the string here should be derived from it:
+-  ```js
+-  // If a constant exists, e.g.:

+-  console.log(cyan('Γëí╞Æ├å├¡') + ` Background reviews may take up to ${REVIEW_TIMEOUT_MINUTES} minutes to complete.`);
+-  ```
+-  For the current one-liner addition, this is not blocking ╬ô├ç├╢ just worth keeping in mind as the codebase matures.
+-
+-- **The message at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L372` has no corresponding log in the background process itself** (e.g., a timestamp when the review actually completes or fails). A user waiting 1╬ô├ç├┤2 minutes has no way to know if the process finished successfully, timed out, or crashed silently ╬ô├ç├╢ they must check the output file manually. The tip raises the expectation; a completion signal would close the loop. This is a broader UX gap, not introduced by this commit, but this line makes it more noticeable.

+-***
+-
+-## Security & Performance
+-
+-No security or performance implications. This is a single `console.log` call in the CLI output path, executed once per `spawnBackground` invocation after `child.unref()` has already been called (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L370`). The message is a static string with no user input interpolated.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Overall Score: 9/10
+-
+-This is a correct, well-placed, stylistically consistent UX improvement. The one-point deduction is for the hardcoded duration string having no tie to any underlying timeout constant ╬ô├ç├╢ a minor maintainability note, not a functional issue. There is nothing to block here.
+-
+-***
+-
+-## Recommendations
+-
+-1. **No blocking changes required.** The addition is correct and mergeable as-is.
+-2. **Future:** When/if a `REVIEW_TIMEOUT_MS` or similar constant is introduced in `cli/proxima-review.cjs`, update `L372` to derive the human-readable duration from it so the UX copy and the actual timeout stay in sync automatically.
+-3. **Future:** Consider adding a completion/failure signal from the background process (e.g., a final `[DONE]` or `[ERROR]` line appended to `background.log`) so users have a way to confirm the review finished ╬ô├ç├╢ the tip at `L372` implicitly promises a result within 1╬ô├ç├┤2 minutes, but there is currently no signal when that promise is fulfilled.

++const path = require('path');

++function findGitRoot() {
++    try {
++        return execSync('git rev-parse --show-toplevel', { encoding: 'utf8' }).trim();
++    } catch {
++        return process.cwd();
++    }
++}
++

++    reviewDir: "review",
++    baseUrl: "http://localhost:3210",
++

++    repairModel: "claude",
++    maxIterations: 3,
++    pushBotBranches: true,
++    createPullRequest: false,
++    pullRequestDraft: true,
++    pullRequestLabels: ["proxima-auto-fix"],
++    protectedBranches: ["main", "master", "develop"],
++    skipReviewBranches: ["proxima/fix-*"],
++    skipReviewCommitMarkers: ["[proxima-auto-fix]"],
++    testCommands: ["npm test"],

++    allowGeneratedScripts: false,
++    allowWorkflowModification: false,
++    allowReviewHistoryModification: false,
++    repairBranchPrefix: "proxima/fix",
++    autoMerge: false
++};

++function loadConfig() {
++    const gitRoot = findGitRoot();
++    const configPath = path.join(gitRoot, 'proxima-automation.config.json');
++    let userConfig = {};
++
++    if (fs.existsSync(configPath)) {
++        try {
++            userConfig = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(configPath, 'utf8'));
++        } catch (e) {
++            console.error(`Error parsing config file: ${e.message}`);
++        }
++    }
++
++    // Merge env vars
++    const envConfig = {
++        baseUrl: process.env.PROXIMA_BASE_URL,
++        reviewModel: process.env.PROXIMA_REVIEW_MODEL,
++        repairModel: process.env.PROXIMA_REPAIR_MODEL,

++    };

++    // Remove undefined values from envConfig
++    Object.keys(envConfig).forEach(key => envConfig[key] === undefined && delete envConfig[key]);
++
++    return {
++        ...DEFAULT_CONFIG,
++        ...userConfig,
++        ...envConfig,
++        gitRoot
++    };

++const { execSync } = require('child_process');
++const fs = require('fs');
++const path = require('path');
++
++function runCommand(cmd, options = {}) {
++    try {
++        const stdout = execSync(cmd, { 
++            encoding: 'utf8', 
++            stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
++            ...options 
++        });
++        return { success: true, stdout: stdout.trim(), stderr: '' };
++    } catch (e) {
++        return { 
++            success: false, 
++            stdout: e.stdout ? e.stdout.toString().trim() : '', 
++            stderr: e.stderr ? e.stderr.toString().trim() : e.message 
++        };
++    }
++}
++
++function getHeadSha() {
++    return runCommand('git rev-parse HEAD').stdout;
++}
++
++function getShortSha(sha) {
++    return runCommand(`git rev-parse --short ${sha || 'HEAD'}`).stdout;
++}
++
++function getCurrentBranch() {
++    return runCommand('git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD').stdout;
++}
++
++function getCommitMessage(sha) {
++    return runCommand(`git log -1 --pretty=%B ${sha || 'HEAD'}`).stdout;
++}
++
++function ensureInsideGitRepo() {
++    const res = runCommand('git rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree');
++    if (!res.success) throw new Error('Not inside a git repository');
++}
++
++function ensureCleanWorkingTree() {
++    const res = runCommand('git status --porcelain');
++    if (res.stdout !== '') {
++        return false;
++    }
++    return true;
++}
++
++function createBranch(branchName) {
++    return runCommand(`git checkout -b ${branchName}`);
++}
++
++function checkoutBranch(branchName) {
++    return runCommand(`git checkout ${branchName}`);
++}
++
++function isProtectedBranch(branch, protectedBranches) {
++    return protectedBranches.some(pattern => {
++        const regex = new RegExp('^' + pattern.replace(/\*/g, '.*') + '$');
++        return regex.test(branch);
++    });
++}
++
++function isBotBranch(branch, prefix) {
++    return branch.startsWith(prefix);
++}
++
++function isBotCommit(message, markers) {
++    return markers.some(marker => message.includes(marker));
++}
++
++function applyPatchCheck(patchPath) {
++    return runCommand(`git apply --check "${patchPath}"`);
++}
++
++function applyPatch(patchPath) {
++    return runCommand(`git apply "${patchPath}"`);
++}
++
++function commitAll(message) {
++    runCommand('git add .');
++    return runCommand(`git commit -m "${message.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`);
++}
++
++function pushBranch(branchName) {
++    return runCommand(`git push origin ${branchName}`);
++}
++
++function createPullRequest({ title, body, labels, draft }) {
++    let cmd = `gh pr create --title "${title.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}" --body "${body.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`;
++    if (draft) cmd += ' --draft';
++    if (labels && labels.length > 0) {
++        cmd += ` --label "${labels.join(',')}"`;
++    }
++    return runCommand(cmd);
++}
++

++const http = require('http');
++const url = require('url');
++
++async function askProxima(message, model, baseUrl = 'http://localhost:3210') {
++    const targetUrl = new url.URL('/v1/chat/completions', baseUrl);
++    
++    const payload = JSON.stringify({
++        model: model,
++        messages: [{ role: 'user', content: message }],
++        stream: false
++    });
++
++    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
++        const req = http.request(targetUrl, {
++            method: 'POST',
++            headers: {
++                'Content-Type': 'application/json',
++                'Content-Length': Buffer.byteLength(payload)
++            },
++            timeout: 300000 // 5 minutes
++        }, (res) => {
++            let data = '';
++            res.on('data', (chunk) => data += chunk);
++            res.on('end', () => {
++                if (res.statusCode >= 200 && res.statusCode < 300) {
++                    try {
++                        const json = JSON.parse(data);
++                        if (json.choices && json.choices[0] && json.choices[0].message) {
++                            resolve(json.choices[0].message.content);
++                        } else {
++                            reject(new Error('Unexpected response format from Proxima'));
++                        }
++                    } catch (e) {
++                        reject(new Error(`Failed to parse Proxima response: ${e.message}`));
++                    }
++                } else {
++                    reject(new Error(`Proxima returned status ${res.statusCode}: ${data}`));
++                }
++            });
++        });
++
++        req.on('error', (e) => {
++            if (e.code === 'ECONNREFUSED') {
++                reject(new Error(`Proxima is unavailable at ${baseUrl}. Is it running?`));
++            } else {
++                reject(e);
++            }
++        });
++
++        req.on('timeout', () => {
++            req.destroy();
++            reject(new Error('Request to Proxima timed out'));
++        });
++
++        req.write(payload);
++        req.end();
++    });
++}
++
++module.exports = { askProxima };

++function parseSeverityCounts(markdown) {
++    const counts = { critical: 0, high: 0, medium: 0, low: 0 };
++    
++    // Look for severity indicators in tables or bullet points
++    // Common markers: Γëí╞Æ├╢Γöñ Critical, Γëí╞Æ╞Æ├í High, Γëí╞Æ╞Æ├¡ Medium, Γëí╞Æ├╢Γòí Low
++    // Or just "Critical", "High", etc.
++    
++    const lines = markdown.split('\n');
++    for (const line of lines) {
++        const lower = line.toLowerCase();
++        if (lower.includes('critical')) counts.critical++;
++        else if (lower.includes('high')) counts.high++;
++        else if (lower.includes('medium')) counts.medium++;
++        else if (lower.includes('low')) counts.low++;
++    }
++    
++    // Note: This is a bit naive, it might overcount if the word appears in prose.
++    // Better would be to target the "Bugs & Failure Modes" table specifically.
++    const tableMatch = markdown.match(/## Bugs & Failure Modes\n([\s\S]*?)(?:\n##|$)/);
++    if (tableMatch) {
++        const table = tableMatch[1];
++        const tableCounts = { critical: 0, high: 0, medium: 0, low: 0 };
++        const tableLines = table.split('\n');
++        for (const line of tableLines) {
++            const lower = line.toLowerCase();
++            if (lower.includes('critical')) tableCounts.critical++;
++            else if (lower.includes('high')) tableCounts.high++;
++            else if (lower.includes('medium')) tableCounts.medium++;
++            else if (lower.includes('low')) tableCounts.low++;
++        }
++        return tableCounts;
++    }
++
++    return counts;
++}
++
++function parseScore(markdown) {
++    const match = markdown.match(/## Score: (\d+)\/10/);
++    return match ? parseInt(match[1]) : null;
++}
++
++function hasCriticalOrHigh(markdown) {
++    const counts = parseSeverityCounts(markdown);
++    return counts.critical > 0 || counts.high > 0;
++}
++
++module.exports = {
++    parseSeverityCounts,
++    parseScore,
++    hasCriticalOrHigh
++};

++++ b/scripts/lib/safety.cjs

++const path = require('path');
++
++function validatePatchText(patchText) {
++    // Reject markdown fences
++    if (patchText.includes('```')) {
++        throw new Error('Patch contains markdown fences');
++    }
++    
++    // Check if it looks like a unified diff
++    if (!patchText.includes('--- ') || !patchText.includes('+++ ') || !patchText.includes('@@ ')) {
++        throw new Error('Response does not appear to be a valid unified diff');
++    }
++    
++    // Reject prose before/after diff (simple check)
++    const lines = patchText.trim().split('\n');
++    if (!lines[0].startsWith('--- ') && !lines[0].startsWith('Index: ') && !lines[0].startsWith('diff ')) {
++        throw new Error('Patch contains prose before the diff');
++    }
++
++    return true;
++}
++
++function rejectDangerousPaths(patchText, gitRoot) {
++    const lines = patchText.split('\n');
++    for (const line of lines) {
++        if (line.startsWith('--- ') || line.startsWith('+++ ')) {
++            const filePath = line.substring(4).split('\t')[0].trim();
++            if (filePath === '/dev/null') continue;
++            
++            // Remove a/ or b/ prefix
++            const cleanPath = filePath.replace(/^[ab]\//, '');
++            const absolutePath = path.resolve(gitRoot, cleanPath);
++            
++            if (!absolutePath.startsWith(gitRoot)) {
++                throw new Error(`Patch modifies file outside repository: ${cleanPath}`);
++            }
++            
++            const base = path.basename(cleanPath);
++            if (cleanPath.startsWith('.git/') || base === '.env' || base === 'package-lock.json') {
++                throw new Error(`Patch modifies protected file: ${cleanPath}`);
++            }
++            
++            if (cleanPath.startsWith('.github/workflows/')) {
++                throw new Error(`Patch modifies GitHub workflow: ${cleanPath}`);
++            }
++        }
++    }
++    return true;
++}
++
++function rejectScriptExecution(content) {
++    const dangerousPatterns = [
++        /#!/,
++        /powershell/i,
++        /pwsh/i,
++        /cmd\.exe/i,
++        /bash/i,
++        /sh /i,
++        /\.ps1/i,
++        /\.sh/i,
++        /\.bat/i
++    ];
++    
++    for (const pattern of dangerousPatterns) {
++        if (pattern.test(content)) {
++            throw new Error(`Response contains forbidden script pattern: ${pattern}`);
++        }
++    }
++}
++
++module.exports = {
++    validatePatchText,
++    rejectDangerousPaths,
++    rejectScriptExecution
++};

++async function main() {
++    const config = loadConfig();
++    const args = process.argv.slice(2);
++

++    const prRequested = args.includes('--create-pr') || config.createPullRequest;

++    if (git.isProtectedBranch(currentBranch, config.protectedBranches)) {

++        process.exit(1);
++    }
++

++        console.error('╬ô┬Ñ├« Working tree is dirty. Commit your changes or use --allow-dirty.');
++        process.exit(1);
++    }
++

++        console.log('╬ô├à┬í Skipping bot commit.');
++        process.exit(0);
++    }
++
++    if (git.isBotBranch(currentBranch, config.repairBranchPrefix)) {
++        console.log('╬ô├à┬í Skipping bot branch.');
++        process.exit(0);
++    }
++
++    const sessionDir = path.join(config.gitRoot, config.reviewDir, shortSha);
++    if (!fs.existsSync(sessionDir)) fs.mkdirSync(sessionDir, { recursive: true });

++        commit: headSha,
++        shortSha: shortSha,

++        status: "starting",
++        testsPassed: false,
++        patchApplied: false,
++        branchPushed: false,
++        prCreated: false,
++        autoMerged: false,
++        reviewSkippedForBotBranch: true

++

++        status = { ...status, ...updates };
++        fs.writeFileSync(path.join(sessionDir, 'status.json'), JSON.stringify(status, null, 2), 'utf8');
++    };
++
++    updateStatus({});
++

++    console.log('Γëí╞Æ┬║┬¼ Running initial tests...');
++    let testsPassed = true;
++    for (const cmd of config.testCommands) {
++        const res = git.runCommand(cmd);

++        if (!res.success) {
++            testsPassed = false;
++            if (config.stopOnTestFailure) break;
++        }
++    }
++    updateStatus({ testsPassed });
++

+# Proxima Local Automation Workflow
+
+This document describes the automated code review and repair workflow that runs locally on your machine.
+
+## Overview
+
+Proxima's local automation workflow is designed to be local-first, avoiding external API keys and using your existing browser sessions. It automates the process of reviewing commits, generating fixes (as patches), and validating them on a local bot branch.
+
+### Key Principles
+
+- **Local-Only**: No GitHub Actions or external cloud runners required.
+- **No API Keys**: Uses Proxima's local REST API (which utilizes your logged-in browser sessions).
+- **Safe Execution**: AI-generated fixes are applied only as unified diff patches after a `git apply --check` validation.
+- **Iterative**: The workflow can run multiple iterations (default 3) until tests pass and no critical/high issues remain.
+- **Human-in-the-loop**: Bot branches are pushed to GitHub for final review and merge by a human or Cursor.
+
+## Architecture
+
+The automation consists of three main components:
+
+1.  **Reviewer (`cli/proxima-review.cjs`)**: Performs the initial code audit.
+2.  **Repairer (`scripts/proxima-repair.cjs`)**: Generates a unified diff patch based on the review findings.
+3.  **Orchestrator (`scripts/proxima-loop.cjs`)**: Manages the end-to-end loop (test -> review -> repair -> apply -> test -> commit -> push).
+
+## Setup
+
+1.  **Ensure Proxima is running**: The automation requires the Proxima Agent Hub to be active.
+2.  **Enable REST API**: The repairer communicates with Proxima via `http://localhost:3210`.
+3.  **Install Dependencies**: Ensure you have run `npm install`.
+4.  **Configuration**: Customize the behavior in `proxima-automation.config.json`.
+
+## Usage
+
+Run the automation from the root of your repository:
+
+```bash
+# Run local review and repair loop (no push)
+npm run loop:local
+
+# Run loop and push the bot branch to GitHub
+npm run loop:local:push
+
+# Run loop, push branch, and create a draft Pull Request (requires 'gh' CLI)
+npm run loop:local:pr
+```
+
+## Bot Branches and Commits
+
+- **Branch format**: `proxima/fix-<shortSha>-iter-<n>`
+- **Commit message**: `fix: address Proxima review <shortSha> [proxima-auto-fix]`
+
+**Note**: Automatic reviews are automatically skipped for branches matching `proxima/fix-*` and commits containing `[proxima-auto-fix]`.
+
+## Recovery and Troubleshooting
+
+- **Proxima Unavailable**: If the Agent Hub is not running, the script will exit with an error and update `status.json`.
+- **Patch Failed**: If a generated patch cannot be applied cleanly, the script stops and saves the error to `repair/apply.log`.
+- **Tests Failed**: If tests fail after a patch is applied, the script will record the failure in `repair/test.log` and stop the current iteration.
+- **Max Iterations**: If the issues are not resolved after the configured `maxIterations`, the script exits with `max-iterations-reached` status.
+
+Check the `status.json` file in `/root/review/<shortSha>/` for detailed execution state.

+++# Proxima Local Automation Workflow
+++
+++This document describes the automated code review and repair workflow that runs locally on your machine.
+++
+++## Overview
+++
+++Proxima's local automation workflow is designed to be local-first, avoiding external API keys and using your existing browser sessions. It automates the process of reviewing commits, generating fixes (as patches), and validating them on a local bot branch.
+++
+++### Key Principles
+++
+++- **Local-Only**: No GitHub Actions or external cloud runners required.
+++- **No API Keys**: Uses Proxima's local REST API (which utilizes your logged-in browser sessions).
+++- **Safe Execution**: AI-generated fixes are applied only as unified diff patches after a `git apply --check` validation.
+++- **Iterative**: The workflow can run multiple iterations (default 3) until tests pass and no critical/high issues remain.
+++- **Human-in-the-loop**: Bot branches are pushed to GitHub for final review and merge by a human or Cursor.
+++
+++## Architecture
+++
+++The automation consists of three main components:
+++
+++1.  **Reviewer (`cli/proxima-review.cjs`)**: Performs the initial code audit.
+++2.  **Repairer (`scripts/proxima-repair.cjs`)**: Generates a unified diff patch based on the review findings.
+++3.  **Orchestrator (`scripts/proxima-loop.cjs`)**: Manages the end-to-end loop (test -> review -> repair -> apply -> test -> commit -> push).
+++
+++## Setup
+++
+++1.  **Ensure Proxima is running**: The automation requires the Proxima Agent Hub to be active.
+++2.  **Enable REST API**: The repairer communicates with Proxima via `http://localhost:3210`.
+++3.  **Install Dependencies**: Ensure you have run `npm install`.
+++4.  **Configuration**: Customize the behavior in `proxima-automation.config.json`.
+++
+++## Usage
+++
+++Run the automation from the root of your repository:
+++
+++```bash
+++# Run local review and repair loop (no push)
+++npm run loop:local
+++
+++# Run loop and push the bot branch to GitHub
+++npm run loop:local:push
+++
+++# Run loop, push branch, and create a draft Pull Request (requires 'gh' CLI)
+++npm run loop:local:pr
+++```
+++
+++## Bot Branches and Commits
+++
+++- **Branch format**: `proxima/fix-<shortSha>-iter-<n>`
+++- **Commit message**: `fix: address Proxima review <shortSha> [proxima-auto-fix]`
+++
+++**Note**: Automatic reviews are automatically skipped for branches matching `proxima/fix-*` and commits containing `[proxima-auto-fix]`.
+++
+++## Recovery and Troubleshooting
+++
+++- **Proxima Unavailable**: If the Agent Hub is not running, the script will exit with an error and update `status.json`.
+++- **Patch Failed**: If a generated patch cannot be applied cleanly, the script stops and saves the error to `repair/apply.log`.
+++- **Tests Failed**: If tests fail after a patch is applied, the script will record the failure in `repair/test.log` and stop the current iteration.
+++- **Max Iterations**: If the issues are not resolved after the configured `maxIterations`, the script exits with `max-iterations-reached` status.
+++
+++Check the `status.json` file in `/root/review/<shortSha>/` for detailed execution state.

++-
++-# Code Review: 3d894744
++-
++-> Enhance pre-push hook background review spawning and unsetting git environment variables
++-
++-## Summary
++-
++-This commit improves the `spawnBackground` function in `proxima-review.cjs` and the generated shell hook in `install-hooks.cjs`. The key changes are: stripping `GIT_*` environment variables from the background child process (preventing git command interference), routing background process stdout/stderr into a persistent log file, adding Windows compatibility via `cmd.exe /c start /b`, introducing a `logToFile` utility so background-mode messages persist, fixing a new-branch `git rev-list` bug with `-n 1`, and hardcoding the Node.js executable path in the generated shell hook instead of relying on ambient `node` in `$PATH`.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## What's Good
++-
++-- **`GIT_*` env stripping is the right fix.** Git sets variables like `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, and `GIT_WORK_TREE` in hook environments. These break child `git` invocations. The allowlist-by-exclusion pattern (`!key.startsWith('GIT_')`) is a well-known, battle-tested mitigation.
++-- **`PROXIMA_BACKGROUND_REVIEW=1` flag is clean.** Using an env flag to toggle dual logging (`console.log` + `logToFile`) rather than threading a boolean argument through the call stack is idiomatic and low-coupling.
++-- **`logToFile` silent catch `{}` is acceptable here.** Logging must never crash the reviewer; swallowing errors on a non-critical diagnostic path is justified. Good judgment call.
++-- **Hardcoding `process.execPath` in the hook** (`NODE_EXE`) avoids PATH resolution failures in environments like VS Code's Git integration or CI runners, where `node` may not be on `$PATH` for shell hooks. This is a meaningful reliability improvement.
++-- **Windows `windowsHide: true`** prevents a terminal window flashing on Windows during background spawn Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó a polished UX detail.
++-- **`-n 1` fix for new branches** (`remoteSha === NULL_SHA`) is a real bug fix. Previously `git rev-list --reverse <sha>` on a new branch would walk the entire commit history, potentially triggering hundreds of reviews. Limiting to 1 is correct for the "only review the tip" intent.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## Issues & Suggestions
++-
++-- **`cmd.exe /c start /b` doesn't detach cleanly on all Windows versions.** The `start /b` command runs in the same console window and is not equivalent to Unix `detached: true`. The process may still be tied to the parent's console session. A more robust Windows approach is to use `spawn('node', [...], { detached: true, ... })` directly Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó the `cmd.exe` wrapper adds unnecessary complexity without guaranteed detachment.
++-
++-  ```js
++-  // Simpler and more reliable on Windows too:
++-  const child = spawn(process.execPath, [__filename, sha], {
++-      detached: true,
++-      stdio: ['ignore', out, out],
++-      cwd: findGitRoot(),
++-      env: cleanEnv,
++-      windowsHide: true
++-  });
++-  ```
++-
++-- **`fs.openSync` file descriptor is never closed (`out` leaks).** After `child.unref()`, the `out` fd remains open in the parent process. On high-frequency pushes this accumulates. You should close it after spawn:
++-
++-  ```js
++-  child.unref();
++-  fs.closeSync(out); // Γò¼├┤Γö£├æΓö£├½ add this
++-  ```
++-
++-- **`logToFile` duplicates directory-creation logic.** `REVIEW_DIR` existence is checked in at least three places now (`runReview`, `spawnBackground`, `logToFile`). Extract a `ensureReviewDir()` helper called once at startup or lazily, not scattered across functions.
++-
++-- **`log` function in `runReview` strips ANSI when writing to file.** `logToFile` writes raw strings including ANSI escape codes (e.g. `cyan(...)`, `red(...)`). Log files will contain garbled color codes like `\u001b[36m╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓö£ΓûÆΓö£Γòù\u001b[0m`. Strip colors before writing to file:
++-
++-  ```js
++-  const stripAnsi = (s) => s.replace(/\x1b\[[0-9;]*m/g, '');
++-  fs.appendFileSync(logFile, `[${new Date().toISOString()}] ${stripAnsi(msg)}\n`, 'utf8');
++-  ```
++-
++-- **`-n 1` for new branches may miss multi-commit initial pushes.** If a developer creates a branch and pushes 5 commits at once, only HEAD is reviewed. This may be intentional, but it's worth a comment clarifying the decision:
++-
++-  ```js
++-  // New branch: review only HEAD to avoid reviewing entire repo history
++-  ? '-n 1 ' + localSha
++-  ```
++-
++-- **`install-hooks.cjs` escaping inconsistency.** The fix to `\$REVIEW_SCRIPT` and `\$NODE_EXE` in the generated shell script is correct (they must not be interpolated at hook-generation time), but the surrounding variables like `"$REVIEW_SCRIPT"` (non-escaped) and `"${unixPath}"` (JS template literal) in the same heredoc create a subtle mental model mismatch. Consider adding a comment block at the top of `buildHookScript` clarifying which variables are JS-interpolated vs. shell-runtime.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## Security & Performance
++-
++-- **Environment inheritance is conservative but potentially leaky.** The `cleanEnv` strips `GIT_*` but passes everything else Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó including `npm_*`, `NODE_*`, credentials in env vars, CI secrets, etc. Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó to an unmanaged detached background process. For a tool installed on developer machines this is likely acceptable, but worth noting in documentation: background processes inherit the full shell environment minus Git internals.
++-- **Log file has no rotation or size cap.** `background.log` is append-only with no truncation. On active repos over time this file will grow unboundedly. A simple size-check-and-rotate or a max-lines cap would prevent disk issues.
++-- **`fs.openSync` before `spawn` is a TOCTOU-safe pattern**, which is good Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó the fd is opened before the child is created, so the child always inherits a valid write target. This is the correct ordering.
++-- **No sanitization of `sha` passed to `cmd.exe` args on Windows.** While SHAs are hex-only and safe in practice, if `sha` ever originates from user-controlled input (e.g., branch names), the Windows `cmd.exe` invocation path is a command-injection surface. Low risk currently, but worth a `sha.match(/^[0-9a-f]+$/)` guard.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## Overall Score: 7.5/10
++-
++-This is a solid, practical commit that fixes a real bug (GIT_* env pollution) and several reliability gaps (PATH, new-branch rev-list, Windows UX). The implementation is readable and intentional. The score is held back by the fd leak, the ANSI codes in log files, and the over-engineered Windows spawn path that doesn't fully deliver on its promise.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## Recommendations
++-
++-1. **Fix the `fs.openSync` fd leak** Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó add `fs.closeSync(out)` immediately after `child.unref()`. This is the only functional bug introduced.
++-2. **Strip ANSI escape codes in `logToFile`** Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó log files with raw color codes are unusable in most editors and `grep` workflows.
++-3. **Simplify Windows spawning** Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó drop the `cmd.exe /c start /b` wrapper and use `spawn(process.execPath, ...)` with `detached: true` directly; it works on Windows too.
++-4. **Add log rotation** Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó even a simple "truncate if > 1MB" guard on `background.log` prevents a long-term disk leak.
++-5. **Consolidate `ensureReviewDir()`** Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó deduplicate the three `existsSync/mkdirSync` patterns into one shared call.
++-6. **Document the `-n 1` new-branch behavior** Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó clarify in a comment whether reviewing only HEAD on new branches is by design.
++\ No newline at end of file

++-
++-# Code Review: 51b6ecc9
++-
++-> Add Deep Research quota-aware routing with fallbacks
++-
++-This commit (`51b6ecc9`) was already reviewed earlier in this conversation. Rather than repeating the same analysis, here is the full review for your records Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó incorporating everything identified the first time, presented as a clean standalone document.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## Summary
++-
++-This commit introduces quota-aware routing for Deep Research mode in `perplexity-engine.js`. It adds `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` to pre-flight the `/rest/rate-limit/status` endpoint before deciding whether to route a query as `agentic_research` or fall back to `copilot` mode. It wires in dynamic `supported_block_use_cases`, Deep Research-specific API params (`extended_context`, `always_search_override`, `search_mode`, etc.), and removes the old `deepSearchCount` counter from the MCP tools in `mcp-server-v3.js`. The MCP changes are minor cleanups Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó splitting one-liner `toolResponse(await ...)` calls into two-line `const result = await ...; return toolResponse(result)` patterns and minor description wording tweaks. The intent is sound but the implementation is not production-ready as submitted.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## What's Good
++-
++-- **Graceful fallback design**: `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` returns `{ available: true, remaining: -1 }` on both network failure and unexpected response shapes, ensuring Deep Research requests degrade to Pro Search rather than crashing the call.
++-- **Separation of concerns**: Extracting quota-checking into its own named function rather than inlining it in `send()` is the right architectural choice Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó it is independently testable and readable.
++-- **Dynamic `supported_block_use_cases`**: Conditionally pushing `workflow_steps` and `background_agents` only for `agentic_research` mode is correct and semantically clean.
++-- **Replacing the counter with a live API check**: A live quota check from the API is strictly more correct than a manual local counter, which could desync after app restarts, crashes, or external usage.
++-- **MCP result variable refactor**: Splitting `toolResponse(await p.instance.chat(...))` into `const result = await ...; return toolResponse(result)` is a minor but valid readability improvement that also creates a future insertion point for logging or transformation.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## Issues & Suggestions
++-
++-- **`var` throughout an async/modern codebase** (`perplexity-engine.js`, multiple lines): `_checkDeepResearchQuota`, `send()`, and the new param-building block all use `var` (`var res`, `var data`, `var agenticData`, `var quota`, `var params`, `var isDeepSearch`, `var finalMode`, `var supportedBlocks`). The MCP file uses `const`/`let` consistently. In async functions, `var`'s function-scoped hoisting is especially error-prone. **Replace every `var` with `const` or `let`.**
++-
++-- **Indentation regression on `var params`** (`perplexity-engine.js`, line ~307): The `var params = {` block has zero indentation while the surrounding code is indented. The `version: '2.18'` key-value pair inside the object is also flush against column 0 while all other keys are indented. This is a whitespace bug likely caused by a careless manual edit. Fix indentation throughout.
++-
++-- **Comment indentation lost in `mcp-server-v3.js`** (line ~562): The `// If using Perplexity...` comment lost its indentation and sits at column 0. Minor, but reveals the diff was applied sloppily.
++-
++-- **`quota.remaining === 0` edge case is over-blocking**: The condition `quota.available === false || quota.remaining === 0` will block Deep Research when `remaining` is `0`. However, the fallback also sets `remaining: 0` when `remaining_detail` is absent. If the API returns a shape where `agenticData` exists but `remaining_detail` is null, `remaining` becomes `0` and the gate incorrectly blocks an available-but-uncounted quota. Separate the two conditions:
++-  ```js
++-  if (!quota.available) { /* block */ }
++-  else if (quota.remaining !== -1 && quota.remaining === 0) { /* block */ }
++-  ```
++-
++-- **No timeout on the quota `fetch`**: If `https://www.perplexity.ai/rest/rate-limit/status` hangs, the entire `send()` call will hang indefinitely. Add an `AbortController` with a short timeout (e.g., 3 seconds):
++-  ```js
++-  const controller = new AbortController();
++-  const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 3000);
++-  const res = await fetch(url, { signal: controller.signal, ... });
++-  clearTimeout(timer);
++-  ```
++-
++-- **Raw SSE logging left in production path** (`perplexity-engine.js`, line ~149): `console.log('[RAW SSE]', data.substring(0, 500))` fires on **every single SSE message** for every query Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó not just Deep Research queries. This produces massive log output and could expose partial user response content in logs. **Remove or gate behind a debug flag before merging.**
++-
++-- **Block-type logging left in production path** (line ~163): `console.log('[Perplexity block]', Object.keys(block))` has the same problem. Remove or flag.
++-
++-- **`STEP N:` comments throughout production code**: Comments like `// STEP 3:`, `// STEP 4:`, `// STEP 5:`, `// STEP 6:`, `// STEP 7:`, `// STEP 8:` are development-time breadcrumbs that have no place in a committed diff. They signal the code is still in an exploratory state. **Remove all of them.**
++-
++-- **No unit or integration tests added**: A quota-aware routing change of this magnitude warrants at minimum mock tests of `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` covering: success with quota available, `available: false`, `remaining === 0`, missing `agenticData`, network error, and timeout. None were added.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## Security & Performance
++-
++-- **`credentials: 'include'` on the rate-limit endpoint**: Presumably intentional (session-based auth), but confirm this endpoint does not return PII or quota details that should not be accessible from the renderer process. If running in the Electron renderer, session cookies are correctly in scope, but ensure the endpoint is only called when the user session is active.
++-
++-- **Duplicated URL in `x-perplexity-request-endpoint` header**: This custom header mirrors the fetch URL. If one is updated but not the other, a mismatched value is silently sent. Extract to a shared constant:
++-  ```js
++-  const RATE_LIMIT_URL = 'https://www.perplexity.ai/rest/rate-limit/status?version=2.18&source=default';
++-  fetch(RATE_LIMIT_URL, { headers: { 'x-perplexity-request-endpoint': RATE_LIMIT_URL, ... } });
++-  ```
++-
++-- **Additional latency on every Deep Research call**: Every `deepSearch: true` request now makes a blocking pre-flight HTTP round-trip before the actual query. Consider **caching the quota response** for a short TTL (e.g., 60 seconds) in memory so repeated requests don't each pay this cost. At typical API latency of 200Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γöñ500ms, this is directly user-visible.
++-
++-- **`console.log` of `quota.remaining` leaks quota state to renderer logs**: If logs are written to file or sent to a remote service, this may be undesirable. Low-priority but worth noting.
++-
++-- **5-second `setTimeout` in MCP tools** (`await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 5000))`): Pre-existing but worth flagging Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó a hard-coded 5-second delay before every Perplexity call with no back-off, jitter, or queue is fragile. Consider a proper request serialization queue.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## Overall Score: 5.5/10
++-
++-The core idea is correct and well-structured, but the commit is not production-ready. It contains debug logging active in every SSE hot path, development-stage `STEP N` comments, inconsistent `var` usage and indentation regressions, a latency concern with no caching mitigation, and an edge case in the quota guard logic. The MCP changes are clean. With the issues above addressed, this would comfortably be a 7.5Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γöñ8/10.
++-
++-***
++-
++-## Recommendations
++-
++-Prioritized from most to least critical:
++-
++-1. **Remove or gate the raw SSE and block-type `console.log` calls immediately** Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó they fire on every query for every user and will flood logs with potentially sensitive response data.
++-2. **Add an `AbortController` timeout** (3s) to `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` to prevent `send()` from hanging on a slow rate-limit endpoint.
++-3. **Cache the quota response with a 60-second in-memory TTL** to eliminate the blocking pre-flight round-trip on every Deep Research call.
++-4. **Fix the `quota.remaining === 0` guard** to avoid blocking requests when `remaining_detail` is absent by distinguishing `0 remaining` from "count unknown."
++-5. **Replace all `var` with `const`/`let`** and fix the indentation regressions on `var params` and `version: '2.18'`.
++-6. **Remove all `STEP N:` comments** Γò¼├┤Γö£├ºΓö£Γòó they are development scaffolding, not documentation.
++-7. **Extract the rate-limit URL into a named constant** shared between the `fetch` call and the `x-perplexity-request-endpoint` header value.
++-8. **Add tests for `_checkDeepResearchQuota()`** covering the five key scenarios: quota available, `available: false`, `remaining === 0`, missing `agenticData`, and network/timeout failure.

+++        return execSync('git rev-parse --show-toplevel', { encoding: 'utf8' }).trim();
+++    } catch {
+++        return process.cwd();
+++    }
+++}

+++    reviewDir: "review",
+++    baseUrl: "http://localhost:3210",

+++function loadConfig() {
+++    const gitRoot = findGitRoot();
+++    const configPath = path.join(gitRoot, 'proxima-automation.config.json');
+++    let userConfig = {};
+++
+++    if (fs.existsSync(configPath)) {
+++        try {
+++            userConfig = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(configPath, 'utf8'));
+++        } catch (e) {
+++            console.error(`Error parsing config file: ${e.message}`);
+++        }
+++    }
+++
+++    // Merge env vars
+++    const envConfig = {
+++        baseUrl: process.env.PROXIMA_BASE_URL,
+++        reviewModel: process.env.PROXIMA_REVIEW_MODEL,
+++        repairModel: process.env.PROXIMA_REPAIR_MODEL,
+++        reviewDir: process.env.PROXIMA_REVIEW_DIR,
+++    };
+++
+++    // Remove undefined values from envConfig
+++    Object.keys(envConfig).forEach(key => envConfig[key] === undefined && delete envConfig[key]);
+++
+++    return {
+++        ...DEFAULT_CONFIG,
+++        ...userConfig,
+++        ...envConfig,
+++        gitRoot
+++    };
+++}

+++const { execSync } = require('child_process');
+++const fs = require('fs');
+++const path = require('path');
+++
+++function runCommand(cmd, options = {}) {
+++    try {
+++        const stdout = execSync(cmd, { 
+++            encoding: 'utf8', 
+++            stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
+++            ...options 
+++        });
+++        return { success: true, stdout: stdout.trim(), stderr: '' };
+++    } catch (e) {
+++        return { 
+++            success: false, 
+++            stdout: e.stdout ? e.stdout.toString().trim() : '', 
+++            stderr: e.stderr ? e.stderr.toString().trim() : e.message 
+++        };
+++    }
+++}
+++
+++function getHeadSha() {
+++    return runCommand('git rev-parse HEAD').stdout;
+++}
+++
+++function getShortSha(sha) {
+++    return runCommand(`git rev-parse --short ${sha || 'HEAD'}`).stdout;
+++}
+++
+++function getCurrentBranch() {
+++    return runCommand('git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD').stdout;
+++}
+++
+++function getCommitMessage(sha) {
+++    return runCommand(`git log -1 --pretty=%B ${sha || 'HEAD'}`).stdout;
+++}
+++
+++function ensureInsideGitRepo() {
+++    const res = runCommand('git rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree');
+++    if (!res.success) throw new Error('Not inside a git repository');
+++}
+++
+++function ensureCleanWorkingTree() {
+++    const res = runCommand('git status --porcelain');
+++    if (res.stdout !== '') {
+++        return false;
+++    }
+++    return true;
+++}
+++
+++function createBranch(branchName) {
+++    return runCommand(`git checkout -b ${branchName}`);
+++}
+++
+++function checkoutBranch(branchName) {
+++    return runCommand(`git checkout ${branchName}`);
+++}
+++
+++function isProtectedBranch(branch, protectedBranches) {
+++    return protectedBranches.some(pattern => {
+++        const regex = new RegExp('^' + pattern.replace(/\*/g, '.*') + '$');
+++        return regex.test(branch);
+++    });
+++}
+++
+++function isBotBranch(branch, prefix) {
+++    return branch.startsWith(prefix);
+++}
+++
+++function isBotCommit(message, markers) {
+++    return markers.some(marker => message.includes(marker));
+++}
+++
+++function applyPatchCheck(patchPath) {
+++    return runCommand(`git apply --check "${patchPath}"`);
+++}
+++
+++function applyPatch(patchPath) {
+++    return runCommand(`git apply "${patchPath}"`);
+++}
+++
+++function commitAll(message) {
+++    runCommand('git add .');
+++    return runCommand(`git commit -m "${message.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`);
+++}
+++
+++function pushBranch(branchName) {
+++    return runCommand(`git push origin ${branchName}`);
+++}
+++
+++function createPullRequest({ title, body, labels, draft }) {
+++    let cmd = `gh pr create --title "${title.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}" --body "${body.replace(/"/g, '\\"')}"`;
+++    if (draft) cmd += ' --draft';
+++    if (labels && labels.length > 0) {
+++        cmd += ` --label "${labels.join(',')}"`;
+++    }
+++    return runCommand(cmd);
+++}
+++
+++module.exports = {
+++    runCommand,
+++    getHeadSha,
+++    getShortSha,
+++    getCurrentBranch,
+++    getCommitMessage,
+++    ensureInsideGitRepo,
+++    ensureCleanWorkingTree,
+++    createBranch,
+++    checkoutBranch,
+++    isProtectedBranch,
+++    isBotBranch,
+++    isBotCommit,
+++    applyPatchCheck,
+++    applyPatch,
+++    commitAll,
+++    pushBranch,
+++    createPullRequest
+++};

+++const http = require('http');
+++const url = require('url');
+++
+++async function askProxima(message, model, baseUrl = 'http://localhost:3210') {
+++    const targetUrl = new url.URL('/v1/chat/completions', baseUrl);
+++    
+++    const payload = JSON.stringify({
+++        model: model,
+++        messages: [{ role: 'user', content: message }],
+++        stream: false
+++    });
+++
+++    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
+++        const req = http.request(targetUrl, {
+++            method: 'POST',
+++            headers: {
+++                'Content-Type': 'application/json',
+++                'Content-Length': Buffer.byteLength(payload)
+++            },
+++            timeout: 300000 // 5 minutes
+++        }, (res) => {
+++            let data = '';
+++            res.on('data', (chunk) => data += chunk);
+++            res.on('end', () => {
+++                if (res.statusCode >= 200 && res.statusCode < 300) {
+++                    try {
+++                        const json = JSON.parse(data);
+++                        if (json.choices && json.choices[0] && json.choices[0].message) {
+++                            resolve(json.choices[0].message.content);
+++                        } else {
+++                            reject(new Error('Unexpected response format from Proxima'));
+++                        }
+++                    } catch (e) {
+++                        reject(new Error(`Failed to parse Proxima response: ${e.message}`));
+++                    }
+++                } else {
+++                    reject(new Error(`Proxima returned status ${res.statusCode}: ${data}`));
+++                }
+++            });
+++        });
+++
+++        req.on('error', (e) => {
+++            if (e.code === 'ECONNREFUSED') {
+++                reject(new Error(`Proxima is unavailable at ${baseUrl}. Is it running?`));
+++            } else {
+++                reject(e);
+++            }
+++        });
+++
+++        req.on('timeout', () => {
+++            req.destroy();
+++            reject(new Error('Request to Proxima timed out'));
+++        });
+++
+++        req.write(payload);
+++        req.end();
+++    });
+++}
+++
+++module.exports = { askProxima };

+++function parseSeverityCounts(markdown) {
+++    const counts = { critical: 0, high: 0, medium: 0, low: 0 };
+++    
+++    // Look for severity indicators in tables or bullet points
+++    // Common markers: ╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓö£Γòó╬ô├╢├▒ Critical, ╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓò₧├åΓö£├¡ High, ╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓò₧├åΓö£┬í Medium, ╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓö£Γòó╬ô├▓├¡ Low
+++    // Or just "Critical", "High", etc.
+++    
+++    const lines = markdown.split('\n');
+++    for (const line of lines) {
+++        const lower = line.toLowerCase();
+++        if (lower.includes('critical')) counts.critical++;
+++        else if (lower.includes('high')) counts.high++;
+++        else if (lower.includes('medium')) counts.medium++;
+++        else if (lower.includes('low')) counts.low++;
+++    }
+++    
+++    // Note: This is a bit naive, it might overcount if the word appears in prose.
+++    // Better would be to target the "Bugs & Failure Modes" table specifically.
+++    const tableMatch = markdown.match(/## Bugs & Failure Modes\n([\s\S]*?)(?:\n##|$)/);
+++    if (tableMatch) {
+++        const table = tableMatch[1];
+++        const tableCounts = { critical: 0, high: 0, medium: 0, low: 0 };
+++        const tableLines = table.split('\n');
+++        for (const line of tableLines) {
+++            const lower = line.toLowerCase();
+++            if (lower.includes('critical')) tableCounts.critical++;
+++            else if (lower.includes('high')) tableCounts.high++;
+++            else if (lower.includes('medium')) tableCounts.medium++;
+++            else if (lower.includes('low')) tableCounts.low++;
+++        }
+++        return tableCounts;
+++    }
+++
+++    return counts;
+++}
+++
+++function parseScore(markdown) {
+++    const match = markdown.match(/## Score: (\d+)\/10/);
+++    return match ? parseInt(match[1]) : null;
+++}
+++
+++function hasCriticalOrHigh(markdown) {
+++    const counts = parseSeverityCounts(markdown);
+++    return counts.critical > 0 || counts.high > 0;
+++}
+++
+++module.exports = {
+++    parseSeverityCounts,
+++    parseScore,
+++    hasCriticalOrHigh
+++};
++diff --git a/scripts/lib/safety.cjs b/scripts/lib/safety.cjs
++new file mode 100644
++index 0000000..20f1d99
++--- /dev/null
+++++ b/scripts/lib/safety.cjs
++@@ -0,0 +1,75 @@
+++const path = require('path');
+++
+++function validatePatchText(patchText) {
+++    // Reject markdown fences
+++    if (patchText.includes('```')) {
+++        throw new Error('Patch contains markdown fences');
+++    }
+++    
+++    // Check if it looks like a unified diff
+++    if (!patchText.includes('--- ') || !patchText.includes('+++ ') || !patchText.includes('@@ ')) {
+++        throw new Error('Response does not appear to be a valid unified diff');
+++    }
+++    
+++    // Reject prose before/after diff (simple check)
+++    const lines = patchText.trim().split('\n');
+++    if (!lines[0].startsWith('--- ') && !lines[0].startsWith('Index: ') && !lines[0].startsWith('diff ')) {
+++        throw new Error('Patch contains prose before the diff');
+++    }
+++
+++    return true;
+++}
+++
+++function rejectDangerousPaths(patchText, gitRoot) {
+++    const lines = patchText.split('\n');
+++    for (const line of lines) {
+++        if (line.startsWith('--- ') || line.startsWith('+++ ')) {
+++            const filePath = line.substring(4).split('\t')[0].trim();
+++            if (filePath === '/dev/null') continue;
+++            
+++            // Remove a/ or b/ prefix
+++            const cleanPath = filePath.replace(/^[ab]\//, '');
+++            const absolutePath = path.resolve(gitRoot, cleanPath);
+++            
+++            if (!absolutePath.startsWith(gitRoot)) {
+++                throw new Error(`Patch modifies file outside repository: ${cleanPath}`);
+++            }
+++            
+++            const base = path.basename(cleanPath);
+++            if (cleanPath.startsWith('.git/') || base === '.env' || base === 'package-lock.json') {
+++                throw new Error(`Patch modifies protected file: ${cleanPath}`);
+++            }
+++            
+++            if (cleanPath.startsWith('.github/workflows/')) {
+++                throw new Error(`Patch modifies GitHub workflow: ${cleanPath}`);
+++            }
+++        }
+++    }
+++    return true;
+++}
+++
+++function rejectScriptExecution(content) {
+++    const dangerousPatterns = [
+++        /#!/,
+++        /powershell/i,
+++        /pwsh/i,
+++        /cmd\.exe/i,
+++        /bash/i,
+++        /sh /i,
+++        /\.ps1/i,
+++        /\.sh/i,
+++        /\.bat/i
+++    ];
+++    
+++    for (const pattern of dangerousPatterns) {
+++        if (pattern.test(content)) {
+++            throw new Error(`Response contains forbidden script pattern: ${pattern}`);
+++        }
+++    }
+++}
+++
+++module.exports = {
+++    validatePatchText,
+++    rejectDangerousPaths,
+++    rejectScriptExecution
+++};

+++const parser = require('./lib/review-parser.cjs');

+++
+++async function main() {
+++    const config = loadConfig();
+++    const args = process.argv.slice(2);
+++    
+++    const pushRequested = args.includes('--push') || config.pushBotBranches;
+++    const prRequested = args.includes('--create-pr') || config.createPullRequest;
+++    const allowDirty = args.includes('--allow-dirty');
+++
+++    git.ensureInsideGitRepo();
+++

+++    const shortSha = git.getShortSha(headSha);
+++    const currentBranch = git.getCurrentBranch();
+++    const commitMsg = git.getCommitMessage(headSha);
+++
+++    if (git.isProtectedBranch(currentBranch, config.protectedBranches)) {
+++        console.error(`Γò¼├┤Γö¼├æΓö£┬½ Refusing to run on protected branch: ${currentBranch}`);
+++        process.exit(1);
+++    }
+++
+++    if (!allowDirty && !git.ensureCleanWorkingTree()) {
+++        console.error('Γò¼├┤Γö¼├æΓö£┬½ Working tree is dirty. Commit your changes or use --allow-dirty.');
+++        process.exit(1);
+++    }
+++
+++    if (git.isBotCommit(commitMsg, config.skipReviewCommitMarkers)) {
+++        console.log('Γò¼├┤Γö£├áΓö¼├¡ Skipping bot commit.');
+++        process.exit(0);
+++    }
+++
+++    if (git.isBotBranch(currentBranch, config.repairBranchPrefix)) {
+++        console.log('Γò¼├┤Γö£├áΓö¼├¡ Skipping bot branch.');
+++        process.exit(0);
+++    }
+++
+++    const sessionDir = path.join(config.gitRoot, config.reviewDir, shortSha);
+++    if (!fs.existsSync(sessionDir)) fs.mkdirSync(sessionDir, { recursive: true });
+++
+++    let status = {
+++        commit: headSha,
+++        shortSha: shortSha,
+++        sourceBranch: currentBranch,
+++        status: "starting",
+++        testsPassed: false,
+++        patchApplied: false,
+++        branchPushed: false,
+++        prCreated: false,
+++        autoMerged: false,
+++        reviewSkippedForBotBranch: true
+++    };
+++
+++    const updateStatus = (updates) => {
+++        status = { ...status, ...updates };
+++        fs.writeFileSync(path.join(sessionDir, 'status.json'), JSON.stringify(status, null, 2), 'utf8');
+++    };
+++
+++    updateStatus({});
+++
+++    // 1. Run tests before repair
+++    console.log('╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓö¼ΓòæΓö¼┬╝ Running initial tests...');
+++    let testsPassed = true;
+++    for (const cmd of config.testCommands) {
+++        const res = git.runCommand(cmd);
+++        fs.appendFileSync(path.join(sessionDir, 'initial_tests.log'), res.stdout + res.stderr, 'utf8');
+++        if (!res.success) {
+++            testsPassed = false;
+++            if (config.stopOnTestFailure) break;
+++        }
+++    }
+++    updateStatus({ testsPassed });
+++
+++    // 2. Run Review
+++    console.log(`╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓö£ΓûÆΓö£Γòù Running Proxima review for ${shortSha}...`);
+++    const reviewDir = path.join(config.gitRoot, config.reviewDir);
+++    process.env.PROXIMA_REVIEW_DIR = reviewDir;

+++    
+++    // Call the existing review logic
+++    // We assume proxima-review.cjs exports runReview and it's compatible
+++    try {
+++        await runReview(headSha);
+++    } catch (e) {
+++        console.error(`Γò¼├┤Γö¼├æΓö£┬½ Review failed: ${e.message}`);
+++        updateStatus({ status: "proxima-unavailable" });
+++        process.exit(1);
+++    }
+++
+++    if (!fs.existsSync(reviewFile)) {
+++        console.error('Γò¼├┤Γö¼├æΓö£┬½ Review file was not generated.');
+++        updateStatus({ status: "proxima-unavailable" });
+++        process.exit(1);
+++    }
+++
+++    const reviewContent = fs.readFileSync(reviewFile, 'utf8');
+++    const counts = parser.parseSeverityCounts(reviewContent);
+++    const score = parser.parseScore(reviewContent);
+++
+++    updateStatus({
+++        score,
+++        critical: counts.critical,
+++        high: counts.high,
+++        medium: counts.medium,
+++        low: counts.low
+++    });
+++
+++    if (testsPassed && counts.critical === 0 && counts.high === 0) {
+++        console.log('Γò¼├┤Γö¼├║Γö£├í No critical/high findings and tests passed. Work completed.');
+++        updateStatus({ status: "clean" });
+++        process.exit(0);
+++    }
