+++    // 3. Iterative Repair
+++    let iteration = 1;
+++    const maxIterations = config.maxIterations || 3;

+++    while (iteration <= maxIterations) {
+++        console.log(`╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓö¼├│Γö£├¡ Repair iteration ${iteration}/${maxIterations}...`);
+++        const repairDir = path.join(sessionDir, 'repair');
+++        if (!fs.existsSync(repairDir)) fs.mkdirSync(repairDir, { recursive: true });
+++

+++        updateStatus({ botBranch });

+++        const prompt = `You are an expert software engineer. Based on the following code review and the original diff, generate a unified diff patch to fix the Critical and High findings.

+++2. NO markdown fences (e.g., \`\`\`diff).
+++3. NO prose, explanations, or introductory text.
+++4. NO shell scripts, PowerShell, or Bash commands.
+++5. Fix ONLY the issues identified in the review.
+++6. Ensure the patch is compatible with 'git apply'.
+++
+++--- REVIEW ---
+++${reviewContent}

+++                fs.writeFileSync(path.join(repairDir, 'apply.log'), `Validation failed:\n${checkRes.stderr}`, 'utf8');
+++                updateStatus({ status: "patch-failed" });

+++                console.error('Γò¼├┤Γö¼├æΓö£┬½ Patch application failed.');

+++            for (const cmd of config.testCommands) {
+++                const res = git.runCommand(cmd);
+++                fs.appendFileSync(path.join(repairDir, 'test.log'), res.stdout + res.stderr, 'utf8');
+++                if (!res.success) {
+++                    repairTestsPassed = false;
+++                    if (config.stopOnTestFailure) break;
+++                }
+++            }
+++
+++            if (repairTestsPassed) {
+++                console.log('Γò¼├┤Γö¼├║Γö£├í Tests passed on bot branch.');
+++                git.commitAll(`fix: address Proxima review ${shortSha} [proxima-auto-fix]`);
+++                
+++                if (pushRequested) {
+++                    console.log(`╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓö£┬úΓö£├º Pushing branch ${botBranch}...`);
+++                    const pushRes = git.pushBranch(botBranch);
+++                    if (pushRes.success) {
+++                        updateStatus({ branchPushed: true });
+++                    }
+++                }
+++
+++                if (prRequested) {
+++                    console.log('╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓö£ΓöñΓö¼├æ Creating Pull Request...');

+++`;

+++const assert = require('assert');
+++const parser = require('../lib/review-parser.cjs');
+++const safety = require('../lib/safety.cjs');
+++
+++function testReviewParser() {
+++    console.log('Testing Review Parser...');
+++    
+++    const mockReview = `
+++## Bugs & Failure Modes
+++| File:Line | Severity | Finding | Evidence |
+++|-----------|----------|---------|----------|
+++| src/file.js:L42 | ╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓö£Γòó╬ô├╢├▒ Critical | Race condition | Evidence 1 |
+++| src/util.js:L10 | ╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓò₧├åΓö£├¡ High | Missing check | Evidence 2 |
+++| src/main.js:L5 | ╬ô├½├¡Γò₧├åΓò₧├åΓö£┬í Medium | Optimization | Evidence 3 |
+++
+++## Score: 7/10
+++`;
+++
+++    const counts = parser.parseSeverityCounts(mockReview);
+++    assert.strictEqual(counts.critical, 1, 'Critical count should be 1');
+++    assert.strictEqual(counts.high, 1, 'High count should be 1');
+++    assert.strictEqual(counts.medium, 1, 'Medium count should be 1');

++# Proxima Local Automation Workflow
++
++This document describes the automated code review and repair workflow that runs locally on your machine.
++
++## Overview
++
++Proxima's local automation workflow is designed to be local-first, avoiding external API keys and using your existing browser sessions. It automates the process of reviewing commits, generating fixes (as patches), and validating them on a local bot branch.
++
++### Key Principles
++
++- **Local-Only**: No GitHub Actions or external cloud runners required.
++- **No API Keys**: Uses Proxima's local REST API (which utilizes your logged-in browser sessions).
++- **Safe Execution**: AI-generated fixes are applied only as unified diff patches after a `git apply --check` validation.
++- **Iterative**: The workflow can run multiple iterations (default 3) until tests pass and no critical/high issues remain.
++- **Human-in-the-loop**: Bot branches are pushed to GitHub for final review and merge by a human or Cursor.
++
++## Architecture
++
++The automation consists of three main components:
++
++1.  **Reviewer (`cli/proxima-review.cjs`)**: Performs the initial code audit.
++2.  **Repairer (`scripts/proxima-repair.cjs`)**: Generates a unified diff patch based on the review findings.
++3.  **Orchestrator (`scripts/proxima-loop.cjs`)**: Manages the end-to-end loop (test -> review -> repair -> apply -> test -> commit -> push).
++
++## Setup
++
++1.  **Ensure Proxima is running**: The automation requires the Proxima Agent Hub to be active.
++2.  **Enable REST API**: The repairer communicates with Proxima via `http://localhost:3210`.
++3.  **Install Dependencies**: Ensure you have run `npm install`.
++4.  **Configuration**: Customize the behavior in `proxima-automation.config.json`.
++
++## Usage
++
++Run the automation from the root of your repository:
++
++```bash
++# Run local review and repair loop (no push)
++npm run loop:local
++
++# Run loop and push the bot branch to GitHub
++npm run loop:local:push
++
++# Run loop, push branch, and create a draft Pull Request (requires 'gh' CLI)
++npm run loop:local:pr
++```
++
++## Bot Branches and Commits
++
++- **Branch format**: `proxima/fix-<shortSha>-iter-<n>`
++- **Commit message**: `fix: address Proxima review <shortSha> [proxima-auto-fix]`
++
++**Note**: Automatic reviews are automatically skipped for branches matching `proxima/fix-*` and commits containing `[proxima-auto-fix]`.
++
++## Recovery and Troubleshooting
++
++- **Proxima Unavailable**: If the Agent Hub is not running, the script will exit with an error and update `status.json`.
++- **Patch Failed**: If a generated patch cannot be applied cleanly, the script stops and saves the error to `repair/apply.log`.
++- **Tests Failed**: If tests fail after a patch is applied, the script will record the failure in `repair/test.log` and stop the current iteration.
++- **Max Iterations**: If the issues are not resolved after the configured `maxIterations`, the script exits with `max-iterations-reached` status.
++
++Check the `status.json` file in `/root/review/<shortSha>/` for detailed execution state.

++# Proxima Code Review Pipeline
++
++## Overview
++
++The Proxima review pipeline automatically reviews git commits using an LLM (ChatGPT or Perplexity). It can be triggered manually or automatically via a pre-push git hook.
++
++## Triggering Reviews
++
++### Manual Run
++```bash
++node C:\Users\subha\Documents\PROJECTS\Proxima\cli\proxima-review.cjs HEAD
++node C:\Users\subha\Documents\PROJECTS\Proxima\cli\proxima-review.cjs <commit-sha>
++```
++
++### Automatic (Pre-push Hook)
++The script reads commit SHAs from stdin when triggered by git's pre-push hook. It spawns background processes to review each new commit without blocking the push.
++
++## Pipeline Flow
++
++```
++1. Get commit info (sha, shortSha, msg, author, date)
++2. Skip maintenance/chore commits (save quota)
++3. Check if review already exists (root or resolved folder)
++4. Acquire lock (prevent concurrent reviews)
++5. Get git diff (limited to 2MB)
++6. Send diff + adversarial prompt to LLM via IPC
++7. Save review to perplexity-reviews/<shortSha>.md
++8. Release lock
++```
++
++## IPC Communication
++
++- **Protocol**: TCP socket on port 19222 (direct IPC to Proxima Agent Hub)
++- **ChatGPT**: `sendMessage` with `{ message, model }`
++- **Perplexity**: `sendMessage` with `{ message, modelPreference, deepSearch: false }`
++
++## Review Model Configuration
++
++Set via environment variable `PROXIMA_REVIEW_MODEL`:
++- `gpt-*` ╬ô├Ñ├å routes to ChatGPT
++- `claude *` ╬ô├Ñ├å routes to Perplexity (via Claude)
++- anything else ╬ô├Ñ├å routes to Perplexity
++
++Default: `gpt-5-5-thinking`
++
++## The Adversarial Prompt
++
++The LLM is instructed to act as a **hostile, adversarial code auditor** with these rules:
++
++1. **Never trust the commit message** ╬ô├ç├╢ treat it as a hypothesis to be tested
++2. **Do NOT mirror the commit message** ╬ô├ç├╢ summarize what the code actually does
++3. **Assume the author is wrong** until code proves otherwise
++4. **Generic advice is forbidden** ╬ô├ç├╢ every point must cite specific lines
++5. **Use web search** to verify API contracts and security advisories
++
++### Required Sections in Review:
++- **Verdict**: PASS / FAIL / NEEDS WORK
++- **Implementation vs Intent Gap**: Divergences between stated intent and actual code
++- **Bugs & Failure Modes**: Table with file:line, severity, finding, evidence
++- **Missing Changes**: Related code that should have been updated
++- **Security & Data Integrity**: Worst-case impact if code is wrong
++- **Score**: X/10 with justification
++
++## Output
++
++Reviews are saved to:
++- `<repo-root>/perplexity-reviews/<shortSha>.md` ╬ô├ç├╢ new reviews
++- `<repo-root>/perplexity-reviews/resolved/<shortSha>.md` ╬ô├ç├╢ resolved/archived reviews
++- `<repo-root>/perplexity-reviews/error-<shortSha>.md` ╬ô├ç├╢ failed reviews
++- `<repo-root>/perplexity-reviews/background.log` ╬ô├ç├╢ background process logs
++
++## Lock Mechanism
++
++- Universal lock file in `~/.proxima-review.lock`
++- Prevents cross-project Hub overloading
++- 15-minute stale timeout
++- Retries for up to 60 minutes if lock is held

++
++# Code Review: 11dc5df6
++
++> Implement global lock and queueing (wait in line) logic
++
++The wrong skill loaded ╬ô├ç├╢ let me proceed directly with the code review using my analysis of the diff.
++
++## Summary
++
++This commit moves the global lock file from within the project's `REVIEW_DIR` to `~/.proxima-review.lock` in the home directory (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L26`), making it a machine-wide cross-project lock. It also replaces the old "skip-immediately-if-locked" behavior with a new `acquireLockWithRetry()` async polling loop (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L67╬ô├ç├┤L90`) that waits up to 60 minutes, polling every 10 seconds, before giving up. The stale-lock warning log line was removed silently. I did not need to look up external API docs for this change, as it is entirely self-contained Node.js `fs`, `os`, and async timer usage.
++
++***
++
++## What's Good
++
++- **Atomic lock write is preserved** ╬ô├ç├╢ `{ flag: 'wx' }` on `fs.writeFileSync` (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L58`) is the correct pattern to prevent TOCTOU races on the creation side. This was good before and remains intact.
++- **Silent unlink on `releaseLock`** ╬ô├ç├╢ `try { fs.unlinkSync(LOCK_FILE); } catch {}` (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L93`) is a clean, non-throwing release that avoids crashes if the file was already cleaned up.
++- **Cross-project isolation is the right design call** ╬ô├ç├╢ moving the lock to `os.homedir()` (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L26`) is the correct architectural decision for preventing multiple repo hooks from hammering the same AI hub simultaneously. The code comment explains *why* clearly.
++- **Repo identity added to lock metadata** ╬ô├ç├╢ writing `repo: findGitRoot()` into the lock file (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L60`) is a thoughtful observability improvement; it lets you inspect which project is holding the lock.
++- **Graceful catch on stale unlink** ╬ô├ç├╢ wrapping the stale-lock `unlinkSync` in a silent try/catch (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L47`) is correct; a race between two processes discovering a stale lock simultaneously should not crash either one.
++- **`firstWait` flag gives a clean "waiting..." vs "acquired" UX** ╬ô├ç├╢ printing the wait message only once (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L78╬ô├ç├┤L82`) instead of on every poll is good terminal hygiene.
++
++***
++
++## Issues & Suggestions
++
++- **`maxWaitMs` default of 60 minutes is unreasonably long for a CLI tool** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L68`). A git hook or CI runner will silently stall for an hour if anything goes wrong. This should be at most 10╬ô├ç├┤15 minutes (or configurable via `PROXIMA_REVIEW_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS`). Consider also printing a "still waiting... (Xm elapsed)" heartbeat every minute so the user knows the process hasn't frozen.
++
++- **Stale lock warning log was silently removed** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L43`). The old line `console.log(yellow('╬ô├£├í') + ' Stale lock found (from ' + lock.shortSha + '), clearing')` was deleted without replacement. If a stale lock is detected and cleared, users lose all visibility. This is an operational regression ╬ô├ç├╢ restoring the warning (or replacing it with `log(...)` using the passed-in logger) is important for diagnosing stuck/crashed CI jobs.
++
++- **`acquireLockWithRetry` logs via `log` but is called before `log` is defined in `runReview`** ╬ô├ç├╢ if `log` itself depends on the review context (e.g., prefixes the SHA), the "Waiting in line" message at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L79` may emit before the caller has set up its log context. Verify the `log` closure captures everything it needs before the `await acquireLockWithRetry(sha, log)` call at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L244`.
++
++- **`tryAcquireLock` no longer creates `REVIEW_DIR`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L39`). The removed `if (!fs.existsSync(REVIEW_DIR)) fs.mkdirSync(REVIEW_DIR, { recursive: true })` guard was responsible for ensuring the output directory exists before writing. This responsibility now implicitly falls on something else. If `REVIEW_DIR` creation is not guaranteed elsewhere before first use, the rest of the program will crash when it tries to write a review file. This needs to be moved to an explicit initialization step in `runReview` or early in the program.
++
++- **No polling progress message over time** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L83╬ô├ç├┤L87`). After the initial "Waiting in line..." message, the process is silent for up to 60 minutes. Any developer watching the terminal will think the process is hung. Add a periodic log every ~60 seconds: e.g., `log(dim('   Still waiting... (' + Math.round((Date.now() - start) / 60000) + 'm elapsed)'))`.
++
++- **`findGitRoot()` is called inside `JSON.stringify(...)` inside `tryAcquireLock`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L60`). This is called in a tight retry loop (every 10 seconds). `findGitRoot()` almost certainly calls `execSync('git rev-parse --show-toplevel')` ╬ô├ç├╢ spawning a subprocess on every failed lock attempt over 60 minutes is wasteful. Cache the value once at module load time or pass it in as a parameter.
++
++- **No check that the waiting `commitSha` doesn't match the lock holder's SHA** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L68╬ô├ç├┤L90`). If the same commit triggers two hook invocations simultaneously (e.g., a rebase loop or force-push race), both processes will wait for each other indefinitely until timeout. Adding a check like `if (lock.sha === commitSha) { throw new Error('Deadlock: same commit already holds the lock'); }` would short-circuit this.
++
++***
++
++## Security & Performance
++
++- **Lock file in home directory is world-readable on shared machines** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L26`). `~/.proxima-review.lock` is created without explicit permissions. On multi-user machines or shared CI agents, other users could read the lock file and learn which git repo is currently being reviewed (via the `repo` field). Consider writing with `{ mode: 0o600 }` in `fs.writeFileSync` to restrict read access to the owner.
++
++- **Polling every 10 seconds Γö£├╣ 360 iterations = 3600 `fs.existsSync` + `JSON.parse` + `execSync` calls at worst** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L84`). This is negligible for a developer workstation but is meaningless overhead on a CI runner with a 10-minute timeout. Pair a lower default `maxWaitMs` with an exponential backoff (e.g., 10s ╬ô├Ñ├å 20s ╬ô├Ñ├å 30s, capped at 60s) to reduce syscall churn over long waits.
++
++- **`JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(...))` in `tryAcquireLock` has no size guard** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L43`). A maliciously crafted or corrupted lock file with a very large payload could cause memory issues. A simple `stat` size check before parsing (reject if > 1KB) would eliminate this theoretical risk.
++
++***
++
++## Overall Score: 7/10
++
++The architectural intent is correct ╬ô├ç├╢ a home-directory lock is the right solution for cross-project serialization. The new retry/queue behavior is a significant UX improvement over silently skipping. However, the 60-minute default timeout is dangerously long for a CLI tool, the silent removal of the stale-lock warning is an operational regression, the `REVIEW_DIR` initialization concern could cause a hard crash on first use, and the `findGitRoot()` call inside the polling hot path is a minor but unnecessary inefficiency.
++
++***
++
++## Recommendations
++
++1. **Restore the stale-lock warning** at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L43` ╬ô├ç├╢ replace the removed `console.log` with `log(yellow('╬ô├£├í') + ...)` using the passed-in logger for consistency.
++2. **Reduce default `maxWaitMs` from 60 min to 10 min** at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L68` and expose it as `PROXIMA_REVIEW_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` env var.
++3. **Move `REVIEW_DIR` mkdir back** to `runReview()` initialization (or keep it in `tryAcquireLock` at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L39`) ╬ô├ç├╢ verify it isn't silently missing elsewhere.
++4. **Add a per-minute heartbeat** inside the `while` loop in `acquireLockWithRetry` (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L83`) so long waits are visible.
++5. **Cache `findGitRoot()`** ╬ô├ç├╢ hoist the call out of the `tryAcquireLock` inner logic and pass the result as a parameter to avoid repeated `execSync` calls during polling.
++6. **Add same-SHA deadlock detection** ╬ô├ç├╢ check `lock.sha === commitSha` early in `tryAcquireLock` and throw rather than wait.
++7. **Write lock file with `mode: 0o600`** at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L55╬ô├ç├┤L60` on shared/CI machines to prevent information disclosure via the `repo` path in lock metadata.

++
++# Code Review: 31718509
++
++> Address Perplexity review 9c14c39c: Restore Windows silent spawn and optimize gitRoot calls
++
++Now I have more than enough to write a thorough, research-backed review. Let me put it together.
++
++***
++
++## Summary
++
++This commit adds Windows-specific spawn logic to `spawnBackground()` using `cmd.exe /c start "" /b` to suppress a new console window on detached Node.js processes, and hoists the `findGitRoot()` call into a shared variable to avoid calling it twice. The motivation is to fix a known Windows behavior: `windowsHide: true` is documented to be silently ignored by the OS when `detached: true` is also set for console-subsystem processes. The `cmd.exe start /b` approach is a recognized workaround. I verified the behavior of `start /b`, the `""` empty title quoting requirement, Node.js stdio redirection across a `cmd.exe` shell intermediary, and `process.execPath`/`__filename` path-with-spaces behavior on Windows against Node.js official docs and community references.
++
++***
++
++## What's Good
++
++- **`findGitRoot()` deduplication** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L394-L395`). The prior code called `findGitRoot()` inline inside the `spawn` options object. Hoisting it into `const gitRoot` at the top of the function means a single call regardless of the `isWin` branch taken ╬ô├ç├╢ clean and correct.
++- **Accurate platform detection** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L394`). `process.platform === 'win32'` is the correct Node.js idiom for detecting Windows ╬ô├ç├╢ it covers all modern Win32 builds including 64-bit Windows where `platform` is still `'win32'`.
++- **Empty title `""` before `/b`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L415`). `cmd.exe start` interprets the first double-quoted string as the window title, not the executable path. The `""` prevents `process.execPath` from being swallowed as a title when it is quoted (which it may need to be on paths with spaces). This is a subtle but essential correctness detail, and getting it right is good.
++- **Removed `windowsHide: true` from Unix path** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L425-L430`). Since `windowsHide` has no effect on non-Windows platforms per the Node.js docs, removing it from the Unix branch is a clean trim of dead options.
++- **Non-Windows branch preserved cleanly** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L423-L430`). The original clean Unix spawn logic is reproduced faithfully with no unintended changes ╬ô├ç├╢ a safe split.
++
++***
++
++## Issues & Suggestions
++
++**[CRITICAL] `stdio` redirection is broken for the Windows `cmd.exe` path**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L416-L420`
++
++When you spawn `cmd.exe` as the process, the `stdio` option in Node.js applies to the `cmd.exe` process itself ╬ô├ç├╢ **not** to the child it launches via `start /b`. The inner Node.js process (`process.execPath`) is spawned by `cmd.exe` as a fully independent process with its own stdio handles, completely disconnected from the file descriptors `out` passed in `['ignore', out, out]`. As a result, the background review worker's stdout/stderr will **not** appear in `background.log` ╬ô├ç├╢ they will be lost. This silently defeats the entire purpose of the log-file setup at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L410-L412` for Windows users.
++
++To actually redirect stdout/stderr into the log file via `cmd.exe start`, the redirection must be inside the `start` command's arguments using shell syntax:
++
++```js
++// Correct Windows approach: redirect via shell within the cmd invocation
++const logFilePath = path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'background.log');
++child = spawn('cmd.exe', [
++    '/c',
++    `start "" /b ${process.execPath} ${__filename} ${sha} >>"${logFilePath}" 2>&1`
++], {
++    detached: true,
++    stdio: 'ignore',
++    cwd: gitRoot,
++    env: cleanEnv,
++    windowsHide: true
++});
++```
++
++However, this introduces path-quoting complexity (see below). Alternatively, consider abandoning the `cmd.exe` wrapper and instead using `{windowsHide: true, detached: true, shell: false}` directly ╬ô├ç├╢ which, per community testing, does suppress the window on most modern Windows 10/11 + Node versions even though the docs warn otherwise. Or, write the child's own output to the log from *within* the child process itself at startup.
++
++***
++
++**[CRITICAL] `process.execPath` and `__filename` are not quoted ╬ô├ç├╢ will break on paths with spaces**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L415`
++
++```js
++['/c', 'start', '""', '/b', process.execPath, __filename, sha]
++```
++
++When Node.js calls `spawn('cmd.exe', args, ...)`, it joins these arguments into a command line string for the Windows process creation API. `process.execPath` on a typical Windows installation resolves to something like `C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe`, which contains a space. Without quoting, `cmd.exe` will interpret `Files\nodejs\node.exe` as a separate token and `start` will fail silently or launch the wrong executable. You must quote both:
++
++```js
++['/c', `start "" /b "${process.execPath}" "${__filename}" "${sha}"`]
++// with shell: false (default) or using windowsVerbatimArguments: true
++```
++
++Or pass it as a single shell string with `{shell: true}`. Note that SHA values won't contain spaces, but the defensive quoting is still required for the paths.
++
++***
++
++**[MAJOR] `child.unref()` may not detach the grandchild correctly via `cmd.exe`**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs` (following `L432`, not shown in diff but affected)
++
++`child.unref()` detaches the `cmd.exe` *wrapper* process from Node's event loop, but the actual background Node.js worker is a grandchild spawned by `cmd.exe`. Once `cmd.exe` finishes executing the `start /b` command, it exits ╬ô├ç├╢ and whether the grandchild stays alive depends on whether Windows has properly detached it. With `start /b` and `detached: true` on `cmd.exe`, the grandchild *should* survive, but there is no deterministic guarantee that `child.unref()` on the intermediate `cmd.exe` process provides the same lifecycle semantics as calling it directly on the worker process. This should be stress-tested and documented with a comment.
++
++***
++
++**[MODERATE] `windowsHide: true` is still passed on the Windows branch but remains ineffective**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L421`
++
++The comment at `L407-L410` explicitly explains that `windowsHide` is ignored when `detached: true` is used ╬ô├ç├╢ that's the entire reason for the `cmd.exe` workaround. Yet `windowsHide: true` is still present in the Windows spawn options at `L421`. This is dead/contradictory code and will confuse future readers. It should be removed from the Windows branch.
++
++***
++
++**[MODERATE] The `cmd.exe`/`start` approach makes `cwd` semantically unreliable**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L419`
++
++The `cwd` option passed to the outer `spawn` sets the working directory of `cmd.exe`, and `cmd.exe` will inherit that as the CWD for the `start /b` child. However, `start /b` can override this via its own working-directory semantics, and when paths are not quoted correctly (see CRITICAL above), `cmd.exe` may not launch the process from the intended `gitRoot`. If the `gitRoot` path itself contains spaces, this silently fails. The robustness of this entire approach depends on the quoting being correct end-to-end.
++
++***
++
++**[MINOR] No error event handler on `child` for the Windows branch**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L413-L422`
++
++If `cmd.exe` itself fails to spawn (e.g., `PATH` manipulation in `cleanEnv` removes it, or the system is in a broken state), the `spawn` call will emit an `'error'` event that goes unhandled and crashes the parent process. The Unix branch has the same issue, but the Windows path is more fragile because it chains through a shell. A minimal defensive handler should be attached:
++
++```js
++child.on('error', (err) => {
++    // Best-effort: log to stderr; parent should not crash on background spawn failure
++    process.stderr.write(`[proxima] background spawn failed: ${err.message}\n`);
++});
++```
++
++***
++
++## Security & Performance
++
++**Security:**
++The `cleanEnv` construction (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L397-L405`) strips all `GIT_*` variables, which is the correct approach to prevent environment injection from parent Git hooks into the child process. However, on the Windows `cmd.exe` path, the command line is assembled from `process.execPath`, `__filename`, and `sha` without sanitization. While `sha` is a Git SHA (hex-safe), if `process.execPath` or `__filename` contains characters with special meaning in `cmd.exe` (e.g., `&`, `|`, `^`, `%` via unusual install paths or enterprise environments), they could be interpreted as shell metacharacters by `cmd.exe`. Proper quoting with `"` around each path mitigates this but does not fully eliminate the risk if paths contain embedded double quotes .
++
++**Performance:**
++The `findGitRoot()` deduplication (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L395`) is a small but correct optimization. No other performance concerns introduced by this diff.
++
++***
++
++## Overall Score: 5/10
++
++The intent and diagnosis are exactly right ╬ô├ç├╢ `windowsHide` really is unreliable on Windows with `detached: true`, and the `cmd.exe start /b` pattern is a legitimate workaround. The `findGitRoot()` deduplication is clean. However, the implementation has two critical correctness bugs: stdio redirection is silently broken for the Windows path (logs will be lost), and unquoted paths will cause silent failures for any user with spaces in their Node.js install path (essentially all Windows users who installed to `Program Files`). These are not edge cases ╬ô├ç├╢ they are the norm on Windows.
++
++***
++
++## Recommendations
++
++1. **(Highest Priority) Fix stdio redirection on the Windows branch** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L416-L420`). Either move the log-file redirection inside the shell command string (e.g., `>>"${logFile}" 2>&1` appended to the `start` invocation), or redesign the child process to open and write to `background.log` itself at startup. The current code silently discards all output on Windows.
++
++2. **(Highest Priority) Quote `process.execPath` and `__filename` in the Windows args array** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L415`). Both must be wrapped in double quotes to handle spaces in paths like `C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe`. Consider using a single shell-string argument rather than an args array to have full control over quoting.
++
++3. **(Medium Priority) Remove `windowsHide: true` from the Windows branch** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L421`). It is explicitly called out as ineffective in the comment above it, and leaving it in is misleading. Delete it from the Windows spawn options.
++
++4. **(Medium Priority) Add a `child.on('error', ...)` handler** on both branches (Windows path most urgently). A silent spawn failure should not crash the parent process running inside a Git hook.
++
++5. **(Low Priority) Add a code comment explaining the `child.unref()` lifecycle** for the Windows grandchild scenario ╬ô├ç├╢ specifically that `cmd.exe` exits after launching the worker and the worker should survive independently, so future maintainers understand the expected process tree behavior.

++---
++
++# Code Review: 3d894744
++
++> Enhance pre-push hook background review spawning and unsetting git environment variables
++
++## Summary
++
++This commit improves the `spawnBackground` function in `proxima-review.cjs` and the generated shell hook in `install-hooks.cjs`. The key changes are: stripping `GIT_*` environment variables from the background child process (preventing git command interference), routing background process stdout/stderr into a persistent log file, adding Windows compatibility via `cmd.exe /c start /b`, introducing a `logToFile` utility so background-mode messages persist, fixing a new-branch `git rev-list` bug with `-n 1`, and hardcoding the Node.js executable path in the generated shell hook instead of relying on ambient `node` in `$PATH`.
++
++***
++
++## What's Good
++
++- **`GIT_*` env stripping is the right fix.** Git sets variables like `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, and `GIT_WORK_TREE` in hook environments. These break child `git` invocations. The allowlist-by-exclusion pattern (`!key.startsWith('GIT_')`) is a well-known, battle-tested mitigation.
++- **`PROXIMA_BACKGROUND_REVIEW=1` flag is clean.** Using an env flag to toggle dual logging (`console.log` + `logToFile`) rather than threading a boolean argument through the call stack is idiomatic and low-coupling.
++- **`logToFile` silent catch `{}` is acceptable here.** Logging must never crash the reviewer; swallowing errors on a non-critical diagnostic path is justified. Good judgment call.
++- **Hardcoding `process.execPath` in the hook** (`NODE_EXE`) avoids PATH resolution failures in environments like VS Code's Git integration or CI runners, where `node` may not be on `$PATH` for shell hooks. This is a meaningful reliability improvement.
++- **Windows `windowsHide: true`** prevents a terminal window flashing on Windows during background spawn ╬ô├ç├╢ a polished UX detail.
++- **`-n 1` fix for new branches** (`remoteSha === NULL_SHA`) is a real bug fix. Previously `git rev-list --reverse <sha>` on a new branch would walk the entire commit history, potentially triggering hundreds of reviews. Limiting to 1 is correct for the "only review the tip" intent.
++
++***
++
++## Issues & Suggestions
++
++- **`cmd.exe /c start /b` doesn't detach cleanly on all Windows versions.** The `start /b` command runs in the same console window and is not equivalent to Unix `detached: true`. The process may still be tied to the parent's console session. A more robust Windows approach is to use `spawn('node', [...], { detached: true, ... })` directly ╬ô├ç├╢ the `cmd.exe` wrapper adds unnecessary complexity without guaranteed detachment.
++
++  ```js
++  // Simpler and more reliable on Windows too:
++  const child = spawn(process.execPath, [__filename, sha], {
++      detached: true,
++      stdio: ['ignore', out, out],
++      cwd: findGitRoot(),
++      env: cleanEnv,
++      windowsHide: true
++  });
++  ```
++
++- **`fs.openSync` file descriptor is never closed (`out` leaks).** After `child.unref()`, the `out` fd remains open in the parent process. On high-frequency pushes this accumulates. You should close it after spawn:
++
++  ```js
++  child.unref();
++  fs.closeSync(out); // ╬ô├Ñ├ë add this
++  ```
++
++- **`logToFile` duplicates directory-creation logic.** `REVIEW_DIR` existence is checked in at least three places now (`runReview`, `spawnBackground`, `logToFile`). Extract a `ensureReviewDir()` helper called once at startup or lazily, not scattered across functions.
++
++- **`log` function in `runReview` strips ANSI when writing to file.** `logToFile` writes raw strings including ANSI escape codes (e.g. `cyan(...)`, `red(...)`). Log files will contain garbled color codes like `\u001b[36mΓëí╞Æ├▒├╗\u001b[0m`. Strip colors before writing to file:
++
++  ```js
++  const stripAnsi = (s) => s.replace(/\x1b\[[0-9;]*m/g, '');
++  fs.appendFileSync(logFile, `[${new Date().toISOString()}] ${stripAnsi(msg)}\n`, 'utf8');
++  ```
++
++- **`-n 1` for new branches may miss multi-commit initial pushes.** If a developer creates a branch and pushes 5 commits at once, only HEAD is reviewed. This may be intentional, but it's worth a comment clarifying the decision:
++
++  ```js
++  // New branch: review only HEAD to avoid reviewing entire repo history
++  ? '-n 1 ' + localSha
++  ```
++
++- **`install-hooks.cjs` escaping inconsistency.** The fix to `\$REVIEW_SCRIPT` and `\$NODE_EXE` in the generated shell script is correct (they must not be interpolated at hook-generation time), but the surrounding variables like `"$REVIEW_SCRIPT"` (non-escaped) and `"${unixPath}"` (JS template literal) in the same heredoc create a subtle mental model mismatch. Consider adding a comment block at the top of `buildHookScript` clarifying which variables are JS-interpolated vs. shell-runtime.
++
++***
++
++## Security & Performance
++
++- **Environment inheritance is conservative but potentially leaky.** The `cleanEnv` strips `GIT_*` but passes everything else ╬ô├ç├╢ including `npm_*`, `NODE_*`, credentials in env vars, CI secrets, etc. ╬ô├ç├╢ to an unmanaged detached background process. For a tool installed on developer machines this is likely acceptable, but worth noting in documentation: background processes inherit the full shell environment minus Git internals.
++- **Log file has no rotation or size cap.** `background.log` is append-only with no truncation. On active repos over time this file will grow unboundedly. A simple size-check-and-rotate or a max-lines cap would prevent disk issues.
++- **`fs.openSync` before `spawn` is a TOCTOU-safe pattern**, which is good ╬ô├ç├╢ the fd is opened before the child is created, so the child always inherits a valid write target. This is the correct ordering.
++- **No sanitization of `sha` passed to `cmd.exe` args on Windows.** While SHAs are hex-only and safe in practice, if `sha` ever originates from user-controlled input (e.g., branch names), the Windows `cmd.exe` invocation path is a command-injection surface. Low risk currently, but worth a `sha.match(/^[0-9a-f]+$/)` guard.
++
++***
++
++## Overall Score: 7.5/10
++
++This is a solid, practical commit that fixes a real bug (GIT_* env pollution) and several reliability gaps (PATH, new-branch rev-list, Windows UX). The implementation is readable and intentional. The score is held back by the fd leak, the ANSI codes in log files, and the over-engineered Windows spawn path that doesn't fully deliver on its promise.
++
++***
++
++## Recommendations
++
++1. **Fix the `fs.openSync` fd leak** ╬ô├ç├╢ add `fs.closeSync(out)` immediately after `child.unref()`. This is the only functional bug introduced.
++2. **Strip ANSI escape codes in `logToFile`** ╬ô├ç├╢ log files with raw color codes are unusable in most editors and `grep` workflows.
++3. **Simplify Windows spawning** ╬ô├ç├╢ drop the `cmd.exe /c start /b` wrapper and use `spawn(process.execPath, ...)` with `detached: true` directly; it works on Windows too.
++4. **Add log rotation** ╬ô├ç├╢ even a simple "truncate if > 1MB" guard on `background.log` prevents a long-term disk leak.
++5. **Consolidate `ensureReviewDir()`** ╬ô├ç├╢ deduplicate the three `existsSync/mkdirSync` patterns into one shared call.
++6. **Document the `-n 1` new-branch behavior** ╬ô├ç├╢ clarify in a comment whether reviewing only HEAD on new branches is by design.

++# Code Review: 51b6ecc9
++
++> Add Deep Research quota-aware routing with fallbacks
++
++This commit (`51b6ecc9`) was already reviewed earlier in this conversation. Rather than repeating the same analysis, here is the full review for your records ╬ô├ç├╢ incorporating everything identified the first time, presented as a clean standalone document.
++
++***
++
++## Summary
++
++This commit introduces quota-aware routing for Deep Research mode in `perplexity-engine.js`. It adds `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` to pre-flight the `/rest/rate-limit/status` endpoint before deciding whether to route a query as `agentic_research` or fall back to `copilot` mode. It wires in dynamic `supported_block_use_cases`, Deep Research-specific API params (`extended_context`, `always_search_override`, `search_mode`, etc.), and removes the old `deepSearchCount` counter from the MCP tools in `mcp-server-v3.js`. The MCP changes are minor cleanups ╬ô├ç├╢ splitting one-liner `toolResponse(await ...)` calls into two-line `const result = await ...; return toolResponse(result)` patterns and minor description wording tweaks. The intent is sound but the implementation is not production-ready as submitted.
++
++***
++
++## What's Good
++
++- **Graceful fallback design**: `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` returns `{ available: true, remaining: -1 }` on both network failure and unexpected response shapes, ensuring Deep Research requests degrade to Pro Search rather than crashing the call.
++- **Separation of concerns**: Extracting quota-checking into its own named function rather than inlining it in `send()` is the right architectural choice ╬ô├ç├╢ it is independently testable and readable.
++- **Dynamic `supported_block_use_cases`**: Conditionally pushing `workflow_steps` and `background_agents` only for `agentic_research` mode is correct and semantically clean.
++- **Replacing the counter with a live API check**: A live quota check from the API is strictly more correct than a manual local counter, which could desync after app restarts, crashes, or external usage.
++- **MCP result variable refactor**: Splitting `toolResponse(await p.instance.chat(...))` into `const result = await ...; return toolResponse(result)` is a minor but valid readability improvement that also creates a future insertion point for logging or transformation.
++
++***
++
++## Issues & Suggestions
++
++- **`var` throughout an async/modern codebase** (`perplexity-engine.js`, multiple lines): `_checkDeepResearchQuota`, `send()`, and the new param-building block all use `var` (`var res`, `var data`, `var agenticData`, `var quota`, `var params`, `var isDeepSearch`, `var finalMode`, `var supportedBlocks`). The MCP file uses `const`/`let` consistently. In async functions, `var`'s function-scoped hoisting is especially error-prone. **Replace every `var` with `const` or `let`.**
++
++- **Indentation regression on `var params`** (`perplexity-engine.js`, line ~307): The `var params = {` block has zero indentation while the surrounding code is indented. The `version: '2.18'` key-value pair inside the object is also flush against column 0 while all other keys are indented. This is a whitespace bug likely caused by a careless manual edit. Fix indentation throughout.
++
++- **Comment indentation lost in `mcp-server-v3.js`** (line ~562): The `// If using Perplexity...` comment lost its indentation and sits at column 0. Minor, but reveals the diff was applied sloppily.
++
++- **`quota.remaining === 0` edge case is over-blocking**: The condition `quota.available === false || quota.remaining === 0` will block Deep Research when `remaining` is `0`. However, the fallback also sets `remaining: 0` when `remaining_detail` is absent. If the API returns a shape where `agenticData` exists but `remaining_detail` is null, `remaining` becomes `0` and the gate incorrectly blocks an available-but-uncounted quota. Separate the two conditions:
++  ```js
++  if (!quota.available) { /* block */ }
++  else if (quota.remaining !== -1 && quota.remaining === 0) { /* block */ }
++  ```
++
++- **No timeout on the quota `fetch`**: If `https://www.perplexity.ai/rest/rate-limit/status` hangs, the entire `send()` call will hang indefinitely. Add an `AbortController` with a short timeout (e.g., 3 seconds):
++  ```js
++  const controller = new AbortController();
++  const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 3000);
++  const res = await fetch(url, { signal: controller.signal, ... });
++  clearTimeout(timer);
++  ```
++
++- **Raw SSE logging left in production path** (`perplexity-engine.js`, line ~149): `console.log('[RAW SSE]', data.substring(0, 500))` fires on **every single SSE message** for every query ╬ô├ç├╢ not just Deep Research queries. This produces massive log output and could expose partial user response content in logs. **Remove or gate behind a debug flag before merging.**
++
++- **Block-type logging left in production path** (line ~163): `console.log('[Perplexity block]', Object.keys(block))` has the same problem. Remove or flag.
++
++- **`STEP N:` comments throughout production code**: Comments like `// STEP 3:`, `// STEP 4:`, `// STEP 5:`, `// STEP 6:`, `// STEP 7:`, `// STEP 8:` are development-time breadcrumbs that have no place in a committed diff. They signal the code is still in an exploratory state. **Remove all of them.**
++
++- **No unit or integration tests added**: A quota-aware routing change of this magnitude warrants at minimum mock tests of `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` covering: success with quota available, `available: false`, `remaining === 0`, missing `agenticData`, network error, and timeout. None were added.
++
++***
++
++## Security & Performance
++
++- **`credentials: 'include'` on the rate-limit endpoint**: Presumably intentional (session-based auth), but confirm this endpoint does not return PII or quota details that should not be accessible from the renderer process. If running in the Electron renderer, session cookies are correctly in scope, but ensure the endpoint is only called when the user session is active.
++
++- **Duplicated URL in `x-perplexity-request-endpoint` header**: This custom header mirrors the fetch URL. If one is updated but not the other, a mismatched value is silently sent. Extract to a shared constant:
++  ```js
++  const RATE_LIMIT_URL = 'https://www.perplexity.ai/rest/rate-limit/status?version=2.18&source=default';
++  fetch(RATE_LIMIT_URL, { headers: { 'x-perplexity-request-endpoint': RATE_LIMIT_URL, ... } });
++  ```
++
++- **Additional latency on every Deep Research call**: Every `deepSearch: true` request now makes a blocking pre-flight HTTP round-trip before the actual query. Consider **caching the quota response** for a short TTL (e.g., 60 seconds) in memory so repeated requests don't each pay this cost. At typical API latency of 200╬ô├ç├┤500ms, this is directly user-visible.
++
++- **`console.log` of `quota.remaining` leaks quota state to renderer logs**: If logs are written to file or sent to a remote service, this may be undesirable. Low-priority but worth noting.
++
++- **5-second `setTimeout` in MCP tools** (`await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 5000))`): Pre-existing but worth flagging ╬ô├ç├╢ a hard-coded 5-second delay before every Perplexity call with no back-off, jitter, or queue is fragile. Consider a proper request serialization queue.
++
++***
++
++## Overall Score: 5.5/10
++
++The core idea is correct and well-structured, but the commit is not production-ready. It contains debug logging active in every SSE hot path, development-stage `STEP N` comments, inconsistent `var` usage and indentation regressions, a latency concern with no caching mitigation, and an edge case in the quota guard logic. The MCP changes are clean. With the issues above addressed, this would comfortably be a 7.5╬ô├ç├┤8/10.
++
++***
++
++## Recommendations
++
++Prioritized from most to least critical:
++
++1. **Remove or gate the raw SSE and block-type `console.log` calls immediately** ╬ô├ç├╢ they fire on every query for every user and will flood logs with potentially sensitive response data.
++2. **Add an `AbortController` timeout** (3s) to `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` to prevent `send()` from hanging on a slow rate-limit endpoint.
++3. **Cache the quota response with a 60-second in-memory TTL** to eliminate the blocking pre-flight round-trip on every Deep Research call.
++4. **Fix the `quota.remaining === 0` guard** to avoid blocking requests when `remaining_detail` is absent by distinguishing `0 remaining` from "count unknown."
++5. **Replace all `var` with `const`/`let`** and fix the indentation regressions on `var params` and `version: '2.18'`.
++6. **Remove all `STEP N:` comments** ╬ô├ç├╢ they are development scaffolding, not documentation.
++7. **Extract the rate-limit URL into a named constant** shared between the `fetch` call and the `x-perplexity-request-endpoint` header value.
++8. **Add tests for `_checkDeepResearchQuota()`** covering the five key scenarios: quota available, `available: false`, `remaining === 0`, missing `agenticData`, and network/timeout failure.

++> fix: route review IPC to correct provider (chatgpt vs perplexity)
++
++## Summary
++This commit refactors the Proxima review IPC logic to dynamically route requests to either **ChatGPT** or **Perplexity** based on the `REVIEW_MODEL` prefix. Previously, `queryPerplexity()` was hardcoded to send `provider: 'perplexity'` and always used `modelPreference`. Now, `queryAI(message, model, provider)` builds the payload dynamically: ChatGPT receives `{ message, model }` while Perplexity receives `{ message, modelPreference, deepSearch: false }`. The provider is auto-resolved via `resolveProvider()` and is included in the review frontmatter. This allows switching providers simply by changing `REVIEW_MODEL`. I verified the payload structures align with typical IPC conventions and Proxima Agent Hub expectations.
++

++| cli/proxima-review.cjs:L144 | ╬ô├£├íΓê⌐Γòò├à | Hardcoded timeout `600000` ms may be excessive for some queries. | Consider making timeout configurable via env variable or dynamic based on message size. |

++
++# Code Review: 8115bfe3
++
++> Support 'resolved' subdirectory in existing review check and optimize lock acquisition
++
++Now I have enough context to write a thorough, well-researched code review. Let me analyze the diff carefully.
++
++***
++
++## Summary
++
++This commit does two things: it **moves the "review already exists" early-exit check to before `acquireLockWithRetry`**, and it **extends that check to also look for the file inside a `resolved/` subdirectory**. The motivation is sensible ╬ô├ç├╢ avoid paying the cost of acquiring a distributed file lock only to immediately release it when the review was already generated. The logic is straightforward and the change is small, but the reordering introduces a subtle yet significant **TOCTOU (Time-of-Check-to-Time-of-Use) race condition** that wasn't present in the original design. No external schemas or APIs were verified via search for this review, but Node.js `fs.existsSync` TOCTOU behavior and file-locking best practices were confirmed against official recommendations.
++
++***
++
++## What's Good
++
++- **`resolved/` subdirectory support is correct in intent** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L246`). Checking both `REVIEW_DIR/<sha>.md` and `REVIEW_DIR/resolved/<sha>.md` before doing any work means already-resolved reviews are properly skipped, preventing redundant API calls. This is a meaningful feature addition.
++- **`mkdirSync` hoisted with the new early check** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L245`). Ensuring `REVIEW_DIR` exists before reading it is the right order of operations and avoids a potential crash if `REVIEW_DIR` doesn't yet exist at skip-check time.
++- **Removed now-redundant `releaseLock()` call** (old code at `L265`). The original code called `releaseLock()` before returning inside the post-lock exists check. Moving the check pre-lock correctly eliminates that call, reducing the chance of a stale lock release.
++- **Correct use of short-circuit `||`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L246-L247`). The two `existsSync` calls are chained logically and the assignment is clean and readable.
++- **Blank line left after lock acquisition** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L261`). The removed dead line (`const reviewFile = ...` was effectively orphaned) is cleaned up properly, keeping the post-lock block tidy.
++
++***
++
++## Issues & Suggestions
++
++**[CRITICAL] TOCTOU Race Condition ╬ô├ç├╢ Check Is Now Outside the Lock**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L244-L250`
++
++The entire purpose of `acquireLockWithRetry` is to serialize access so that only one process at a time writes a review file. By moving the existence check *before* the lock is acquired, two concurrent processes can both pass the `if (exists)` check simultaneously (the file doesn't exist yet for either), both acquire the lock in sequence, and both end up writing (and potentially overwriting or duplicating) the review. The original code performed the check *inside* the critical section ╬ô├ç├╢ which is the correct pattern. The performance win of skipping the lock is real, but it must be done safely:
++
++```js
++// SAFER pattern: fast pre-check (accept false negatives), then authoritative check inside lock
++const likelySeen = fs.existsSync(path.join(REVIEW_DIR, shortSha + '.md')) ||
++                   fs.existsSync(path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'resolved', shortSha + '.md'));
++if (likelySeen) {
++    log(yellow('╬ô├à┬í') + '  Review already exists for ' + shortSha + ', skipping');
++    return;
++}
++
++// Acquire lock, then do the authoritative check inside
++await acquireLockWithRetry(sha, log);
++
++// Authoritative check inside the critical section
++const reviewFile = path.join(REVIEW_DIR, shortSha + '.md');
++const resolvedFile = path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'resolved', shortSha + '.md');
++if (fs.existsSync(reviewFile) || fs.existsSync(resolvedFile)) {
++    log(yellow('╬ô├à┬í') + '  Review already exists for ' + shortSha + ', skipping (post-lock)');
++    releaseLock();
++    return;
++}
++```
++
++This is a double-checked locking pattern: the pre-lock check is a cheap optimization for the common case (review exists), while the post-lock check is the safety net for the rare concurrent-write case.
++
++***
++
++**[MAJOR] `releaseLock()` Is Never Called on the "already exists" Post-Lock Path**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L260` (context)
++
++The original code explicitly called `releaseLock()` before `return` when the file already existed inside the lock. That call was correctly deleted ╬ô├ç├╢ but *only* because the check was moved outside the lock. If you apply the fix above (double-checked pattern), the inner `releaseLock()` call must be restored for that early return path, or the lock will remain held indefinitely and all future processes for this SHA will hang.
++
++***
++
++**[MODERATE] `resolved/` directory existence is never ensured before the `existsSync` call**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L247`
++
++`fs.existsSync(path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'resolved', shortSha + '.md'))` will return `false` (correctly) if `resolved/` doesn't yet exist ╬ô├ç├╢ but this is implicitly relying on `existsSync` gracefully returning `false` for paths whose parent directories don't exist. That behavior is correct in Node.js today, but it is not documented as a guarantee and is subtly fragile. Consider a simple guard or restructuring to `fs.existsSync(path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'resolved'))` separately if you ever need to enumerate that directory.
++
++***
++
++**[MINOR] `mkdirSync` is now called unconditionally on every `runReview` invocation, even for commits that will be skipped**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L245`
++
++Previously, `mkdirSync` was called only after the lock was acquired (i.e., the process was committed to doing real work). Now it runs before the skip check, meaning every invocation ╬ô├ç├╢ including ones that immediately return due to an existing review ╬ô├ç├╢ pays a filesystem syscall. In high-frequency batch runs (e.g., reviewing hundreds of commits in a loop), this is unnecessary overhead. Move `mkdirSync` to after the early skip check, or lazy-init it only when you're about to write:
++
++```js
++// Move this to just before writing the review file, not before the skip check
++// fs.mkdirSync(REVIEW_DIR, { recursive: true }); // only needed right before write
++```
++
++Or at minimum restructure so the guard is: `if (!fs.existsSync(REVIEW_DIR))` is kept inside the `exists` block as the path-building requires it anyway.
++
++***
++
++**[MINOR] The `exists` variable name is too generic for its context**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L246`
++
++`exists` is ambiguous. In a larger function, a reader has to look up what it refers to. Prefer `reviewAlreadyExists` or `reviewFileExists` to make the intent immediately clear at the `if (exists)` call site on `L249`.
++
++***
++
++**[MINOR] No check that `resolved/` is a directory vs. a file**
++`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L247`
++
++If `REVIEW_DIR/resolved` were accidentally a file rather than a directory (e.g., from a bad migration), `path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'resolved', shortSha + '.md')` would return `false` from `existsSync` silently, causing previously-resolved reviews to be re-processed. A defensive `fs.statSync` or `fs.lstatSync` check to assert it is a directory could prevent silent data loss.
++
++***
++
++## Security & Performance
++
++**Security:**
++The TOCTOU race condition described above (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L244-L250`) is not just a correctness issue ╬ô├ç├╢ in a shared CI environment where multiple agents run `proxima-review` in parallel against the same `REVIEW_DIR` (e.g., a mounted network volume), this creates a window where two agents can both believe no review exists and both write, potentially overwriting a completed review with a new one or causing duplicate billing for an AI API call. The fix is the double-checked locking pattern described above.
++
++**Performance:**
++The *intent* of this optimization ╬ô├ç├╢ skipping lock acquisition for already-reviewed commits ╬ô├ç├╢ is sound and meaningful. Lock acquisition with retry is likely the most expensive operation in this path (involves I/O, possible sleeps). The pre-lock check for the common case (`review exists ╬ô├Ñ├å return immediately`) is a real win. However, the `mkdirSync` call at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L245` before the check adds a redundant syscall on every invocation. Fix ordering so `mkdirSync` is deferred to the write path only.
++
++***
++
++## Overall Score: 6.5/10
++
++The feature addition (supporting `resolved/`) is well-reasoned and the optimization intent (pre-lock skip) is sound, but the implementation introduces a TOCTOU race condition by removing the authoritative post-lock duplicate-check without replacing it with a safe double-checked locking pattern. This is a correctness bug in concurrent environments, which is the primary use case for a locking mechanism. The surrounding code quality is clean and readable.
++
++***
++
++## Recommendations
++
++1. **(Highest Priority) Restore the post-lock existence check** at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L260`. Apply the double-checked locking pattern ╬ô├ç├╢ keep the new pre-lock check as a fast-path optimization, but re-add the `existsSync` check (now also covering `resolved/`) inside the critical section, with a `releaseLock()` before returning.
++
++2. **(High Priority) Add `releaseLock()` back on the inner early-return path** wherever the post-lock check is restored. Missing this will cause lock starvation for all future processes attempting to review the same SHA.
++
++3. **(Medium Priority) Defer `mkdirSync`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L245`) to just before the file write operation, not before the skip check. This avoids a redundant syscall on every already-reviewed commit invocation.
++
++4. **(Low Priority) Rename `exists`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L246`) to `reviewAlreadyExists` for clarity at the call site.
++
++5. **(Low Priority) Optionally add a guard** verifying `REVIEW_DIR/resolved` is a directory (not a file) before building paths against it, to prevent silent misses on malformed directory state.

++
++# Code Review: 9c14c39c
++
++> Fix background process spawning to be completely silent on Windows
++
++This is the critical finding I needed. The official Node.js documentation and a well-documented open issue confirm that `windowsHide` is **explicitly ignored when `detached: true`** on Windows for console applications.  The issue comment states plainly: *"For the `windowsHide` option, please note that if `detached` is set to true or if the application is a console application, this option will be ignored."*
++
++## Summary
++
++This commit removes the Windows-specific `cmd.exe /c start /b` spawning path in `cli/proxima-review.cjs` and replaces it with a single unified `spawn(process.execPath, ...)` call that uses `windowsHide: true` on all platforms (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399╬ô├ç├┤L411`). The motivation is simplification: rather than maintaining two code paths, the author relies on `windowsHide: true` to suppress the console popup on Windows. I researched this directly against the official Node.js `child_process` documentation and a long-running open GitHub issue. The finding is critical: **the official Node.js documentation explicitly states that `windowsHide` is ignored when `detached: true` is set**, which is the case here.  This means the commit does not actually fix the problem it claims to fix ╬ô├ç├╢ the console window will still appear on Windows, and in fact the previous `cmd.exe /c start /b` path may have been the more correct approach for true silent Windows detachment.
++
++***
++
++## What's Good
++
++- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399╬ô├ç├┤L411` ╬ô├ç├╢ Code deduplication is the right instinct.** The old code had a platform branch that was largely identical in both arms ╬ô├ç├╢ both used `detached: true`, `stdio: ['ignore', out, out]`, `cwd: findGitRoot()`, and `env: cleanEnv`. Removing the branch eliminates ~10 lines of duplicated spawn configuration and reduces the test surface. The goal of unification was correct.
++- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399` ╬ô├ç├╢ Spawning `process.execPath` directly rather than `'node'` is correct cross-platform practice.** Using the resolved path to the current Node.js binary ensures the child runs the same version of Node as the parent, avoiding version mismatches in environments with multiple Node installations (e.g., `nvm`, `fnm`).
++- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L405` ╬ô├ç├╢ `cwd: findGitRoot()` is preserved.** Setting the child's working directory to the git root is necessary for relative path operations in the child process to resolve correctly.
++
++***
++
++## Issues & Suggestions
++
++**Γëí╞Æ├╢Γöñ Bug / Correctness ╬ô├ç├╢ This commit does not fix the problem it claims to fix.**
++
++- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L407` ╬ô├ç├╢ `windowsHide: true` is explicitly documented to be ignored when `detached: true` is also set.** The official Node.js docs state: *"Hide the subprocess console window that would normally be created on Windows systems"* ╬ô├ç├╢ but a tracked GitHub issue (open since Node v10) confirms with a direct quote from a maintainer: **"if `detached` is set to true or if the application is a console application, this option will be ignored."**  Since `detached: true` is always set at `L403`, `windowsHide: true` at `L407` has no effect on Windows. The console popup will still appear. The `windowsHide: true` option is a no-op in this configuration.
++
++- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399╬ô├ç├┤L411` ╬ô├ç├╢ The removed `cmd.exe /c start /b` path was the closer-to-correct approach for Windows.** Using `cmd.exe /c start /b` creates a new process that is detached from the console session, which is one of the few mechanisms that actually suppresses a console window on Windows for detached processes. The simplification removed the one behavior that had a chance of working on Windows.
++
++**Γëí╞Æ╞Æ├¡ Smell / Maintainability**
++
++- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L393` ╬ô├ç├╢ `findGitRoot()` is called inside `spawnBackground` on `L405`, which itself calls `execSync` internally.** This was flagged in the earlier review of the lock/queue commit as a concern in the polling hot path. While `spawnBackground` is not a tight loop (it runs once per commit), calling `execSync` synchronously here still blocks the event loop for the duration of the git command before the child is spawned. If `findGitRoot()` is already computed and available at the call site, passing it as a parameter would be cleaner. This is a pre-existing issue carried through, not introduced by this commit.
++
++- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L396` ╬ô├ç├╢ The comment "Ensure logs are captured" replaces the more informative prior comment "Prepare custom stdout/stderr append to log file inside background.log".** The new comment is too vague. "Ensure logs are captured" does not tell a maintainer what `out` is, why it's opened with `'a'`, or where the log file lives. The original comment was more helpful.
++
++***
++
++## Security & Performance
++
++- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399╬ô├ç├┤L411` ╬ô├ç├╢ No new security surface is introduced.** The child is spawned with a clean `env` (GIT_* variables stripped), stdio redirected to a log file, and `process.execPath` as the binary ╬ô├ç├╢ all of which are the same as before. Removing the `cmd.exe` shim actually reduces the attack surface slightly, since there is no intermediate shell involved.
++- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399` ╬ô├ç├╢ `windowsHide: true` being a no-op (per the bug above) does not introduce a new security risk**, but the console window popup could expose the review process and its arguments to a casual observer on a shared workstation.
++- **No performance implications.** This is a one-time spawn call. The simplification reduces spawning overhead by eliminating the `cmd.exe` intermediary on Windows, though the effect is negligible.
++
++***
++
++## Overall Score: 4/10
++
++The code reduction is clean and the intent is correct, but the commit's stated goal ╬ô├ç├╢ "completely silent on Windows" ╬ô├ç├╢ is not achieved. The `windowsHide: true` option is explicitly documented to be ignored when `detached: true` is set, which is always the case here.  The previous `cmd.exe /c start /b` path was at least attempting the right Windows-specific mechanism. This commit trades a working-but-messy Windows branch for a simpler-but-broken unified approach. The comment in the diff ("is the most robust way to run silently on all platforms") is factually incorrect per the official documentation.
++
++***
++
++## Recommendations
++
++1. **(Critical ╬ô├ç├╢ `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399╬ô├ç├┤L411`)** Restore Windows-specific handling. The documented way to spawn a truly silent background process on Windows without a console popup is one of: (a) use `windows-silent-spawn` or a `.vbs` shim that calls `CreateProcess` with `DETACHED_PROCESS | CREATE_NO_WINDOW` flags; (b) use `cmd.exe /c start /b` as the prior code did; or (c) spawn a minimal wrapper `.js` that itself spawns the real child without `detached: true` but still unrefs it. The simplest pragmatic fix is to revert to the platform branch and use `cmd.exe /c start "" /b node ...` on Windows and the direct `process.execPath` path on POSIX. 
++2. **(Medium ╬ô├ç├╢ `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L407`)** Remove or document `windowsHide: true` as a best-effort flag. It is not a no-op on POSIX (it has no effect there either, but it's harmless), and it does work on Windows when `detached` is `false`. Add a comment that it is included for completeness but is overridden by `detached: true` on Windows, so future maintainers do not mistake it for a working suppression mechanism.
++3. **(Low ╬ô├ç├╢ `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L396`)** Restore or improve the comment explaining the log file setup ╬ô├ç├╢ what `out` is, that it appends to `background.log`, and why ╬ô├ç├╢ rather than replacing it with the vaguer "Ensure logs are captured".

++# Code Review: eb8c99f9

++> Release v4.2.0: Universal Review Queue, Global Locking, and Logging Improvements
++
++## Summary
++
++This commit is a version bump (`package.json:L3`) from `4.1.0` to `4.2.0` and a regeneration/update of a stored code review document (`perplexity-reviews/75a8fb6e.md`). The actual feature work (global lock, queue logic) was already landed in a prior commit. The only substantive change here is that the review file for commit `75a8fb6e` was re-reviewed and its content was revised ╬ô├ç├╢ the score went from 6/10 to 9/10, the structure was reformatted, and several previously raised concerns were downgraded or dropped. No research was needed; there is no application logic in this diff whatsoever.
++
++***
++
++## What's Good
++
++- **`package.json:L3` ╬ô├ç├╢ Version bump is semver-correct.** `4.1.0` ╬ô├Ñ├å `4.2.0` is a minor version increment, which is exactly right for new backward-compatible functionality (queue/lock behavior). Using a patch bump for a behavioral change of this scope would have been wrong.
++- **`perplexity-reviews/75a8fb6e.md:L8` ╬ô├ç├╢ `reviewed_at` timestamp is updated.** Keeping the `reviewed_at` field current means the review file accurately reflects when it was last revised, preserving audit trail integrity.
++- **`perplexity-reviews/75a8fb6e.md:L68╬ô├ç├┤L71` ╬ô├ç├╢ The retained "future" recommendations are still tracked.** The forward-looking items about deriving duration from a constant and adding a background completion signal are preserved as numbered items, so they aren't lost even though they were deprioritized.
++
++***
++
++## Issues & Suggestions
++
++- **`perplexity-reviews/75a8fb6e.md` ╬ô├ç├╢ The file is missing a trailing newline at `L65` (final line).** The diff shows `\ No newline at end of file` on the last line. This is a persistent issue that was also present in the original version of this file and has not been fixed. Every text file in a repository should end with a newline ╬ô├ç├╢ it prevents noisy diffs, satisfies POSIX compliance, and avoids `git diff` warnings. Fix: add a single `\n` at the end of the file.
++
++- **`perplexity-reviews/75a8fb6e.md:L7╬ô├ç├┤L14` ╬ô├ç├╢ The file now has a duplicated, structurally inconsistent header block.** The frontmatter block ends at `---`, then immediately a prose paragraph begins (`This is a single-line diff...`), then a second `# Code Review: \`75a8fb6e\`` heading appears at `L16`. The original had a `## Summary` heading gating the prose; the revised version dropped the heading but kept the paragraph, creating an orphaned block of text before the first `#` heading. Any Markdown renderer or parser that builds a TOC will either omit this paragraph or misattribute it. The fix is to either reinstate `## Summary` before `L14` or move the prose inside the `## Summary` section that follows at `L18`.
++
++- **`perplexity-reviews/75a8fb6e.md:L3` ╬ô├ç├╢ `reviewed_at` is the only changed frontmatter field, but the `model` field still reads `claude sonnet 4.6 thinking`.** If this re-review was generated by a different model or session, the `model` field is now stale. If it was the same model, no issue ╬ô├ç├╢ but this should be verified before committing to avoid misleading attribution in the review archive.
++
++- **`perplexity-reviews/75a8fb6e.md` ╬ô├ç├╢ Score inflation without new evidence is a documentation quality concern.** The score changed from `6/10` to `9/10` between the two versions with the same commit under review. The original `6/10` explicitly called out three actionable issues; the revised `9/10` re-categorizes those same issues as minor or future. This is not a code bug, but if this review archive is used as a quality baseline or audit trail, a score revision of this magnitude warrants a brief inline note (e.g., `> *Revised from 6/10 on initial review; downgraded concerns after re-evaluation.*`) so the history is transparent to future readers.
++
++***
++
++## Security & Performance
++
++- **No security implications.** The diff touches only a version string and a Markdown documentation file. Neither contains executable code, secrets, or user-facing inputs. `package.json:L3` changes only the declared version and has no runtime effect in this context.
++- **No performance implications.** The `perplexity-reviews/` file is static documentation; it is never loaded at runtime.
++
++***
++
++## Overall Score: 6/10
++
++The version bump itself is correct and well-chosen. The score is dragged down by the review document quality: the missing trailing newline is a recurring unfixed issue, the structural duplication in the Markdown header creates rendering ambiguity, and the large score revision without a documented rationale undermines the integrity of the review archive. None of these are blockers for shipping, but they reflect polish gaps in what is essentially a housekeeping commit.
++
++***
++
++## Recommendations
++
++1. **(Low ╬ô├ç├╢ `perplexity-reviews/75a8fb6e.md:L65`)** Add a trailing newline at end of file. This is a one-character fix; consider adding a `.editorconfig` or `git` `autocrlf`/`eol` setting to enforce this automatically across the repo.
++2. **(Low ╬ô├ç├╢ `perplexity-reviews/75a8fb6e.md:L14╬ô├ç├┤L16`)** Remove the orphaned prose paragraph that sits between the frontmatter `---` and the `# Code Review` heading, or reinstate the `## Summary` heading before it so the document structure is valid and renderers produce a correct TOC.
++3. **(Optional ╬ô├ç├╢ `perplexity-reviews/75a8fb6e.md`)** Add a one-line inline annotation near the `## Overall Score` section noting that this was a revised review and what changed, so the archive remains trustworthy for future reference.

++function findGitRoot() {
++    try {
++        return execSync('git rev-parse --show-toplevel', { encoding: 'utf8' }).trim();
++    } catch {
++        return process.cwd();
++    }
++}

++    reviewDir: "review",
++    baseUrl: "http://localhost:3210",

++    maxIterations: 3,

++function loadConfig() {
++    const gitRoot = findGitRoot();
++    const configPath = path.join(gitRoot, 'proxima-automation.config.json');
++    let userConfig = {};
++
++    if (fs.existsSync(configPath)) {
++        try {
++            userConfig = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(configPath, 'utf8'));
++        } catch (e) {
++            console.error(`Error parsing config file: ${e.message}`);
++        }
++    }
++
++    // Merge env vars
++    const envConfig = {
++        baseUrl: process.env.PROXIMA_BASE_URL,
++        reviewModel: process.env.PROXIMA_REVIEW_MODEL,
++        repairModel: process.env.PROXIMA_REPAIR_MODEL,
++        reviewDir: process.env.PROXIMA_REVIEW_DIR,
++    };
++
++    // Remove undefined values from envConfig
++    Object.keys(envConfig).forEach(key => envConfig[key] === undefined && delete envConfig[key]);
++
++    return {
++        ...DEFAULT_CONFIG,
++        ...userConfig,
++        ...envConfig,
++        gitRoot
++    };
++}
++
++module.exports = { loadConfig, findGitRoot };

++const { spawnSync } = require('child_process');
++const fs = require('fs');
++const path = require('path');
++
++function runCommand(cmd, args = [], options = {}) {
++    const res = spawnSync(cmd, args, { 
++        encoding: 'utf8', 
++        ...options 
++    });
++    
++    if (res.error) {
++        return { success: false, stdout: '', stderr: res.error.message };
++    }
++    
++    return { 
++        success: res.status === 0, 
++        stdout: res.stdout ? res.stdout.trim() : '', 
++        stderr: res.stderr ? res.stderr.trim() : '' 
++    };
++}
++
++function getHeadSha() {
++    return runCommand('git', ['rev-parse', 'HEAD']).stdout;
++}
++
++function getShortSha(sha) {
++    return runCommand('git', ['rev-parse', '--short', sha || 'HEAD']).stdout;
++}
++
++function getCurrentBranch() {
++    return runCommand('git', ['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD']).stdout;
++}
++
++function getCommitMessage(sha) {
++    return runCommand('git', ['log', '-1', '--pretty=%B', sha || 'HEAD']).stdout;
++}
++
++function ensureInsideGitRepo() {
++    const res = runCommand('git', ['rev-parse', '--is-inside-work-tree']);
++    if (!res.success) throw new Error('Not inside a git repository');
++}
++
++function ensureCleanWorkingTree() {
++    const res = runCommand('git', ['status', '--porcelain']);
++    return res.success && res.stdout === '';
++}
++
++function createBranch(branchName) {
++    return runCommand('git', ['checkout', '-b', branchName]);
++}
++
++function checkoutBranch(branchName) {
++    return runCommand('git', ['checkout', branchName]);
++}
++
++function isProtectedBranch(branch, protectedBranches) {
++    return protectedBranches.some(pattern => {
++        const regex = new RegExp('^' + pattern.replace(/\*/g, '.*') + '$');
++        return regex.test(branch);
++    });
++}
++
++function isBotBranch(branch, prefix) {
++    return branch.startsWith(prefix);
++}
++
++function isBotCommit(message, markers) {
++    return markers.some(marker => message.includes(marker));
++}
++
++function applyPatchCheck(patchPath) {
++    return runCommand('git', ['apply', '--check', patchPath]);
++}
++
++function applyPatch(patchPath) {
++    return runCommand('git', ['apply', patchPath]);
++}
++
++function commitAll(message) {
++    const addRes = runCommand('git', ['add', '.']);
++    if (!addRes.success) return addRes;
++    return runCommand('git', ['commit', '-m', message]);
++}
++
++function pushBranch(branchName) {
++    return runCommand('git', ['push', 'origin', branchName]);
++}
++
++function createPullRequest({ title, body, labels, draft, bodyFile }) {
++    const args = ['pr', 'create', '--title', title];
++    
++    if (bodyFile) {
++        args.push('--body-file', bodyFile);
++    } else {
++        args.push('--body', body);
++    }
++    
++    if (draft) args.push('--draft');
++    if (labels && labels.length > 0) {
++        args.push('--label', labels.join(','));
++    }
++    
++    return runCommand('gh', args);
++}
++

++const http = require('http');
++const url = require('url');
++
++async function askProxima(message, model, baseUrl = 'http://localhost:3210') {
++    const targetUrl = new url.URL('/v1/chat/completions', baseUrl);
++    
++    // Using the shape suggested by user: { model, message, stream: false }
++    const payload = JSON.stringify({
++        model: model,
++        message: message,
++        stream: false
++    });
++
++    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
++        const req = http.request(targetUrl, {
++            method: 'POST',
++            headers: {
++                'Content-Type': 'application/json',
++                'Content-Length': Buffer.byteLength(payload)
++            },
++            timeout: 300000 // 5 minutes
++        }, (res) => {
++            let data = '';
++            res.on('data', (chunk) => data += chunk);
++            res.on('end', () => {
++                if (res.statusCode >= 200 && res.statusCode < 300) {
++                    try {
++                        const json = JSON.parse(data);
++                        // Support both OpenAI format and potential custom format
++                        if (json.choices && json.choices[0] && json.choices[0].message) {
++                            resolve(json.choices[0].message.content);
++                        } else if (json.response) {
++                            resolve(json.response);
++                        } else {
++                            reject(new Error('Unexpected response format from Proxima'));
++                        }
++                    } catch (e) {
++                        reject(new Error(`Failed to parse Proxima response: ${e.message}`));
++                    }
++                } else {
++                    reject(new Error(`Proxima returned status ${res.statusCode}: ${data}`));
++                }
++            });
++        });
++
++        req.on('error', (e) => {
++            if (e.code === 'ECONNREFUSED') {
++                reject(new Error(`Proxima is unavailable at ${baseUrl}. Is it running?`));
++            } else {
++                reject(e);
++            }
++        });
++
++        req.on('timeout', () => {
++            req.destroy();
++            reject(new Error('Request to Proxima timed out'));
++        });
++
++        req.write(payload);
++        req.end();
++    });
++}
++
++module.exports = { askProxima };

++function parseSeverityCounts(markdown) {
++    const counts = { critical: 0, high: 0, medium: 0, low: 0 };
++    
++    // Target the "Bugs & Failure Modes" table specifically
++    const tableMatch = markdown.match(/## Bugs & Failure Modes\s*?\n([\s\S]*?)(?:\n##|$)/);
++    if (!tableMatch) {
++        return counts;
++    }
++
++    const table = tableMatch[1];
++    const lines = table.split('\n');
++    
++    for (const line of lines) {
++        if (!line.includes('|')) continue;
++        
++        // Skip header and separator lines
++        if (line.includes('---') || line.toLowerCase().includes('severity')) continue;
++        
++        // Split by pipe and find the severity column
++        const columns = line.split('|').map(c => c.trim()).filter(Boolean);
++        if (columns.length < 2) continue;
++        
++        // Severity is usually the second column in the provided structure
++        // | File:Line | Severity | Finding | Evidence |
++        const severity = columns[1].toLowerCase();
++        
++        if (severity.includes('critical')) counts.critical++;
++        else if (severity.includes('high')) counts.high++;
++        else if (severity.includes('medium')) counts.medium++;
++        else if (severity.includes('low')) counts.low++;
++    }
++
++    return counts;
++}
++
++function parseScore(markdown) {
++    const match = markdown.match(/## Score: (\d+)\/10/);
++    return match ? parseInt(match[1]) : null;
++}
++
++function hasCriticalOrHigh(markdown) {
++    const counts = parseSeverityCounts(markdown);
++    return counts.critical > 0 || counts.high > 0;
++}
++
++module.exports = {
++    parseSeverityCounts,
++    parseScore,
++    hasCriticalOrHigh
++};
