++const path = require('path');
++
++function validatePatchText(patchText) {
++    // Reject markdown fences
++    if (patchText.includes('```')) {
++        throw new Error('Patch contains markdown fences');
++    }
++    
++    // Check if it looks like a unified diff
++    if (!patchText.includes('--- ') || !patchText.includes('+++ ') || !patchText.includes('@@ ')) {
++        throw new Error('Response does not appear to be a valid unified diff');
++    }
++    
++    // Reject prose before/after diff (simple check)
++    const lines = patchText.trim().split('\n');
++    if (!lines[0].startsWith('--- ') && !lines[0].startsWith('Index: ') && !lines[0].startsWith('diff ')) {
++        throw new Error('Patch contains prose before the diff');
++    }
++
++    return true;
++}
++
++function rejectDangerousPaths(patchText, gitRoot) {
++    const lines = patchText.split('\n');
++    const blockedPatterns = [
++        /\.env.*/i,
++        /.*\.pem$/i,
++        /.*\.key$/i,
++        /id_rsa/i,
++        /\.npmrc$/i,
++        /\.pypirc$/i
++    ];
++
++    for (const line of lines) {
++        if (line.startsWith('--- ') || line.startsWith('+++ ')) {
++            const filePath = line.substring(4).split('\t')[0].trim();
++            if (filePath === '/dev/null') continue;
++            
++            // Remove a/ or b/ prefix
++            const cleanPath = filePath.replace(/^[ab]\//, '');
++            const absolutePath = path.resolve(gitRoot, cleanPath);
++            const relativePath = path.relative(gitRoot, absolutePath);
++            
++            // Check if it's outside the git root
++            if (relativePath.startsWith('..') || path.isAbsolute(relativePath)) {
++                throw new Error(`Patch modifies file outside repository: ${cleanPath}`);
++            }
++            
++            const base = path.basename(cleanPath);
++            
++            // Block sensitive files
++            for (const pattern of blockedPatterns) {
++                if (pattern.test(base)) {
++                    throw new Error(`Patch modifies blocked sensitive file: ${cleanPath}`);
++                }
++            }
++
++            if (cleanPath.startsWith('.git/')) {
++                throw new Error(`Patch modifies internal git directory: ${cleanPath}`);
++            }
++            
++            if (cleanPath.startsWith('.github/workflows/')) {
++                throw new Error(`Patch modifies GitHub workflow: ${cleanPath}`);
++            }
++        }
++    }
++    return true;
++}
++
++function rejectScriptExecution(content) {
++    const dangerousPatterns = [
++        /#!/,
++        /powershell/i,
++        /pwsh/i,
++        /cmd\.exe/i,
++        /bash/i,
++        /sh /i,
++        /\.ps1/i,
++        /\.sh/i,
++        /\.bat/i
++    ];
++    
++    for (const pattern of dangerousPatterns) {
++        if (pattern.test(content)) {
++            throw new Error(`Response contains forbidden script pattern: ${pattern}`);
++        }
++    }
++}

++async function main() {
++    const config = loadConfig();
++    const args = process.argv.slice(2);
++    
++    // pushRequested is true only if --push CLI flag is present. 
++    // We ignore config.pushBotBranches here to satisfy requirement #1.
++    const pushRequested = args.includes('--push');
++    const prRequested = args.includes('--create-pr') || config.createPullRequest;
++    const allowDirty = args.includes('--allow-dirty');
++
++    git.ensureInsideGitRepo();
++    
++    let currentSha = git.getHeadSha();
++    const originalHeadSha = currentSha;
++    const shortSha = git.getShortSha(currentSha);
++    const sourceBranch = git.getCurrentBranch();
++    const commitMsg = git.getCommitMessage(currentSha);
++
++    if (git.isProtectedBranch(sourceBranch, config.protectedBranches)) {
++        console.error(`╬ô┬Ñ├« Refusing to run on protected branch: ${sourceBranch}`);
++        process.exit(1);
++    }
++
++    if (!allowDirty && !git.ensureCleanWorkingTree()) {
++        console.error('╬ô┬Ñ├« Working tree is dirty. Commit your changes or use --allow-dirty.');
++        process.exit(1);
++    }
++
++    if (git.isBotCommit(commitMsg, config.skipReviewCommitMarkers)) {
++        console.log('╬ô├à┬í Skipping bot commit.');
++        process.exit(0);
++    }
++
++    if (git.isBotBranch(sourceBranch, config.repairBranchPrefix)) {
++        console.log('╬ô├à┬í Skipping bot branch.');
++        process.exit(0);
++    }
++
++    const sessionDir = path.join(config.gitRoot, config.reviewDir, shortSha);
++    if (!fs.existsSync(sessionDir)) fs.mkdirSync(sessionDir, { recursive: true });
++
++    let status = {
++        commit: originalHeadSha,
++        shortSha: shortSha,
++        sourceBranch: sourceBranch,
++        status: "starting",
++        testsPassed: false,
++        patchApplied: false,
++        branchPushed: false,
++        prCreated: false,
++        autoMerged: false,
++        reviewSkippedForBotBranch: true
++    };
++
++    const updateStatus = (updates) => {
++        status = { ...status, ...updates };
++        fs.writeFileSync(path.join(sessionDir, 'status.json'), JSON.stringify(status, null, 2), 'utf8');
++    };
++
++    updateStatus({});
++
++    let iteration = 1;
++    const maxIterations = config.maxIterations || 3;
++    let botBranchCreated = false;
++    let botBranchName = '';
++
++    while (iteration <= maxIterations) {
++        console.log(`\nΓëí╞Æ├╢├ñ Iteration ${iteration}/${maxIterations}...`);
++        
++        // 1. Run Review
++        console.log(`Γëí╞Æ├▒├╗ Running Proxima review for ${git.getShortSha(currentSha)}...`);
++        // Use sessionDir as outputDir. canonical file will be review.md
++        await runReview(currentSha, { outputDir: sessionDir });
++        
++        const reviewFile = path.join(sessionDir, 'review.md');
++        if (!fs.existsSync(reviewFile)) {
++            console.error('╬ô┬Ñ├« Review file was not generated.');
++            updateStatus({ status: "proxima-unavailable" });
++            process.exit(1);
++        }
++
++        const reviewContent = fs.readFileSync(reviewFile, 'utf8');
++        const counts = parser.parseSeverityCounts(reviewContent);
++        const score = parser.parseScore(reviewContent);
++
++        console.log(`Γëí╞Æ├┤├¿ Score: ${score}/10 | Critical: ${counts.critical} | High: ${counts.high}`);
++
++        updateStatus({
++            score,
++            critical: counts.critical,
++            high: counts.high,
++            medium: counts.medium,
++            low: counts.low
++        });
++
++        // 2. Run Tests
++        console.log('Γëí╞Æ┬║┬¼ Running tests...');
++        let testsPassed = true;
++        const testLogPath = path.join(sessionDir, iteration === 1 ? 'initial_tests.log' : `repair_iter_${iteration}_tests.log`);
++        
++        for (const cmd of config.testCommands) {
++            // Split command by spaces for argument array, though it's safer to run as shell if it's a complex npm command
++            // git.runCommand handles the args. For simplicity, we'll assume cmd is like 'npm test'
++            const [baseCmd, ...cmdArgs] = cmd.split(' ');
++            const res = git.runCommand(baseCmd, cmdArgs);
++            fs.appendFileSync(testLogPath, res.stdout + res.stderr, 'utf8');
++            if (!res.success) {
++                testsPassed = false;
++                if (config.stopOnTestFailure) break;
++            }
++        }
++        updateStatus({ testsPassed });
++
++        // 3. Check stop condition
++        if (testsPassed && counts.critical === 0 && counts.high === 0) {
++            console.log('╬ô┬ú├á Clean state reached (Tests pass, no Critical/High findings).');
++            updateStatus({ status: "clean" });
++            
++            if (botBranchCreated && pushRequested) {
++                console.log(`Γëí╞Æ├£├ç Pushing branch ${botBranchName}...`);
++                const pushRes = git.pushBranch(botBranchName);
++                if (pushRes.success) {
++                    updateStatus({ branchPushed: true });
++                } else {
++                    console.error(`╬ô├£├íΓê⌐Γòò├à Push failed: ${pushRes.stderr}`);
++                }
++            }
++
++            if (botBranchCreated && prRequested) {
++                createPR(botBranchName, shortSha, originalHeadSha, sourceBranch, score, counts, sessionDir, config);
++            }
++
++            process.exit(0);
++        }
++
++        // 4. Generate Repair
++        console.log(`Γëí╞Æ┬ó├í Attempting repair (Iteration ${iteration})...`);
++        const repairDir = path.join(sessionDir, 'repair', `iter-${iteration}`);
++        if (!fs.existsSync(repairDir)) fs.mkdirSync(repairDir, { recursive: true });
++
++        const prompt = `You are an expert software engineer. Based on the following code review and the original diff, generate a unified diff patch to fix the Critical and High findings.
++
++RULES:
++1. Output ONLY a valid git-compatible unified diff.
++2. NO markdown fences (e.g., \`\`\`diff).
++3. NO prose, explanations, or introductory text.
++4. NO shell scripts, PowerShell, or Bash commands.
++5. Fix ONLY the issues identified in the review.
++6. Ensure the patch is compatible with 'git apply'.
++

++
++--- ORIGINAL DIFF ---

++
++PATCH:`;

++

++            const patch = await askProxima(prompt, config.repairModel, config.baseUrl);
++            validatePatchText(patch);
++            rejectDangerousPaths(patch, config.gitRoot);
++            rejectScriptExecution(patch);
++
++            const patchPath = path.join(repairDir, 'fix.patch');
++            fs.writeFileSync(patchPath, patch, 'utf8');
++
++            // 5. Apply Repair
++            if (!botBranchCreated) {
++                botBranchName = `${config.repairBranchPrefix}-${shortSha}-iter-${iteration}`;
++                console.log(`Γëí╞Æ├«ΓöÉ Creating branch ${botBranchName}...`);
++                const branchRes = git.createBranch(botBranchName);
++                if (!branchRes.success) {
++                    console.error(`╬ô┬Ñ├« Failed to create branch: ${branchRes.stderr}`);
++                    updateStatus({ status: "branch-creation-failed" });
++                    process.exit(1);
++                }
++                botBranchCreated = true;
++                updateStatus({ botBranch: botBranchName });
++            }
++
++            console.log('Γëí╞ÆΓîÉΓòú Applying patch...');
++            const applyRes = git.applyPatch(patchPath);
++            fs.writeFileSync(path.join(repairDir, 'apply.log'), applyRes.stdout + applyRes.stderr, 'utf8');

++            if (!applyRes.success) {
++                console.error(`╬ô┬Ñ├« Patch application failed: ${applyRes.stderr}`);
++                updateStatus({ status: "patch-failed" });
++                break;
++            }
++
++            console.log('Γëí╞Æ├┤┬Ñ Committing fix...');
++            const commitRes = git.commitAll(`fix: address Proxima review ${shortSha} [proxima-auto-fix]`);
++            if (!commitRes.success) {
++                console.error(`╬ô┬Ñ├« Commit failed: ${commitRes.stderr}`);
++                updateStatus({ status: "commit-failed" });
++                break;
++            }
++
++            currentSha = git.getHeadSha();
++            updateStatus({ patchApplied: true });
++
++        } catch (e) {
++            console.error(`╬ô┬Ñ├« Repair failed: ${e.message}`);
++            updateStatus({ status: "needs-human-review" });
++            break;

++    }
++
++    if (iteration > maxIterations) {
++        console.log('╬ô├àΓòú Max iterations reached.');
++        updateStatus({ status: "max-iterations-reached" });

+# Proxima Code Review Pipeline
+
+## Overview
+
+The Proxima review pipeline automatically reviews git commits using an LLM (ChatGPT or Perplexity). It can be triggered manually or automatically via a pre-push git hook.
+
+## Triggering Reviews
+
+### Manual Run
+```bash
+node C:\Users\subha\Documents\PROJECTS\Proxima\cli\proxima-review.cjs HEAD
+node C:\Users\subha\Documents\PROJECTS\Proxima\cli\proxima-review.cjs <commit-sha>
+```
+
+### Automatic (Pre-push Hook)
+The script reads commit SHAs from stdin when triggered by git's pre-push hook. It spawns background processes to review each new commit without blocking the push.
+
+## Pipeline Flow
+
+```
+1. Get commit info (sha, shortSha, msg, author, date)
+2. Skip maintenance/chore commits (save quota)
+3. Check if review already exists (root or resolved folder)
+4. Acquire lock (prevent concurrent reviews)
+5. Get git diff (limited to 2MB)
+6. Send diff + adversarial prompt to LLM via IPC
+7. Save review to perplexity-reviews/<shortSha>.md
+8. Release lock
+```
+
+## IPC Communication
+
+- **Protocol**: TCP socket on port 19222 (direct IPC to Proxima Agent Hub)
+- **ChatGPT**: `sendMessage` with `{ message, model }`
+- **Perplexity**: `sendMessage` with `{ message, modelPreference, deepSearch: false }`
+
+## Review Model Configuration
+
+Set via environment variable `PROXIMA_REVIEW_MODEL`:
+- `gpt-*` ΓåÆ routes to ChatGPT
+- `claude *` ΓåÆ routes to Perplexity (via Claude)
+- anything else ΓåÆ routes to Perplexity
+
+Default: `gpt-5-5-thinking`
+
+## The Adversarial Prompt
+
+The LLM is instructed to act as a **hostile, adversarial code auditor** with these rules:
+
+1. **Never trust the commit message** ΓÇö treat it as a hypothesis to be tested
+2. **Do NOT mirror the commit message** ΓÇö summarize what the code actually does
+3. **Assume the author is wrong** until code proves otherwise
+4. **Generic advice is forbidden** ΓÇö every point must cite specific lines
+5. **Use web search** to verify API contracts and security advisories
+
+### Required Sections in Review:
+- **Verdict**: PASS / FAIL / NEEDS WORK
+- **Implementation vs Intent Gap**: Divergences between stated intent and actual code
+- **Bugs & Failure Modes**: Table with file:line, severity, finding, evidence
+- **Missing Changes**: Related code that should have been updated
+- **Security & Data Integrity**: Worst-case impact if code is wrong
+- **Score**: X/10 with justification
+
+## Output
+
+Reviews are saved to:
+- `<repo-root>/perplexity-reviews/<shortSha>.md` ΓÇö new reviews
+- `<repo-root>/perplexity-reviews/resolved/<shortSha>.md` ΓÇö resolved/archived reviews
+- `<repo-root>/perplexity-reviews/error-<shortSha>.md` ΓÇö failed reviews
+- `<repo-root>/perplexity-reviews/background.log` ΓÇö background process logs
+
+## Lock Mechanism
+
+- Universal lock file in `~/.proxima-review.lock`
+- Prevents cross-project Hub overloading
+- 15-minute stale timeout
+- Retries for up to 60 minutes if lock is held
\ No newline at end of file

+# Code Review: 1f597599
+
+> fix: remove 800-line diff truncation, keep only 2MB limit
+
+## Verdict
+**PASS** ΓÇö The commit effectively removes the hard 800-line truncation and relies solely on the 2ΓÇ»MB `maxBuffer` limit, which aligns with the stated intent.
+
+## Implementation vs Intent Gap
+- The commit deletes the lines that imposed `MAX_DIFF_LINES = 800` and the slicing/truncation logic in `getDiff` (lines 45 and 224ΓÇô228).  
+- The remaining code continues to fetch the diff with `maxBuffer: 2 * 1024 * 1024` for NodeΓÇÖs `execFileSync`, ensuring large diffs up to 2ΓÇ»MB are handled.  
+- **No gap found** ΓÇö the code matches the commit description.
+
+## Bugs & Failure Modes
+| File:Line | Severity | Finding | Evidence |
+|-----------|----------|---------|----------|
+| `cli/proxima-review.cjs:224-228` | ΓÜá∩╕Å Moderate | Large diffs might still fail silently if >2ΓÇ»MB | NodeΓÇÖs `execFileSync` is limited by `maxBuffer`; extremely large diffs beyond 2ΓÇ»MB will throw an exception, but the 800-line truncation is gone. |
+| `cli/proxima-review.cjs:224` | ΓÜá∩╕Å Low | No feedback on truncation removed | Users previously got a clear ΓÇ£ΓÇª(truncated)ΓÇ¥ message; now large diffs exceeding 2ΓÇ»MB will throw, potentially without user-friendly notice. |
+
+## Missing Changes
+- Optional: Add a user-friendly message if the diff exceeds 2ΓÇ»MB instead of relying solely on NodeΓÇÖs buffer exception.  
+- Optional: Logging/tracking for exceptionally large diffs to avoid silent failures.
+
+## Security & Data Integrity
+- No direct security or data-integrity impact. The worst-case effect is that diffs larger than 2ΓÇ»MB fail to be retrieved, which could cause review gaps.
+
+## Score: 9/10
+- Functionally correct: truncation is removed and 2ΓÇ»MB limit enforced (lines 45, 224ΓÇô228).  
+- Minor deduction because users may lose visibility into very large diffs without a friendly truncation message.

+
+# Code Review: 11dc5df6
+
+> Implement global lock and queueing (wait in line) logic
+
+The wrong skill loaded ΓÇö let me proceed directly with the code review using my analysis of the diff.
+
+## Summary
+
+This commit moves the global lock file from within the project's `REVIEW_DIR` to `~/.proxima-review.lock` in the home directory (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L26`), making it a machine-wide cross-project lock. It also replaces the old "skip-immediately-if-locked" behavior with a new `acquireLockWithRetry()` async polling loop (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L67ΓÇôL90`) that waits up to 60 minutes, polling every 10 seconds, before giving up. The stale-lock warning log line was removed silently. I did not need to look up external API docs for this change, as it is entirely self-contained Node.js `fs`, `os`, and async timer usage.
+
+***
+
+## What's Good
+
+- **Atomic lock write is preserved** ΓÇö `{ flag: 'wx' }` on `fs.writeFileSync` (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L58`) is the correct pattern to prevent TOCTOU races on the creation side. This was good before and remains intact.
+- **Silent unlink on `releaseLock`** ΓÇö `try { fs.unlinkSync(LOCK_FILE); } catch {}` (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L93`) is a clean, non-throwing release that avoids crashes if the file was already cleaned up.
+- **Cross-project isolation is the right design call** ΓÇö moving the lock to `os.homedir()` (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L26`) is the correct architectural decision for preventing multiple repo hooks from hammering the same AI hub simultaneously. The code comment explains *why* clearly.
+- **Repo identity added to lock metadata** ΓÇö writing `repo: findGitRoot()` into the lock file (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L60`) is a thoughtful observability improvement; it lets you inspect which project is holding the lock.
+- **Graceful catch on stale unlink** ΓÇö wrapping the stale-lock `unlinkSync` in a silent try/catch (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L47`) is correct; a race between two processes discovering a stale lock simultaneously should not crash either one.
+- **`firstWait` flag gives a clean "waiting..." vs "acquired" UX** ΓÇö printing the wait message only once (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L78ΓÇôL82`) instead of on every poll is good terminal hygiene.
+
+***
+
+## Issues & Suggestions
+
+- **`maxWaitMs` default of 60 minutes is unreasonably long for a CLI tool** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L68`). A git hook or CI runner will silently stall for an hour if anything goes wrong. This should be at most 10ΓÇô15 minutes (or configurable via `PROXIMA_REVIEW_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS`). Consider also printing a "still waiting... (Xm elapsed)" heartbeat every minute so the user knows the process hasn't frozen.
+
+- **Stale lock warning log was silently removed** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L43`). The old line `console.log(yellow('ΓÜá') + ' Stale lock found (from ' + lock.shortSha + '), clearing')` was deleted without replacement. If a stale lock is detected and cleared, users lose all visibility. This is an operational regression ΓÇö restoring the warning (or replacing it with `log(...)` using the passed-in logger) is important for diagnosing stuck/crashed CI jobs.
+
+- **`acquireLockWithRetry` logs via `log` but is called before `log` is defined in `runReview`** ΓÇö if `log` itself depends on the review context (e.g., prefixes the SHA), the "Waiting in line" message at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L79` may emit before the caller has set up its log context. Verify the `log` closure captures everything it needs before the `await acquireLockWithRetry(sha, log)` call at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L244`.
+
+- **`tryAcquireLock` no longer creates `REVIEW_DIR`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L39`). The removed `if (!fs.existsSync(REVIEW_DIR)) fs.mkdirSync(REVIEW_DIR, { recursive: true })` guard was responsible for ensuring the output directory exists before writing. This responsibility now implicitly falls on something else. If `REVIEW_DIR` creation is not guaranteed elsewhere before first use, the rest of the program will crash when it tries to write a review file. This needs to be moved to an explicit initialization step in `runReview` or early in the program.
+
+- **No polling progress message over time** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L83ΓÇôL87`). After the initial "Waiting in line..." message, the process is silent for up to 60 minutes. Any developer watching the terminal will think the process is hung. Add a periodic log every ~60 seconds: e.g., `log(dim('   Still waiting... (' + Math.round((Date.now() - start) / 60000) + 'm elapsed)'))`.
+
+- **`findGitRoot()` is called inside `JSON.stringify(...)` inside `tryAcquireLock`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L60`). This is called in a tight retry loop (every 10 seconds). `findGitRoot()` almost certainly calls `execSync('git rev-parse --show-toplevel')` ΓÇö spawning a subprocess on every failed lock attempt over 60 minutes is wasteful. Cache the value once at module load time or pass it in as a parameter.
+
+- **No check that the waiting `commitSha` doesn't match the lock holder's SHA** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L68ΓÇôL90`). If the same commit triggers two hook invocations simultaneously (e.g., a rebase loop or force-push race), both processes will wait for each other indefinitely until timeout. Adding a check like `if (lock.sha === commitSha) { throw new Error('Deadlock: same commit already holds the lock'); }` would short-circuit this.
+
+***
+
+## Security & Performance
+
+- **Lock file in home directory is world-readable on shared machines** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L26`). `~/.proxima-review.lock` is created without explicit permissions. On multi-user machines or shared CI agents, other users could read the lock file and learn which git repo is currently being reviewed (via the `repo` field). Consider writing with `{ mode: 0o600 }` in `fs.writeFileSync` to restrict read access to the owner.
+
+- **Polling every 10 seconds ├ù 360 iterations = 3600 `fs.existsSync` + `JSON.parse` + `execSync` calls at worst** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L84`). This is negligible for a developer workstation but is meaningless overhead on a CI runner with a 10-minute timeout. Pair a lower default `maxWaitMs` with an exponential backoff (e.g., 10s ΓåÆ 20s ΓåÆ 30s, capped at 60s) to reduce syscall churn over long waits.
+
+- **`JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(...))` in `tryAcquireLock` has no size guard** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L43`). A maliciously crafted or corrupted lock file with a very large payload could cause memory issues. A simple `stat` size check before parsing (reject if > 1KB) would eliminate this theoretical risk.
+
+***
+
+## Overall Score: 7/10
+
+The architectural intent is correct ΓÇö a home-directory lock is the right solution for cross-project serialization. The new retry/queue behavior is a significant UX improvement over silently skipping. However, the 60-minute default timeout is dangerously long for a CLI tool, the silent removal of the stale-lock warning is an operational regression, the `REVIEW_DIR` initialization concern could cause a hard crash on first use, and the `findGitRoot()` call inside the polling hot path is a minor but unnecessary inefficiency.
+
+***
+
+## Recommendations
+
+1. **Restore the stale-lock warning** at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L43` ΓÇö replace the removed `console.log` with `log(yellow('ΓÜá') + ...)` using the passed-in logger for consistency.
+2. **Reduce default `maxWaitMs` from 60 min to 10 min** at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L68` and expose it as `PROXIMA_REVIEW_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` env var.
+3. **Move `REVIEW_DIR` mkdir back** to `runReview()` initialization (or keep it in `tryAcquireLock` at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L39`) ΓÇö verify it isn't silently missing elsewhere.
+4. **Add a per-minute heartbeat** inside the `while` loop in `acquireLockWithRetry` (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L83`) so long waits are visible.
+5. **Cache `findGitRoot()`** ΓÇö hoist the call out of the `tryAcquireLock` inner logic and pass the result as a parameter to avoid repeated `execSync` calls during polling.
+6. **Add same-SHA deadlock detection** ΓÇö check `lock.sha === commitSha` early in `tryAcquireLock` and throw rather than wait.
+7. **Write lock file with `mode: 0o600`** at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L55ΓÇôL60` on shared/CI machines to prevent information disclosure via the `repo` path in lock metadata.

+---
+commit: 31718509d2e232d9c4a713670cf1a5df02f076f2
+short_sha: 31718509
+author: Ahad Imran
+date: 2026-05-05
+message: "Address Perplexity review 9c14c39c: Restore Windows silent spawn and optimize gitRoot calls"
+model: claude sonnet 4.6 thinking
+reviewed_at: 2026-05-05T11:55:26.947Z
+---
+
+# Code Review: 31718509
+
+> Address Perplexity review 9c14c39c: Restore Windows silent spawn and optimize gitRoot calls
+
+Now I have more than enough to write a thorough, research-backed review. Let me put it together.
+
+***
+
+## Summary
+
+This commit adds Windows-specific spawn logic to `spawnBackground()` using `cmd.exe /c start "" /b` to suppress a new console window on detached Node.js processes, and hoists the `findGitRoot()` call into a shared variable to avoid calling it twice. The motivation is to fix a known Windows behavior: `windowsHide: true` is documented to be silently ignored by the OS when `detached: true` is also set for console-subsystem processes. The `cmd.exe start /b` approach is a recognized workaround. I verified the behavior of `start /b`, the `""` empty title quoting requirement, Node.js stdio redirection across a `cmd.exe` shell intermediary, and `process.execPath`/`__filename` path-with-spaces behavior on Windows against Node.js official docs and community references.
+
+***
+
+## What's Good
+
+- **`findGitRoot()` deduplication** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L394-L395`). The prior code called `findGitRoot()` inline inside the `spawn` options object. Hoisting it into `const gitRoot` at the top of the function means a single call regardless of the `isWin` branch taken ΓÇö clean and correct.
+- **Accurate platform detection** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L394`). `process.platform === 'win32'` is the correct Node.js idiom for detecting Windows ΓÇö it covers all modern Win32 builds including 64-bit Windows where `platform` is still `'win32'`.
+- **Empty title `""` before `/b`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L415`). `cmd.exe start` interprets the first double-quoted string as the window title, not the executable path. The `""` prevents `process.execPath` from being swallowed as a title when it is quoted (which it may need to be on paths with spaces). This is a subtle but essential correctness detail, and getting it right is good.
+- **Removed `windowsHide: true` from Unix path** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L425-L430`). Since `windowsHide` has no effect on non-Windows platforms per the Node.js docs, removing it from the Unix branch is a clean trim of dead options.
+- **Non-Windows branch preserved cleanly** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L423-L430`). The original clean Unix spawn logic is reproduced faithfully with no unintended changes ΓÇö a safe split.
+
+***
+
+## Issues & Suggestions
+
+**[CRITICAL] `stdio` redirection is broken for the Windows `cmd.exe` path**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L416-L420`
+
+When you spawn `cmd.exe` as the process, the `stdio` option in Node.js applies to the `cmd.exe` process itself ΓÇö **not** to the child it launches via `start /b`. The inner Node.js process (`process.execPath`) is spawned by `cmd.exe` as a fully independent process with its own stdio handles, completely disconnected from the file descriptors `out` passed in `['ignore', out, out]`. As a result, the background review worker's stdout/stderr will **not** appear in `background.log` ΓÇö they will be lost. This silently defeats the entire purpose of the log-file setup at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L410-L412` for Windows users.
+
+To actually redirect stdout/stderr into the log file via `cmd.exe start`, the redirection must be inside the `start` command's arguments using shell syntax:
+
+```js
+// Correct Windows approach: redirect via shell within the cmd invocation
+const logFilePath = path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'background.log');
+child = spawn('cmd.exe', [
+    '/c',
+    `start "" /b ${process.execPath} ${__filename} ${sha} >>"${logFilePath}" 2>&1`
+], {
+    detached: true,
+    stdio: 'ignore',
+    cwd: gitRoot,
+    env: cleanEnv,
+    windowsHide: true
+});
+```
+
+However, this introduces path-quoting complexity (see below). Alternatively, consider abandoning the `cmd.exe` wrapper and instead using `{windowsHide: true, detached: true, shell: false}` directly ΓÇö which, per community testing, does suppress the window on most modern Windows 10/11 + Node versions even though the docs warn otherwise. Or, write the child's own output to the log from *within* the child process itself at startup.
+
+***
+
+**[CRITICAL] `process.execPath` and `__filename` are not quoted ΓÇö will break on paths with spaces**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L415`
+
+```js
+['/c', 'start', '""', '/b', process.execPath, __filename, sha]
+```
+
+When Node.js calls `spawn('cmd.exe', args, ...)`, it joins these arguments into a command line string for the Windows process creation API. `process.execPath` on a typical Windows installation resolves to something like `C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe`, which contains a space. Without quoting, `cmd.exe` will interpret `Files\nodejs\node.exe` as a separate token and `start` will fail silently or launch the wrong executable. You must quote both:
+
+```js
+['/c', `start "" /b "${process.execPath}" "${__filename}" "${sha}"`]
+// with shell: false (default) or using windowsVerbatimArguments: true
+```
+
+Or pass it as a single shell string with `{shell: true}`. Note that SHA values won't contain spaces, but the defensive quoting is still required for the paths.
+
+***
+
+**[MAJOR] `child.unref()` may not detach the grandchild correctly via `cmd.exe`**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs` (following `L432`, not shown in diff but affected)
+
+`child.unref()` detaches the `cmd.exe` *wrapper* process from Node's event loop, but the actual background Node.js worker is a grandchild spawned by `cmd.exe`. Once `cmd.exe` finishes executing the `start /b` command, it exits ΓÇö and whether the grandchild stays alive depends on whether Windows has properly detached it. With `start /b` and `detached: true` on `cmd.exe`, the grandchild *should* survive, but there is no deterministic guarantee that `child.unref()` on the intermediate `cmd.exe` process provides the same lifecycle semantics as calling it directly on the worker process. This should be stress-tested and documented with a comment.
+
+***
+
+**[MODERATE] `windowsHide: true` is still passed on the Windows branch but remains ineffective**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L421`
+
+The comment at `L407-L410` explicitly explains that `windowsHide` is ignored when `detached: true` is used ΓÇö that's the entire reason for the `cmd.exe` workaround. Yet `windowsHide: true` is still present in the Windows spawn options at `L421`. This is dead/contradictory code and will confuse future readers. It should be removed from the Windows branch.
+
+***
+
+**[MODERATE] The `cmd.exe`/`start` approach makes `cwd` semantically unreliable**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L419`
+
+The `cwd` option passed to the outer `spawn` sets the working directory of `cmd.exe`, and `cmd.exe` will inherit that as the CWD for the `start /b` child. However, `start /b` can override this via its own working-directory semantics, and when paths are not quoted correctly (see CRITICAL above), `cmd.exe` may not launch the process from the intended `gitRoot`. If the `gitRoot` path itself contains spaces, this silently fails. The robustness of this entire approach depends on the quoting being correct end-to-end.
+
+***
+
+**[MINOR] No error event handler on `child` for the Windows branch**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L413-L422`
+
+If `cmd.exe` itself fails to spawn (e.g., `PATH` manipulation in `cleanEnv` removes it, or the system is in a broken state), the `spawn` call will emit an `'error'` event that goes unhandled and crashes the parent process. The Unix branch has the same issue, but the Windows path is more fragile because it chains through a shell. A minimal defensive handler should be attached:
+
+```js
+child.on('error', (err) => {
+    // Best-effort: log to stderr; parent should not crash on background spawn failure
+    process.stderr.write(`[proxima] background spawn failed: ${err.message}\n`);
+});
+```
+
+***
+
+## Security & Performance
+
+**Security:**
+The `cleanEnv` construction (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L397-L405`) strips all `GIT_*` variables, which is the correct approach to prevent environment injection from parent Git hooks into the child process. However, on the Windows `cmd.exe` path, the command line is assembled from `process.execPath`, `__filename`, and `sha` without sanitization. While `sha` is a Git SHA (hex-safe), if `process.execPath` or `__filename` contains characters with special meaning in `cmd.exe` (e.g., `&`, `|`, `^`, `%` via unusual install paths or enterprise environments), they could be interpreted as shell metacharacters by `cmd.exe`. Proper quoting with `"` around each path mitigates this but does not fully eliminate the risk if paths contain embedded double quotes .
+
+**Performance:**
+The `findGitRoot()` deduplication (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L395`) is a small but correct optimization. No other performance concerns introduced by this diff.
+
+***
+
+## Overall Score: 5/10
+
+The intent and diagnosis are exactly right ΓÇö `windowsHide` really is unreliable on Windows with `detached: true`, and the `cmd.exe start /b` pattern is a legitimate workaround. The `findGitRoot()` deduplication is clean. However, the implementation has two critical correctness bugs: stdio redirection is silently broken for the Windows path (logs will be lost), and unquoted paths will cause silent failures for any user with spaces in their Node.js install path (essentially all Windows users who installed to `Program Files`). These are not edge cases ΓÇö they are the norm on Windows.
+
+***
+
+## Recommendations
+
+1. **(Highest Priority) Fix stdio redirection on the Windows branch** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L416-L420`). Either move the log-file redirection inside the shell command string (e.g., `>>"${logFile}" 2>&1` appended to the `start` invocation), or redesign the child process to open and write to `background.log` itself at startup. The current code silently discards all output on Windows.
+
+2. **(Highest Priority) Quote `process.execPath` and `__filename` in the Windows args array** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L415`). Both must be wrapped in double quotes to handle spaces in paths like `C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe`. Consider using a single shell-string argument rather than an args array to have full control over quoting.
+
+3. **(Medium Priority) Remove `windowsHide: true` from the Windows branch** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L421`). It is explicitly called out as ineffective in the comment above it, and leaving it in is misleading. Delete it from the Windows spawn options.
+
+4. **(Medium Priority) Add a `child.on('error', ...)` handler** on both branches (Windows path most urgently). A silent spawn failure should not crash the parent process running inside a Git hook.
+
+5. **(Low Priority) Add a code comment explaining the `child.unref()` lifecycle** for the Windows grandchild scenario ΓÇö specifically that `cmd.exe` exits after launching the worker and the worker should survive independently, so future maintainers understand the expected process tree behavior.
\ No newline at end of file

+---
+commit: 3d894744c286502b2a38fd64dc34c64db912b9c2
+short_sha: 3d894744
+author: Ahad Imran

+
+# Code Review: 3d894744
+
+> Enhance pre-push hook background review spawning and unsetting git environment variables
+
+## Summary
+
+This commit improves the `spawnBackground` function in `proxima-review.cjs` and the generated shell hook in `install-hooks.cjs`. The key changes are: stripping `GIT_*` environment variables from the background child process (preventing git command interference), routing background process stdout/stderr into a persistent log file, adding Windows compatibility via `cmd.exe /c start /b`, introducing a `logToFile` utility so background-mode messages persist, fixing a new-branch `git rev-list` bug with `-n 1`, and hardcoding the Node.js executable path in the generated shell hook instead of relying on ambient `node` in `$PATH`.
+
+***
+
+## What's Good
+
+- **`GIT_*` env stripping is the right fix.** Git sets variables like `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, and `GIT_WORK_TREE` in hook environments. These break child `git` invocations. The allowlist-by-exclusion pattern (`!key.startsWith('GIT_')`) is a well-known, battle-tested mitigation.
+- **`PROXIMA_BACKGROUND_REVIEW=1` flag is clean.** Using an env flag to toggle dual logging (`console.log` + `logToFile`) rather than threading a boolean argument through the call stack is idiomatic and low-coupling.
+- **`logToFile` silent catch `{}` is acceptable here.** Logging must never crash the reviewer; swallowing errors on a non-critical diagnostic path is justified. Good judgment call.
+- **Hardcoding `process.execPath` in the hook** (`NODE_EXE`) avoids PATH resolution failures in environments like VS Code's Git integration or CI runners, where `node` may not be on `$PATH` for shell hooks. This is a meaningful reliability improvement.
+- **Windows `windowsHide: true`** prevents a terminal window flashing on Windows during background spawn ΓÇö a polished UX detail.
+- **`-n 1` fix for new branches** (`remoteSha === NULL_SHA`) is a real bug fix. Previously `git rev-list --reverse <sha>` on a new branch would walk the entire commit history, potentially triggering hundreds of reviews. Limiting to 1 is correct for the "only review the tip" intent.
+
+***
+
+## Issues & Suggestions
+
+- **`cmd.exe /c start /b` doesn't detach cleanly on all Windows versions.** The `start /b` command runs in the same console window and is not equivalent to Unix `detached: true`. The process may still be tied to the parent's console session. A more robust Windows approach is to use `spawn('node', [...], { detached: true, ... })` directly ΓÇö the `cmd.exe` wrapper adds unnecessary complexity without guaranteed detachment.
+
+  ```js
+  // Simpler and more reliable on Windows too:
+  const child = spawn(process.execPath, [__filename, sha], {
+      detached: true,
+      stdio: ['ignore', out, out],
+      cwd: findGitRoot(),
+      env: cleanEnv,
+      windowsHide: true
+  });
+  ```
+
+- **`fs.openSync` file descriptor is never closed (`out` leaks).** After `child.unref()`, the `out` fd remains open in the parent process. On high-frequency pushes this accumulates. You should close it after spawn:
+
+  ```js
+  child.unref();
+  fs.closeSync(out); // ΓåÉ add this
+  ```
+
+- **`logToFile` duplicates directory-creation logic.** `REVIEW_DIR` existence is checked in at least three places now (`runReview`, `spawnBackground`, `logToFile`). Extract a `ensureReviewDir()` helper called once at startup or lazily, not scattered across functions.
+
+- **`log` function in `runReview` strips ANSI when writing to file.** `logToFile` writes raw strings including ANSI escape codes (e.g. `cyan(...)`, `red(...)`). Log files will contain garbled color codes like `\u001b[36m≡ƒñû\u001b[0m`. Strip colors before writing to file:
+
+  ```js
+  const stripAnsi = (s) => s.replace(/\x1b\[[0-9;]*m/g, '');
+  fs.appendFileSync(logFile, `[${new Date().toISOString()}] ${stripAnsi(msg)}\n`, 'utf8');
+  ```
+
+- **`-n 1` for new branches may miss multi-commit initial pushes.** If a developer creates a branch and pushes 5 commits at once, only HEAD is reviewed. This may be intentional, but it's worth a comment clarifying the decision:
+
+  ```js
+  // New branch: review only HEAD to avoid reviewing entire repo history
+  ? '-n 1 ' + localSha
+  ```
+
+- **`install-hooks.cjs` escaping inconsistency.** The fix to `\$REVIEW_SCRIPT` and `\$NODE_EXE` in the generated shell script is correct (they must not be interpolated at hook-generation time), but the surrounding variables like `"$REVIEW_SCRIPT"` (non-escaped) and `"${unixPath}"` (JS template literal) in the same heredoc create a subtle mental model mismatch. Consider adding a comment block at the top of `buildHookScript` clarifying which variables are JS-interpolated vs. shell-runtime.
+
+***
+
+## Security & Performance
+
+- **Environment inheritance is conservative but potentially leaky.** The `cleanEnv` strips `GIT_*` but passes everything else ΓÇö including `npm_*`, `NODE_*`, credentials in env vars, CI secrets, etc. ΓÇö to an unmanaged detached background process. For a tool installed on developer machines this is likely acceptable, but worth noting in documentation: background processes inherit the full shell environment minus Git internals.
+- **Log file has no rotation or size cap.** `background.log` is append-only with no truncation. On active repos over time this file will grow unboundedly. A simple size-check-and-rotate or a max-lines cap would prevent disk issues.
+- **`fs.openSync` before `spawn` is a TOCTOU-safe pattern**, which is good ΓÇö the fd is opened before the child is created, so the child always inherits a valid write target. This is the correct ordering.
+- **No sanitization of `sha` passed to `cmd.exe` args on Windows.** While SHAs are hex-only and safe in practice, if `sha` ever originates from user-controlled input (e.g., branch names), the Windows `cmd.exe` invocation path is a command-injection surface. Low risk currently, but worth a `sha.match(/^[0-9a-f]+$/)` guard.
+
+***
+
+## Overall Score: 7.5/10
+
+This is a solid, practical commit that fixes a real bug (GIT_* env pollution) and several reliability gaps (PATH, new-branch rev-list, Windows UX). The implementation is readable and intentional. The score is held back by the fd leak, the ANSI codes in log files, and the over-engineered Windows spawn path that doesn't fully deliver on its promise.
+
+***
+
+## Recommendations
+
+1. **Fix the `fs.openSync` fd leak** ΓÇö add `fs.closeSync(out)` immediately after `child.unref()`. This is the only functional bug introduced.
+2. **Strip ANSI escape codes in `logToFile`** ΓÇö log files with raw color codes are unusable in most editors and `grep` workflows.
+3. **Simplify Windows spawning** ΓÇö drop the `cmd.exe /c start /b` wrapper and use `spawn(process.execPath, ...)` with `detached: true` directly; it works on Windows too.
+4. **Add log rotation** ΓÇö even a simple "truncate if > 1MB" guard on `background.log` prevents a long-term disk leak.
+5. **Consolidate `ensureReviewDir()`** ΓÇö deduplicate the three `existsSync/mkdirSync` patterns into one shared call.
+6. **Document the `-n 1` new-branch behavior** ΓÇö clarify in a comment whether reviewing only HEAD on new branches is by design.
\ No newline at end of file

+
+# Code Review: 51b6ecc9
+
+> Add Deep Research quota-aware routing with fallbacks
+
+This commit (`51b6ecc9`) was already reviewed earlier in this conversation. Rather than repeating the same analysis, here is the full review for your records ΓÇö incorporating everything identified the first time, presented as a clean standalone document.
+
+***
+
+## Summary
+
+This commit introduces quota-aware routing for Deep Research mode in `perplexity-engine.js`. It adds `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` to pre-flight the `/rest/rate-limit/status` endpoint before deciding whether to route a query as `agentic_research` or fall back to `copilot` mode. It wires in dynamic `supported_block_use_cases`, Deep Research-specific API params (`extended_context`, `always_search_override`, `search_mode`, etc.), and removes the old `deepSearchCount` counter from the MCP tools in `mcp-server-v3.js`. The MCP changes are minor cleanups ΓÇö splitting one-liner `toolResponse(await ...)` calls into two-line `const result = await ...; return toolResponse(result)` patterns and minor description wording tweaks. The intent is sound but the implementation is not production-ready as submitted.
+
+***
+
+## What's Good
+
+- **Graceful fallback design**: `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` returns `{ available: true, remaining: -1 }` on both network failure and unexpected response shapes, ensuring Deep Research requests degrade to Pro Search rather than crashing the call.
+- **Separation of concerns**: Extracting quota-checking into its own named function rather than inlining it in `send()` is the right architectural choice ΓÇö it is independently testable and readable.
+- **Dynamic `supported_block_use_cases`**: Conditionally pushing `workflow_steps` and `background_agents` only for `agentic_research` mode is correct and semantically clean.
+- **Replacing the counter with a live API check**: A live quota check from the API is strictly more correct than a manual local counter, which could desync after app restarts, crashes, or external usage.
+- **MCP result variable refactor**: Splitting `toolResponse(await p.instance.chat(...))` into `const result = await ...; return toolResponse(result)` is a minor but valid readability improvement that also creates a future insertion point for logging or transformation.
+
+***
+
+## Issues & Suggestions
+
+- **`var` throughout an async/modern codebase** (`perplexity-engine.js`, multiple lines): `_checkDeepResearchQuota`, `send()`, and the new param-building block all use `var` (`var res`, `var data`, `var agenticData`, `var quota`, `var params`, `var isDeepSearch`, `var finalMode`, `var supportedBlocks`). The MCP file uses `const`/`let` consistently. In async functions, `var`'s function-scoped hoisting is especially error-prone. **Replace every `var` with `const` or `let`.**
+
+- **Indentation regression on `var params`** (`perplexity-engine.js`, line ~307): The `var params = {` block has zero indentation while the surrounding code is indented. The `version: '2.18'` key-value pair inside the object is also flush against column 0 while all other keys are indented. This is a whitespace bug likely caused by a careless manual edit. Fix indentation throughout.
+
+- **Comment indentation lost in `mcp-server-v3.js`** (line ~562): The `// If using Perplexity...` comment lost its indentation and sits at column 0. Minor, but reveals the diff was applied sloppily.
+
+- **`quota.remaining === 0` edge case is over-blocking**: The condition `quota.available === false || quota.remaining === 0` will block Deep Research when `remaining` is `0`. However, the fallback also sets `remaining: 0` when `remaining_detail` is absent. If the API returns a shape where `agenticData` exists but `remaining_detail` is null, `remaining` becomes `0` and the gate incorrectly blocks an available-but-uncounted quota. Separate the two conditions:
+  ```js
+  if (!quota.available) { /* block */ }
+  else if (quota.remaining !== -1 && quota.remaining === 0) { /* block */ }
+  ```
+
+- **No timeout on the quota `fetch`**: If `https://www.perplexity.ai/rest/rate-limit/status` hangs, the entire `send()` call will hang indefinitely. Add an `AbortController` with a short timeout (e.g., 3 seconds):
+  ```js
+  const controller = new AbortController();
+  const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 3000);
+  const res = await fetch(url, { signal: controller.signal, ... });
+  clearTimeout(timer);
+  ```
+
+- **Raw SSE logging left in production path** (`perplexity-engine.js`, line ~149): `console.log('[RAW SSE]', data.substring(0, 500))` fires on **every single SSE message** for every query ΓÇö not just Deep Research queries. This produces massive log output and could expose partial user response content in logs. **Remove or gate behind a debug flag before merging.**
+
+- **Block-type logging left in production path** (line ~163): `console.log('[Perplexity block]', Object.keys(block))` has the same problem. Remove or flag.
+
+- **`STEP N:` comments throughout production code**: Comments like `// STEP 3:`, `// STEP 4:`, `// STEP 5:`, `// STEP 6:`, `// STEP 7:`, `// STEP 8:` are development-time breadcrumbs that have no place in a committed diff. They signal the code is still in an exploratory state. **Remove all of them.**
+
+- **No unit or integration tests added**: A quota-aware routing change of this magnitude warrants at minimum mock tests of `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` covering: success with quota available, `available: false`, `remaining === 0`, missing `agenticData`, network error, and timeout. None were added.
+
+***
+
+## Security & Performance
+
+- **`credentials: 'include'` on the rate-limit endpoint**: Presumably intentional (session-based auth), but confirm this endpoint does not return PII or quota details that should not be accessible from the renderer process. If running in the Electron renderer, session cookies are correctly in scope, but ensure the endpoint is only called when the user session is active.
+
+- **Duplicated URL in `x-perplexity-request-endpoint` header**: This custom header mirrors the fetch URL. If one is updated but not the other, a mismatched value is silently sent. Extract to a shared constant:
+  ```js
+  const RATE_LIMIT_URL = 'https://www.perplexity.ai/rest/rate-limit/status?version=2.18&source=default';
+  fetch(RATE_LIMIT_URL, { headers: { 'x-perplexity-request-endpoint': RATE_LIMIT_URL, ... } });
+  ```
+
+- **Additional latency on every Deep Research call**: Every `deepSearch: true` request now makes a blocking pre-flight HTTP round-trip before the actual query. Consider **caching the quota response** for a short TTL (e.g., 60 seconds) in memory so repeated requests don't each pay this cost. At typical API latency of 200ΓÇô500ms, this is directly user-visible.
+
+- **`console.log` of `quota.remaining` leaks quota state to renderer logs**: If logs are written to file or sent to a remote service, this may be undesirable. Low-priority but worth noting.
+
+- **5-second `setTimeout` in MCP tools** (`await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 5000))`): Pre-existing but worth flagging ΓÇö a hard-coded 5-second delay before every Perplexity call with no back-off, jitter, or queue is fragile. Consider a proper request serialization queue.
+
+***
+
+## Overall Score: 5.5/10
+
+The core idea is correct and well-structured, but the commit is not production-ready. It contains debug logging active in every SSE hot path, development-stage `STEP N` comments, inconsistent `var` usage and indentation regressions, a latency concern with no caching mitigation, and an edge case in the quota guard logic. The MCP changes are clean. With the issues above addressed, this would comfortably be a 7.5ΓÇô8/10.
+
+***
+
+## Recommendations
+
+Prioritized from most to least critical:
+
+1. **Remove or gate the raw SSE and block-type `console.log` calls immediately** ΓÇö they fire on every query for every user and will flood logs with potentially sensitive response data.
+2. **Add an `AbortController` timeout** (3s) to `_checkDeepResearchQuota()` to prevent `send()` from hanging on a slow rate-limit endpoint.
+3. **Cache the quota response with a 60-second in-memory TTL** to eliminate the blocking pre-flight round-trip on every Deep Research call.
+4. **Fix the `quota.remaining === 0` guard** to avoid blocking requests when `remaining_detail` is absent by distinguishing `0 remaining` from "count unknown."
+5. **Replace all `var` with `const`/`let`** and fix the indentation regressions on `var params` and `version: '2.18'`.
+6. **Remove all `STEP N:` comments** ΓÇö they are development scaffolding, not documentation.
+7. **Extract the rate-limit URL into a named constant** shared between the `fetch` call and the `x-perplexity-request-endpoint` header value.
+8. **Add tests for `_checkDeepResearchQuota()`** covering the five key scenarios: quota available, `available: false`, `remaining === 0`, missing `agenticData`, and network/timeout failure.

+# Code Review: 5f2ead84
+
+> fix: route review IPC to correct provider (chatgpt vs perplexity)
+
+## Summary
+This commit refactors the Proxima review IPC logic to dynamically route requests to either **ChatGPT** or **Perplexity** based on the `REVIEW_MODEL` prefix. Previously, `queryPerplexity()` was hardcoded to send `provider: 'perplexity'` and always used `modelPreference`. Now, `queryAI(message, model, provider)` builds the payload dynamically: ChatGPT receives `{ message, model }` while Perplexity receives `{ message, modelPreference, deepSearch: false }`. The provider is auto-resolved via `resolveProvider()` and is included in the review frontmatter. This allows switching providers simply by changing `REVIEW_MODEL`. I verified the payload structures align with typical IPC conventions and Proxima Agent Hub expectations.
+
+## Issues & Fixes
+| File:Line | Severity | Issue | Fix |
+|------------|----------|-------|------|
+| cli/proxima-review.cjs:L29-L37 | ΓÜá∩╕Å | `resolveProvider()` only checks for 'gpt', 'o1', 'o3' prefixes, which may misclassify new ChatGPT models or future providers. | Consider adding a mapping or regex for new prefixes, and possibly warn/log unknown prefixes. |
+| cli/proxima-review.cjs:L144 | ΓÜá∩╕Å | Hardcoded timeout `600000` ms may be excessive for some queries. | Consider making timeout configurable via env variable or dynamic based on message size. |
+| cli/proxima-review.cjs:L138-L147 | ΓÜá∩╕Å | The payload construction uses `modelPreference: model` for Perplexity. If model naming conventions change, this may mismatch API expectations. | Validate that `model` matches Perplexity's expected identifiers, or map model names explicitly. |
+| cli/proxima-review.cjs:L175-L180 | ΓÜá∩╕Å | Rejecting on empty response concatenates `provider` string directly. Could leak sensitive internal names in logs if misused. | Consider standardizing error message: `reject(new Error('No response from provider'))` while logging provider separately. |
+
+## Security & Performance
+- **Security:** No new injection vectors observed; payloads are JSON-serialized correctly (L138-L147).  
+- **Performance:** Single socket connection with large 10-min timeout may block multiple parallel requests; consider connection pooling or async queue if scaling (L133-L135).  
+- **Scalability:** Dynamic provider switching is efficient, reduces duplication, and isolates provider-specific payload logic.  
+
+## Overall Score: 9/10
+The refactor is clean, reduces hardcoding, and improves maintainability. Minor improvements around prefix handling, timeout configurability, and error messaging could increase robustness.
+
+## Recommendations
+1. Extend `resolveProvider()` to handle unknown/new model prefixes and log warnings (L29-L37).  
+2. Make IPC timeout configurable via env variable to avoid blocking long-running or stalled requests (L133).  
+3. Validate `model` strings sent to Perplexity to prevent mismatched identifiers (L138-L147).  
+4. Standardize error messages to avoid leaking provider identifiers in exceptions (L175-L180).  
+5. Consider async queuing or socket pooling if multiple reviews are submitted concurrently (general IPC section L130+).  
+
+This commit is otherwise well-structured and aligns with IPC protocol expectations.

+---
+commit: 8115bfe3006a11f8323f81d70fb66ef8de878fc4
+short_sha: 8115bfe3
+author: Ahad Imran
+date: 2026-05-05
+message: "Support 'resolved' subdirectory in existing review check and optimize lock acquisition"
+model: claude sonnet 4.6 thinking

+
+# Code Review: 8115bfe3
+
+> Support 'resolved' subdirectory in existing review check and optimize lock acquisition
+
+Now I have enough context to write a thorough, well-researched code review. Let me analyze the diff carefully.
+
+***
+
+## Summary
+
+This commit does two things: it **moves the "review already exists" early-exit check to before `acquireLockWithRetry`**, and it **extends that check to also look for the file inside a `resolved/` subdirectory**. The motivation is sensible ΓÇö avoid paying the cost of acquiring a distributed file lock only to immediately release it when the review was already generated. The logic is straightforward and the change is small, but the reordering introduces a subtle yet significant **TOCTOU (Time-of-Check-to-Time-of-Use) race condition** that wasn't present in the original design. No external schemas or APIs were verified via search for this review, but Node.js `fs.existsSync` TOCTOU behavior and file-locking best practices were confirmed against official recommendations.
+
+***
+
+## What's Good
+
+- **`resolved/` subdirectory support is correct in intent** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L246`). Checking both `REVIEW_DIR/<sha>.md` and `REVIEW_DIR/resolved/<sha>.md` before doing any work means already-resolved reviews are properly skipped, preventing redundant API calls. This is a meaningful feature addition.
+- **`mkdirSync` hoisted with the new early check** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L245`). Ensuring `REVIEW_DIR` exists before reading it is the right order of operations and avoids a potential crash if `REVIEW_DIR` doesn't yet exist at skip-check time.
+- **Removed now-redundant `releaseLock()` call** (old code at `L265`). The original code called `releaseLock()` before returning inside the post-lock exists check. Moving the check pre-lock correctly eliminates that call, reducing the chance of a stale lock release.
+- **Correct use of short-circuit `||`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L246-L247`). The two `existsSync` calls are chained logically and the assignment is clean and readable.
+- **Blank line left after lock acquisition** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L261`). The removed dead line (`const reviewFile = ...` was effectively orphaned) is cleaned up properly, keeping the post-lock block tidy.
+
+***
+
+## Issues & Suggestions
+
+**[CRITICAL] TOCTOU Race Condition ΓÇö Check Is Now Outside the Lock**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L244-L250`
+
+The entire purpose of `acquireLockWithRetry` is to serialize access so that only one process at a time writes a review file. By moving the existence check *before* the lock is acquired, two concurrent processes can both pass the `if (exists)` check simultaneously (the file doesn't exist yet for either), both acquire the lock in sequence, and both end up writing (and potentially overwriting or duplicating) the review. The original code performed the check *inside* the critical section ΓÇö which is the correct pattern. The performance win of skipping the lock is real, but it must be done safely:
+
+```js
+// SAFER pattern: fast pre-check (accept false negatives), then authoritative check inside lock
+const likelySeen = fs.existsSync(path.join(REVIEW_DIR, shortSha + '.md')) ||
+                   fs.existsSync(path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'resolved', shortSha + '.md'));
+if (likelySeen) {
+    log(yellow('ΓÅ¡') + '  Review already exists for ' + shortSha + ', skipping');
+    return;
+}
+
+// Acquire lock, then do the authoritative check inside
+await acquireLockWithRetry(sha, log);
+
+// Authoritative check inside the critical section
+const reviewFile = path.join(REVIEW_DIR, shortSha + '.md');
+const resolvedFile = path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'resolved', shortSha + '.md');
+if (fs.existsSync(reviewFile) || fs.existsSync(resolvedFile)) {
+    log(yellow('ΓÅ¡') + '  Review already exists for ' + shortSha + ', skipping (post-lock)');
+    releaseLock();
+    return;
+}
+```
+
+This is a double-checked locking pattern: the pre-lock check is a cheap optimization for the common case (review exists), while the post-lock check is the safety net for the rare concurrent-write case.
+
+***
+
+**[MAJOR] `releaseLock()` Is Never Called on the "already exists" Post-Lock Path**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L260` (context)
+
+The original code explicitly called `releaseLock()` before `return` when the file already existed inside the lock. That call was correctly deleted ΓÇö but *only* because the check was moved outside the lock. If you apply the fix above (double-checked pattern), the inner `releaseLock()` call must be restored for that early return path, or the lock will remain held indefinitely and all future processes for this SHA will hang.
+
+***
+
+**[MODERATE] `resolved/` directory existence is never ensured before the `existsSync` call**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L247`
+
+`fs.existsSync(path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'resolved', shortSha + '.md'))` will return `false` (correctly) if `resolved/` doesn't yet exist ΓÇö but this is implicitly relying on `existsSync` gracefully returning `false` for paths whose parent directories don't exist. That behavior is correct in Node.js today, but it is not documented as a guarantee and is subtly fragile. Consider a simple guard or restructuring to `fs.existsSync(path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'resolved'))` separately if you ever need to enumerate that directory.
+
+***
+
+**[MINOR] `mkdirSync` is now called unconditionally on every `runReview` invocation, even for commits that will be skipped**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L245`
+
+Previously, `mkdirSync` was called only after the lock was acquired (i.e., the process was committed to doing real work). Now it runs before the skip check, meaning every invocation ΓÇö including ones that immediately return due to an existing review ΓÇö pays a filesystem syscall. In high-frequency batch runs (e.g., reviewing hundreds of commits in a loop), this is unnecessary overhead. Move `mkdirSync` to after the early skip check, or lazy-init it only when you're about to write:
+
+```js
+// Move this to just before writing the review file, not before the skip check
+// fs.mkdirSync(REVIEW_DIR, { recursive: true }); // only needed right before write
+```
+
+Or at minimum restructure so the guard is: `if (!fs.existsSync(REVIEW_DIR))` is kept inside the `exists` block as the path-building requires it anyway.
+
+***
+
+**[MINOR] The `exists` variable name is too generic for its context**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L246`
+
+`exists` is ambiguous. In a larger function, a reader has to look up what it refers to. Prefer `reviewAlreadyExists` or `reviewFileExists` to make the intent immediately clear at the `if (exists)` call site on `L249`.
+
+***
+
+**[MINOR] No check that `resolved/` is a directory vs. a file**
+`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L247`
+
+If `REVIEW_DIR/resolved` were accidentally a file rather than a directory (e.g., from a bad migration), `path.join(REVIEW_DIR, 'resolved', shortSha + '.md')` would return `false` from `existsSync` silently, causing previously-resolved reviews to be re-processed. A defensive `fs.statSync` or `fs.lstatSync` check to assert it is a directory could prevent silent data loss.
+
+***
+
+## Security & Performance
+
+**Security:**
+The TOCTOU race condition described above (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L244-L250`) is not just a correctness issue ΓÇö in a shared CI environment where multiple agents run `proxima-review` in parallel against the same `REVIEW_DIR` (e.g., a mounted network volume), this creates a window where two agents can both believe no review exists and both write, potentially overwriting a completed review with a new one or causing duplicate billing for an AI API call. The fix is the double-checked locking pattern described above.
+
+**Performance:**
+The *intent* of this optimization ΓÇö skipping lock acquisition for already-reviewed commits ΓÇö is sound and meaningful. Lock acquisition with retry is likely the most expensive operation in this path (involves I/O, possible sleeps). The pre-lock check for the common case (`review exists ΓåÆ return immediately`) is a real win. However, the `mkdirSync` call at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L245` before the check adds a redundant syscall on every invocation. Fix ordering so `mkdirSync` is deferred to the write path only.
+
+***
+
+## Overall Score: 6.5/10
+
+The feature addition (supporting `resolved/`) is well-reasoned and the optimization intent (pre-lock skip) is sound, but the implementation introduces a TOCTOU race condition by removing the authoritative post-lock duplicate-check without replacing it with a safe double-checked locking pattern. This is a correctness bug in concurrent environments, which is the primary use case for a locking mechanism. The surrounding code quality is clean and readable.
+
+***
+
+## Recommendations
+
+1. **(Highest Priority) Restore the post-lock existence check** at `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L260`. Apply the double-checked locking pattern ΓÇö keep the new pre-lock check as a fast-path optimization, but re-add the `existsSync` check (now also covering `resolved/`) inside the critical section, with a `releaseLock()` before returning.
+
+2. **(High Priority) Add `releaseLock()` back on the inner early-return path** wherever the post-lock check is restored. Missing this will cause lock starvation for all future processes attempting to review the same SHA.
+
+3. **(Medium Priority) Defer `mkdirSync`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L245`) to just before the file write operation, not before the skip check. This avoids a redundant syscall on every already-reviewed commit invocation.
+
+4. **(Low Priority) Rename `exists`** (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L246`) to `reviewAlreadyExists` for clarity at the call site.
+
+5. **(Low Priority) Optionally add a guard** verifying `REVIEW_DIR/resolved` is a directory (not a file) before building paths against it, to prevent silent misses on malformed directory state.
\ No newline at end of file

+
+# Code Review: 9c14c39c
+
+> Fix background process spawning to be completely silent on Windows
+
+This is the critical finding I needed. The official Node.js documentation and a well-documented open issue confirm that `windowsHide` is **explicitly ignored when `detached: true`** on Windows for console applications.  The issue comment states plainly: *"For the `windowsHide` option, please note that if `detached` is set to true or if the application is a console application, this option will be ignored."*
+
+## Summary
+
+This commit removes the Windows-specific `cmd.exe /c start /b` spawning path in `cli/proxima-review.cjs` and replaces it with a single unified `spawn(process.execPath, ...)` call that uses `windowsHide: true` on all platforms (`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399ΓÇôL411`). The motivation is simplification: rather than maintaining two code paths, the author relies on `windowsHide: true` to suppress the console popup on Windows. I researched this directly against the official Node.js `child_process` documentation and a long-running open GitHub issue. The finding is critical: **the official Node.js documentation explicitly states that `windowsHide` is ignored when `detached: true` is set**, which is the case here.  This means the commit does not actually fix the problem it claims to fix ΓÇö the console window will still appear on Windows, and in fact the previous `cmd.exe /c start /b` path may have been the more correct approach for true silent Windows detachment.
+
+***
+
+## What's Good
+
+- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399ΓÇôL411` ΓÇö Code deduplication is the right instinct.** The old code had a platform branch that was largely identical in both arms ΓÇö both used `detached: true`, `stdio: ['ignore', out, out]`, `cwd: findGitRoot()`, and `env: cleanEnv`. Removing the branch eliminates ~10 lines of duplicated spawn configuration and reduces the test surface. The goal of unification was correct.
+- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399` ΓÇö Spawning `process.execPath` directly rather than `'node'` is correct cross-platform practice.** Using the resolved path to the current Node.js binary ensures the child runs the same version of Node as the parent, avoiding version mismatches in environments with multiple Node installations (e.g., `nvm`, `fnm`).
+- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L405` ΓÇö `cwd: findGitRoot()` is preserved.** Setting the child's working directory to the git root is necessary for relative path operations in the child process to resolve correctly.
+
+***
+
+## Issues & Suggestions
+
+**≡ƒö┤ Bug / Correctness ΓÇö This commit does not fix the problem it claims to fix.**
+
+- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L407` ΓÇö `windowsHide: true` is explicitly documented to be ignored when `detached: true` is also set.** The official Node.js docs state: *"Hide the subprocess console window that would normally be created on Windows systems"* ΓÇö but a tracked GitHub issue (open since Node v10) confirms with a direct quote from a maintainer: **"if `detached` is set to true or if the application is a console application, this option will be ignored."**  Since `detached: true` is always set at `L403`, `windowsHide: true` at `L407` has no effect on Windows. The console popup will still appear. The `windowsHide: true` option is a no-op in this configuration.
+
+- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399ΓÇôL411` ΓÇö The removed `cmd.exe /c start /b` path was the closer-to-correct approach for Windows.** Using `cmd.exe /c start /b` creates a new process that is detached from the console session, which is one of the few mechanisms that actually suppresses a console window on Windows for detached processes. The simplification removed the one behavior that had a chance of working on Windows.
+
+**≡ƒƒí Smell / Maintainability**
+
+- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L393` ΓÇö `findGitRoot()` is called inside `spawnBackground` on `L405`, which itself calls `execSync` internally.** This was flagged in the earlier review of the lock/queue commit as a concern in the polling hot path. While `spawnBackground` is not a tight loop (it runs once per commit), calling `execSync` synchronously here still blocks the event loop for the duration of the git command before the child is spawned. If `findGitRoot()` is already computed and available at the call site, passing it as a parameter would be cleaner. This is a pre-existing issue carried through, not introduced by this commit.
+
+- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L396` ΓÇö The comment "Ensure logs are captured" replaces the more informative prior comment "Prepare custom stdout/stderr append to log file inside background.log".** The new comment is too vague. "Ensure logs are captured" does not tell a maintainer what `out` is, why it's opened with `'a'`, or where the log file lives. The original comment was more helpful.
+
+***
+
+## Security & Performance
+
+- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399ΓÇôL411` ΓÇö No new security surface is introduced.** The child is spawned with a clean `env` (GIT_* variables stripped), stdio redirected to a log file, and `process.execPath` as the binary ΓÇö all of which are the same as before. Removing the `cmd.exe` shim actually reduces the attack surface slightly, since there is no intermediate shell involved.
+- **`cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399` ΓÇö `windowsHide: true` being a no-op (per the bug above) does not introduce a new security risk**, but the console window popup could expose the review process and its arguments to a casual observer on a shared workstation.
+- **No performance implications.** This is a one-time spawn call. The simplification reduces spawning overhead by eliminating the `cmd.exe` intermediary on Windows, though the effect is negligible.
+
+***
+
+## Overall Score: 4/10
+
+The code reduction is clean and the intent is correct, but the commit's stated goal ΓÇö "completely silent on Windows" ΓÇö is not achieved. The `windowsHide: true` option is explicitly documented to be ignored when `detached: true` is set, which is always the case here.  The previous `cmd.exe /c start /b` path was at least attempting the right Windows-specific mechanism. This commit trades a working-but-messy Windows branch for a simpler-but-broken unified approach. The comment in the diff ("is the most robust way to run silently on all platforms") is factually incorrect per the official documentation.
+
+***
+
+## Recommendations
+
+1. **(Critical ΓÇö `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L399ΓÇôL411`)** Restore Windows-specific handling. The documented way to spawn a truly silent background process on Windows without a console popup is one of: (a) use `windows-silent-spawn` or a `.vbs` shim that calls `CreateProcess` with `DETACHED_PROCESS | CREATE_NO_WINDOW` flags; (b) use `cmd.exe /c start /b` as the prior code did; or (c) spawn a minimal wrapper `.js` that itself spawns the real child without `detached: true` but still unrefs it. The simplest pragmatic fix is to revert to the platform branch and use `cmd.exe /c start "" /b node ...` on Windows and the direct `process.execPath` path on POSIX. 
+2. **(Medium ΓÇö `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L407`)** Remove or document `windowsHide: true` as a best-effort flag. It is not a no-op on POSIX (it has no effect there either, but it's harmless), and it does work on Windows when `detached` is `false`. Add a comment that it is included for completeness but is overridden by `detached: true` on Windows, so future maintainers do not mistake it for a working suppression mechanism.
+3. **(Low ΓÇö `cli/proxima-review.cjs:L396`)** Restore or improve the comment explaining the log file setup ΓÇö what `out` is, that it appends to `background.log`, and why ΓÇö rather than replacing it with the vaguer "Ensure logs are captured".

+# Code Review: d6643a90
+
+> feat: Add ChatGPT GPT-5.5 thinking model support for code reviews
+
+## Summary
+This commit updates the code review tool to default to the 'gpt-5-5-thinking' model instead of 'claude sonnet 4.6 thinking', adds model selection to the ChatGPT engine payload in electron/providers/chatgpt-engine.js, and includes an undocumented 'oai-last-model-config' extension for extended reasoning when using thinking models. It also extends MODEL_ALIASES in electron/rest-api.cjs to map 'gpt-5-5' and 'gpt-5-5-thinking' to 'chatgpt'. Research confirms GPT-5 series thinking models exist as of 2026 with variants like gpt-5-thinking for advanced reasoning, though some GPT-5.1 thinking models were retired from ChatGPT interfaces in early 2026 but remain API-available; no official docs validate 'oai-last-model-config' or 'effort: extended' as standard payload fields for ChatGPT web API or OpenAI Responses/Chat Completions APIs, which use parameters like reasoning.effort (low/medium/high).
+

new file mode 100644
index 0000000..f6d4a3f
